mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil cīṉā intiyā kaltēyā ciṉṉaāciyā amerikkā mutaliya tēcaṅkaḷ jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻintupōṉatākavum, appaṭi aḻinta kālattil nōvāviṉ kuṭumpattaip pōṉṟa oru uttamamāṉa kuṭumpam kāppāṟṟappaṭṭatākavum collikkoḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. ākavē, jalappiraḷayam oru kālattil uṇṭāyirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. oruvēḷai antantattēcaṅkaḷil jalappiraḷaya muṇṭāyirukkumā eṉṟu nām cantēkikkalām. ākilum uṟṟu nōkkuvōmāṉāl, avaikaḷ yāvum uṇmaiyē eṉṟu kāṇpōm. lemūriyā eṉṉum peruṅkaṇṭam taṇṇīrukkuḷ amiḻntu pōkaiyil, ataṉ cuṟṟukkaraiyiluḷḷōr taṅkaḷukku etirppaṭṭa maṟṟa iṭaṅkaḷukku ēṉ pōyirukkak kūṭātu? āpattāṉa kālaṅkaḷil aṉēkar aḻintu pōyirukka, cilarmāttiram tappakkūṭātā? tappiyavarkaḷ taṅkaḷukkup pakkattiluḷḷa iṭaṅkaḷil tayārittu viruttiyākak kūṭātā? viruttiyāṉa piṉṉaṭiyār eṅkaḷ muṟpitāvākiya iṉṉār kālattil nāṅkaḷ iṅku vantōmeṉṟu meṉmaiyāyc collikkoḷvatu iyalputāṉē. lemūriyākkaṇṭam aḻinta kālattil ciṉṉa āciyāvil nōvāvum intiyāvil cattiyavirataṉum cīṉāvil pōkiyum amerikkāvil oru paktaṉum āppirikkāvil oru puṇṇiyavāṉum tappippiḻaittārkaḷeṉṟu colvatu acampāvitamallavē. ittēcaṅkaḷiṉ ōraṅkaḷ lemūriyāviṟku camīpittiruntateṉṟu nām aṟiyavēṇṭum. ivarkaḷaippōla iṉṉum ettaṉaiyō pēr tappiyirukkalām.
13. aḻintupōṉa uvarippaṭṭiṉam.
Essays by S.V. Thomas, M.A. Page 85.
“About six miles from Cape Comorin stands a small fishing village called Uvari, or Ovari. This village, I am inclined to think, occupies the site of the Ophir of the Bible. In the first place, although Ovari is at present only a small fishing village, yet unlike other villages of a similar kind, it posseses a solid stone temple, which is as old as any other temple in India, and is to-day visited by pilgrims from the remotest corners of the country, thus showing that it once had a glory, and a name that have long ago departed. Again, the word Uvari or Ovari means in Tamil nothing more or less than a scaport, being connected with Uvar, salt or salt sea. We may, therefore, reasonably conclude that Ovari was a great emporium of trade and that it was by pre-eminence called the seaport, which the merchants of Tyre might have mistaken for its proper name, a thing which often happens in South India. To more than 95 percent of the Tamil people Madras is known only by the name of ‘the city.’ Further with regard to the general appearance of Ovari the sand banks that are found there have the character of earth piled by artificial means, that is, they looks as if they have been dug out of the Earth and thrown up as mounds. Add to this circumstance the fact that when heavy rains have washed down the earth from thesde hcaps of sand, people have now and then picked up pieces of gold. The inhabitants mention this fact not because they know anything about Ophir and its gold, so as to be ambitious of connecting their village with that name, but as an instance to show that once their village was inhabited by very rich people, who left their gold in their place when they themselves went the way of all mankind.”
“kaṉṉiyākumari muṉaikkuccaṟṟēṟakkuṟaiya āṟumail tūrattil uvari allatu ovari eṉṟa cempaṭavar vacikkum oru kirāmam irukkiṟaJ. antakkirāmam cattiya vētattiṟ kūṟiyuḷḷa oppīr nakarirunta iṭattilirukkiṟateṉṟu nāṉ eṇṇukiṟēṉ. ataṟkup pala mukāntaraṅkaḷ uḷa. mutalāvatu, atu mukkiyamaṟṟa cempaṭavaruṭaiya kirāmamāṉapōtilum, ataippōlotta maṟṟa kirāmaṅkaḷukku vittiyācamāka, oru stiramāṉa kallālamaintakōyil ataṟku uṇṭu. antakkōyil intiyāviluḷḷa maṟṟakkōyilkaḷaippōlavē atikappaḻamaiyāṉatāyum tēcattiṉ nāṉā ticaikaḷilumiruntu jaṉaṅkaḷ aṅkē cuvāmi taricaṉattukku varumpaṭiyāṉa avvaḷavu makimaiyuḷḷatāyum irukkiṟaJ. itaṉālē atu orukālattil atikappēr pirastāpa makimaiyuṭaiyatāyiruntateṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. iraṇṭāvatu, uvari eṉṟāl tamiḻil kaṭaṟṟuṟaimukam eṉṟu arttam. atāvatu uppu eṉṟu poruḷpaṭum uvar eṉṟamoḻiyuṭaṉ campantappaṭṭaJ. intakkuṟippukaḷiṉiṉṟu uvariyāṉatu varttakattukkup pērpōṉa iṭamā yiruntirukkavēṇṭum eṉṟum, mukkiya viyāpāra stalamāṉatiṉāl atai uvari allatu vicēṣitta tuṟaimukam eṉṟaḻaittārkaḷ eṉṟum, tīrunakariṉ varttakar uvariyē ataṟkup pēr eṉṟu tappāy niṉaittārkaḷ eṉṟum cantēkamaṟac collalām. ippērppaṭṭa tappāṉa eṇṇaṅkaḷ teṉṉintiyāvil vaḻakkamāy uṇṭākiṟatutāṉ. (utāraṇamāka, nūṟṟil 95 pēr matarācaip ‘paṭṭaṇam’ eṉṟutāṉē aḻaikkiṟārkaḷ.) mūṉṟāvatu, anta uvari eṉṉum kirāmattiṉ potuvāṉa veḷittōṟṟattaik kavaṉittāl, aṅkuḷḷa maṇal mēṭukaḷ taraiyil iruntu veṭṭappaṭṭuk kuvikkappaṭṭa mēṭukaḷpōl kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. nāṉkāvatu, perumaḻai peytu inta maṇalmēṭukaḷ karaiyumpōtu jaṉaṅkaḷ ammēṭukaḷiliruntu poṉ nāṇayaṅkaḷai eṭuttirukkiṟārkaḷ. akkirāmavācikaḷ intaccaṅkatiyaic collumpōtu taṅkaḷ kirāmam pūrvakālattil makimaipeṟṟirunta “oppīr” eṉṉum nakaramtāṉ eṉṟa perumaiyāṉa kāriyattai stāpikka varāmal, taṅkaḷ kirāmam muṟkālattil taṉavāṉkaḷ vacitta iṭameṉṟum avarkaḷ marikkuṅkāl taṅkaḷ poṉṉaiyum tiraviyattaiyum avviṭattil viṭṭu marittārkaḷ eṉṟum arttappaṭumpaṭiyākavē appaṭiccollukiṟārkaḷ.”
kaṉṉiyākumarikkuc cumār āṟumail tūrattiluḷḷa uvariyeṉṉum kaṭaṟṟuṟaimukattil pūrva kālattil kaṭṭappaṭṭa paḻamaiyāṉa kōyilkaḷiruntatākavum, avai ippōtu piratāṉamaṟṟuppōṉatākavum, aṅkuḷḷa mēṭāṉa iṭaṅkaḷil poṉnāṇayaṅkaḷ maḻaipeykiṟa kālaṅkaḷil kaṇṭeṭukkappaṭukiṟatākavum tōṉṟukiṟatu. avviṭattil vaḻaṅkivanta poṉ nāṇayaṅkaḷ mika māṟṟuyarntāyiruntatiṉ nimittam cālomōṉ eṉṉum yūtēyātēcattu rājaṉ tāṉ mikunta makimai yuṭaiyatāyk kaṭṭiya tēvālayattiṉ katavukaḷaip pacumpoṉ takaṭṭiṉāl mūṭiṉāṉ eṉpataikkoṇṭum, oppīriṉ taṅkattai īrāmiṉ kappalkaḷ varuṣantōṟum kāṇikkaiyākakkoṇṭu vantu cālomōṉukkuk koṭuttaṉaveṉṟum 1. rājākkaḷ 10, 11, 22-l mūṉṟu varuṣattukku orutaram tarṣīsiṉ kappalkaḷ poṉṉaiyum veḷḷiyaiyum yāṉait tantaṅkaḷaiyum kuraṅkukaḷaiyum mayilkaḷaiyum koṇṭuvarum eṉṟum colliyiruppataik koṇṭum, cumār 3,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē uvariyeṉṉum kirāmam kaṭaṟṟuṟaip paṭṭaṇamāyiruntateṉṟum, aṅkuḷḷa poṉ mikavum nērttiyāṉateṉṟum, atai mēṟṟicai viyāpārikaḷ vāṅkikkoṇṭu pōṉārkaḷeṉṟum teḷivākat terikiṟatu. iṟṟaikkuc cumār 3,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ pacumpoṉṉaiyum rattiṉaṅkaḷaiyum vācaṉaittiraviyaṅkaḷaiyum cantaṉakkaṭṭaikaḷaiyum yāṉait tantaṅ kaḷaiyum taṅkaḷ viyāpārap poruḷāka maṟṟattēcaṅkaḷukku ēṟṟumati ceyta avviṭam ippōtu aḻintu aṭaiyāḷam teriyāmal niṟkiṟaJ. pūrvakālattil aṅkuḷḷōr ceyta viyāpārattaik kavaṉikkum pōtu avarkaḷ mikunta nākarikamuṭaiyavarkaḷāyum celvapperukkuṭaiyavarkaḷāyu miruntārkaḷeṉṟu collāmalē viḷaṅkukiṟaJ.
itaṉmuṉ carittira ārāyccikkārar colliya cilavarikaḷaik kavaṉitta nām teṉṉintiyāviṟkut teṟkē intumakācamuttirattil vicālittiruntu jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻintu pōṉa pūpākam lemūriyā veṉṟum, intumakācamuttirattil atu irunta iṭam teriyāmal aḻintupōka, atiluḷḷōr taṅkaḷukkuc camīpattiluḷḷa teṉṉintiyā  vilum intu cīṉatīpa kaṟpattilum cantā cumāttirā jāvā mutaliya tīvukaḷilum matakāskar tīvilum āppirikka amerikkāviṉ teṉ pākaṅkaḷilum pōyk kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum terikiṟatu. ippaṭi aḻintupōṉa lemūriyāk kaṇṭattiluḷḷōr ulakattiṉ carittiram eḻuta ārampikkum muṉṉālēyē mikunta nākarīkamum kalviyumuṭaiyavarkaḷā yiruntārka ḷeṉṟu pala kalvimāṉkaḷ colliyirukkiṟārkaḷ. atōṭu aḻintupōṉa akkaṇṭattiliruntavarkaḷ tamiḻp pāṣaiyaiyē pēci vantārkaḷeṉṟum lemūriyā aḻintapiṉpē maṟṟappāṣaikaḷ tamiḻil kalakka ārampittateṉṟum nām aṟivōm. mēlum, ulaka carittirattai eḻuta ārampitta anēkar, taṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ tēcattil nākarīkam kalvi kait toḻil carittiram uṇṭāṉa kālattaiyē piratāṉamāka vaittukkoṇṭu, ataṟkuc caṟṟu muṉpiṉṉākavē maṟṟat tēcattuc carittiramirukkumeṉṟu uttēcaṅ kaṭṭiṉārkaḷ. itaṉāl oru tēcattiṉuṭaiya pūrvīkam māṟi, uṇmai kaṇṭu aṟivataṟku ētuvillāmaṟ pōkiṟaJ. maṟṟuñ cilar tamakku muṉ eḻutiyavarkaḷuṭaiya apippirāyattaiyē cātittuc collukiṟārkaḷ. ulakattiṉ iyaṟkai amaippaik kavaṉittuc cariyāṉa kālam collukiṟavarkaḷ paittiyakkārarāka niṉaikkappaṭu kiṟārkaḷ. vēṟu cilar, tāṅkaḷ collum kāriyaṅkaḷ ellārālum nampappaṭavēṇṭumeṉpatai uttēcittut tāṅkaḷ eḻutivaittavaikaḷai anātiyāyuḷḷateṉṟum, vekupūr vantoṭṭu irukkiṟateṉṟum, itu riṣikaḷāl eḻutappaṭṭa teṉṟum, itai nampātavarkaḷ iṉṉiṉṉa cāpattukkuḷḷāvārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟārkaḷ. vēṟu cilar, pūrvamāyuḷḷa kirantaṅkaḷil nūtaṉamāyt taṅkaḷ cuyapirayōjaṉattai virumpicceyta cila cūttiraṅkaḷai aṅkaṅkē nuḻaittuviṭukiṟārkaḷ. itaṉāl intiyāviṉ pūrva carittiraṅkaḷ kāla varaiyaṟai colla ētuvillāmal cantēkikkum nilaiyil vantuviṭṭatu. nūṟṟaik keṭuttatu kuṟuṇi eṉpatupōla taṟkālattavar ceyyum coṟpap puraṭṭukaḷ pūrvamāyuḷḷa nallataiyum keṭuttuviṭukiṟaJ. appaṭi yiruntālum teṉṉintiyāvil pūrvamāyp pēcappaṭṭuvanta tamiḻp pāṣaiyiṉ ilakkaṇamākiya tolkāppiyam nāḷatu varaiyum evvitak kalappumaṟṟut taṉ pūrvattai viḷakkik kāṭṭikkoṇṭu niṟpatai nām kāṇkiṟōm. annūl mutal ūḻiyiṉ iṟutiyilirunta atāvatu iṟṟaikkuc cumār 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta mutaṟcaṅkattiṉ kaṭaiciyil kaṭalāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭa teṉmaturaiyil āṇṭukoṇṭirunta nilantarutiruviṟ pāṇṭiyaṉ kālattil araṅkēṟṟappaṭṭateṉṟum annūlē iṭaiccaṅkattārukku ātāra nūlāyiruntateṉṟum itaṉ piṉ kāṇpōm. tolkāppiyar tamatu kuruvākiya akattiyarāl iyal icai nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻaippaṟṟiyum eḻutiya 80,000 cūttiraṅkaḷaikkuṟukki 8,000 cūttiramākac ceytār eṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. III, Page 907.
“Tol (bjhš-tol. Tam.) old - Tolgauppyam (bjhšfh¥ãa« - tolgappiyam). From (above and kavya, san, Poem). Tamil grammar (lacshanam) by Tolgauppyan of Madura, pupil of Agastyan whose grammar, consisting of 80,000 rules, he abridged, reducing the number to 8,000. The Tolgauppyam complete should consist of three parts; on letters, words and prosody, or rather versification as an art. Of these the last part cannot be found complete. Tolgauppyam has had three commentaries written upon it by Natchinarkiny, Yilampooran, and Shenauvareiyan (Nunnool).”
“tolkāppiyam eṉpatu akastiyaruṭaiya māṇākkaṉāṉa maturait tolkāppiyarāl eḻutappaṭṭa tamiḻ ilakkaṇam. 80,000 cūttiraṅkaḷaṭaṅkiya pērakattiyam eṉṉum akastiyaruṭaiya ilakkaṇattaic curukki ivar 8,000 cūttiraṅkaḷāl tamatu ilakkaṇa nūlai eḻutiṉār. tolkāppiyam muḻu nūlum eḻuttu col yāppu eṉṉum mūṉṟu pirivukaḷaiyuṭaiyatā yirukkavēṇṭum. ivaikaḷil kaṭaicippirivu pūraṇamāyk kiṭaikkavillai. nacciṉārkkiṉiyar iḷampūraṇar cēṉāvaraiyar eṉṟa mūvar tolkāppiyattukku urai yeḻutiyirukkiṟārkaḷ.”
ivar eḻutiya cūttiraṅkaḷ tamiḻ makkaḷāl pacumpoṉṉilum ciṟantavaikaḷāka nāḷatuvaraiyum pōṟṟappaṭṭuvarukiṉṟaṉa. iruntālum, avar karuttukkaḷ yāvum terintukoṇṭu aṉupōkattukkuk koṇṭuvantavarkaḷ mikac cilar eṉṟē collavēṇṭum. mēlum, tolkāppiyam 8,000 cūttiraṅkaḷuṭaiyateṉṟu maklīṉ paṇṭitar collum vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, taṟkālattil vaḻaṅkum 1612 cūttiraṅkaḷ mikac coṟpameṉṟē collavēṇṭum; mītiyāṉa 6,388 cūttiraṅkaḷ antiṉ vāyppaṭṭu aḻintupōyiṉaveṉṟu niṉaikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. uviṉslō paṇṭitar collukiṟa 12,000 cūttiraṅkaḷ ciṟṟakattiyamāyirukkalāmeṉṟu eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟatu. ippaṭi aḻintupōṉa tolkāppiyam, pūrva tamiḻariṉ vaḻakka oḻukkaṅkaḷ rājamuṟaimaikaḷ nilattiṉ pākupāṭukaḷ vīṇaiyiṉ vakaikaḷ mutaliyavaikaḷaippaṟṟi mikat teḷivākac collukiṟaJ. taṟkālattil kāṇappaṭum pāṣaiyiṉ tērcci aṉaittiṟkummēlāṉa ilakkaṇamuṭaiyatāy viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. tamiḻiṉ perumai iṉṉateṉṟu aṟintu koḷvataṟku atu pōtumāṉateṉṟē niṉaikkavēṇṭum.
14. mikappūrva kālattaikkāṭṭum cila mukkiya kuṟippukaḷ.
tolkāppiyam uṇṭāvataṟku muṉ 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷākavirunta mutal caṅkattaiyum atil talaimai vakitta pulavarkaḷaiyumpaṟṟi nām kēṭkaiyil, itu uḷḷatāyirukkumō, ulakamuṇṭāki iṉṉum 6,000 varuṣamākavillaiyē 9,000, 10,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkuk kaṇakkuc collukiṟārēyeṉṟu nām niṉaippōm. 3,400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ mōcē muṉivarāl eḻutappaṭṭa ātiyākamattil ulakaciruṣṭi kirama varalāṟṟil collappaṭum ovvoru tiṉamum ovvoru ūḻikālamā yirukkavēṇṭumeṉpatu carittirakkārarkaḷuṭaiya koḷkai. ēṉeṉṟāl, mōcēyiṉ pēḻaikkuḷ vantaṭainta mirukaṅkaḷaip pārkkilum ettaṉaiyō maṭaṅku peritāṉa mirukaṅkaḷiṉ elumpukaḷ maṇṇil putaintiruppatākak kaṇṭupiṭittirukkiṟārkaḷ. mikap peritāṉa maṉita elumpukaḷum varavarak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. kaṭalāl aḻiyāta cila mēṭṭup pāṅkāṉa iṭaṅkaḷil 20,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēṟpaṭṭavaikaḷāṉa maraṅkaḷ irukkiṟatākak kaṇṭupiṭittirukkiṟārkaḷ. marattiṉ vaḷarcciyaikkāṭṭum rēkaikaḷaik koṇṭu kaṇikkum varuṣaṅkaḷ tavaṟutaluṭaiyavaiyākā veṉṟu tuṇintu collalāmeṉpataip piṉ varum vākkiyaṅkaḷāl kaṇṭukoḷvōm.
Doubts of Infidels,
“The bones of man, of the type of the North American Indian, have been exhumed from the delta of the Mississippi at New Orleans, which were found lying below the fourth forest level, and making large allowance, must have lain here for more than fifty-thousand years. The exhumed relics of ancient civilization in the valley of the Nile antedate the History of the Jewish theocracy and the foot prints of the Creator are found in the granite pages of the primary and fossiliferous rocks, long anterior to the fabulous era of this Genesaical history of creation. Humboldt describes a tree now growing in the famous gardens of Montezuma, as more than six-thousand years old, and another in Central America as but little less than twenty-thousand years old.”
“vaṭaamerikka intiyarkaḷiṉ tēka amaippaiyotta maṉita elumpukaḷ, micicippinatiyiṉ caṅkama pūmiyil niyu ārliyaṉs eṉṉum iṭattil veṭṭiyeṭukkappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. vaṉamaṭṭattiṉ nālāvatu paṭaittaraikkukkīḻē avaikaḷ putaintu kiṭantaṉa. ākaiyāl eppaṭippārttālum, kuṟainta paṭcam 50,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēlākavē avaikaḷ aṅkē iruntirukkavēṇṭum. nīlanatiyiṉ paḷḷattākkil veṭṭiyeṭukkappaṭṭavaikaḷāṉa paṇṭaikkālattu nākarīkac ciṉṉaṅkaḷ yūtaruṭaiya vētacarittirattukku muṟpaṭṭavaikaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. aṅkuḷḷa pāṟaikaḷiṉ mutal aṭukkilum stāvarajaṅkama cilārūpaṅkaḷaṭaṅkiya aṭukkukaḷilum ciruṣṭikarttāviṉuṭaiya aticayacceyalkaḷiṉ aṟikuṟikaḷ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. ivaikaḷellām ātiyākamattil kūṟiyuḷḷa ciruṣṭippuc carittirattukku nīṇṭakālattiṟku muṟpaṭṭavaikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉa. moṉṟijūmāviṉ pērpeṟṟa tōṭṭattil 6,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēṟpaṭṭa oru marattaippaṟṟiyum mattiya amerikkāvil koñcaṅkuṟaiya 20,000 varuṣaṅkaḷāyirukkiṟa iṉṉoru marattaip paṟṟiyum hampōlṭs eṉpavar viparamāyk kūṟukiṟār.”
vaṭaamerikka intiyariṉ tēka amaippaiyotta maṉita elumpukaḷ niyūārliyaṉs eṉṉum iṭattil veṭṭiyeṭukkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum avaikaḷ kuṟaintatu 50,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēluḷḷatāka irukkavēṇṭu meṉṟum collukiṟār. piraḷayattiṉāl kāṭukaḷum cōlaikaḷumāyirunta iṭaṅkaḷ maṇṇāl mūṭappaṭṭu maṟupaṭiyum taraiyāka ataṉmēl anēkaāyiram varuṣaṅkaḷāṉatiṉ piṉ vēṟoru piraḷayamuṇṭāki maraṅkaḷ cōlaikaḷai aḻittu maṇṇāl nirappiyatākavum ippaṭiyē nālutaram piraḷayamuṇṭāki vaṉaṅkaḷai aḻittu maṇpaṭaiyuṇṭāyirukkiṟatākavum terikiṟatu. ovvoru maṇpaṭaiyiṉ kuṟippukaḷiṉālum appaṭaikaḷi lirukkum maraṅkaḷiṉ kuṟippukaḷiṉālum ovvorupaṭaiyum uttēcam anēka āyiramvaruṣaṅkaḷāki yirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu cāstirikaḷ kaṇakkiṭukiṟārkaḷ. itil kālāvatiyāyirukkum aṭippaṭaiyil vaṭaamerikka intiyariṉ aṅkaṅkaḷ putaintiruppataik kaṇṭupiṭittavarkaḷ avaikaḷ maṇṇil 50,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē putaintirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. itupōlavē nīlanatiyiṉ karai yōraṅkaḷilum pūrva nākarīkattaik kuṟikkum ciṉṉaṅkaḷiruntatākac collukiṟārkaḷ. mēlum jīvaṉuḷḷavaikaḷil vekukālam nīṭit tirukkakkūṭiya papparappuḷi yeṉṟamaram 20,000 varuṣaṅkaḷ āyuḷuṭaiya tāyirukkiṟateṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. immaram teṉṉintiyāvil aṅkaṅkē nāḷatu varaiyum kāṇkiṟōm. teṉṉintiyāviṟku itu iyaṟkaiyāyuḷḷa maramēyoḻiya nūtaṉamāṉa maramalla. mēlum immarattiṉ taṉmaiyai aṟinta cittarkaḷ atil oru ciṟu tuvārañceytu turucu cuṇṇattai amukkivaippārkaḷ; ciṟitu nērattukkuḷ atiliruntu taṇṇīr ūṟum; antat taṇṇīraic ciṟu pāttiraṅkaḷil piṭittut taṅkaḷukku vēṇṭiya aḷavu cāppiṭuvārkaḷ. cāppiṭṭavuṭaṉ taṅkaḷaiyaṟiyāta mayakkam avarkaḷukku uṇṭāka nālunāḷ pariyantam pirakkiṉaiyaṟṟup paṭuttiruntu piṉ eḻuntiruppārkaḷ. ippaṭi ataṉ racattaic cāppiṭuvatu avarkaḷ ceyyum yōkacātaṉaikkum kaṟpacātaṉaikkum aṉukūlamāṉateṉṟu eṇṇappaṭukiṟaJ. oru marattiṉ upayōkattai naṉṟāy oru tēcattār aṟintiruppārkaḷāṉāl anta maram attēcattiṟkēyuriya teṉṟu collāmalē viḷaṅkum. teṉṉintiyāviṉ cila iṭaṅkaḷil itaippūta virukṣameṉṟu colluvārkaḷ. itu paricuttamāṉa virukṣameṉṟu potuvāka eṇṇappaṭukiṟaJ. ākaiyiṉāl ataṉ kiḷaikaḷaiyāvatu ilaikaḷaiyāvatu oruvarum cētappaṭuttukiṟatillai. ataṉ aṭippākam peruttu nuṉippākam ciṟuttuk kōpurattiṉ cāyvāka vaḷarntirukkum. immarattait tañcāvūr jillāvilum ērāḷamāyk kāṇalām. tirunelveli maturai mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷilum aṅkaṅkēyirukkiṟatu. tañcāvūril, civakaṅkait tōṭṭattil oru maramum veṇṇāṟṟaṅ karaiyil oru maramumirukkiṟaJ. ippaṭiyē kāvērippāyccalilum aṉēka maraṅkaḷirukkalām. immaraṅkaḷaik kuṟukkāka aṟuttu avaikaḷil cuṟṟic cuṟṟiyirukkum rēkaikaḷaikkoṇṭu ittaṉai varuṣamāka immaraṅkaḷ niṟkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu cāstirikaḷ kaṇṭupiṭikkiṟārkaḷ. ivaikaḷaikkoṇṭu nām collukiṟa mutal ūḻiyiṉ kālam atikamākāteṉṟē tōṉṟukiṟatu. immaraṅkaḷ vēṟu cila iṭaṅkaḷiliruppatāka aṭiyilvarum vacaṉattil kāṇalām.
Dravidian comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell,
“Huge old speciments of the Baobab, or Adansonia Digitata, an African tree, of which the Hindus do not know even the name, may still be seen in or near - various sites of foreign commerce in the extreme south of the Indian peninsula: e.g. in Kottar, near Cape Comorin, and near Tuticorin in Tinnevelly - possibly  on the site of the ancient Kolkhi.”
“intiyāviṉ teṉkōṭiyil, anniyatēcaṅkaḷōṭu varttakam naṭattivanta anēka tuṟaimukappaṭṭaṇaṅkaḷilum avaikaḷukkuccamīpattilum, pēyōpāp allatu aṭaṉsōṉiyā ṭijiṟṟāṟṟā eṉṟa āppirikkāk kaṇṭattu maram, nīṇṭu pārittup piramāṇṭamāy vaḷarntu niṟpataik kāṇalām. avaikaḷ vekukālattu maraṅkaḷ. utāraṇamāka, kaṉṉiyākumari muṉaikkuc camīpamāṉa kōṭṭāṟṟilēyum tirunelvēliyaiccārnta tūttukkuṭikkuc camīpamākak koṟkai yeṉṟa paḻaiyanakarirunta iṭattilēyum immaraṅkaḷ niṟkiṉṟaṉa. ivaikaḷiṉ pērmutalāy intukkaḷukkut teriyavillai.”
mēlēkūṟiya pēyōpāp eṉṟamaram āppirikkākkaṇṭattu marameṉṟum aṉṉiyatēca viyāpāracampantamāṉa tuṟaimukappaṭṭaṇaṅkaḷil atu kāṇappaṭukiṟateṉṟum ataṉ peyar intukkaḷukkut teriyavillai yeṉṟum collukiṟār. immarattiṉ upayōkattai aṟintirunta teṉṉintiya jaṉaṅkaḷukku atu nūtaṉamāṉa maramalla. āṉāl āppirikkāvukkum teṉṉintiyāvukkum naṭuviluḷḷa pūpākattil immaram ērāḷamā yiruntirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum, appūpākam aḻintapiṉ ataṟkuc campantappaṭṭatāṉa pūmikaḷil aṅkaṅkē kāṇappaṭukiṟateṉṟum nām niṉaikka ētuvirukkiṟatu. ammarattaippaṟṟi,

uviṉslō akarāti
“papparappuḷi - (also perukkamaram) A species of large tree, Adonsonia digitata, L” eṉṟum, vācutēvanāyuṭu eḻutiya
āyurvēta pārāvāram
“yāṉaippuḷiyamaram, papparappuḷi, pūrimam, aṭaṉsōṉiyā ṭijiṭāṭā haṉaṭiḷēṭinyai ṉupaivaiyavaya pēyōpāp kṣayaṭiyeb maṅki preṭ ṭrī  āṭimēnala-cenayaṉa vacanana. āpirikkā tēcattilum ippōtu namatu tēcattilum inta maram vistāramāyviṭṭatu. paḻattiṉ cataikku saṅkōcani (narampukaḷaiyum carīratātukkaḷaiyum curukki utiram cīḻ mutaliyavaṟṟai niṟuttum) antaras niktakāri (uṭkoṇṭāl tātukkaḷiṉ ericcalaic cānti ceytu avaṟṟaittuvaḷvikkum) ceykaikaḷuṇṭu. itaic cītapētikkum cītacuraṅkaḷukkum koṭukkalām.”
eṉṟum colliyiruppatiṉāl mēlēkūṟiya maram papparappuḷi eṉṟu aṟiyalām. mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil narampukaḷaiyum carīratātukkaḷaiyum curukki utiram cīḻ mutaliyavaṟṟai niṟuttakkūṭiyatāṉa cakti papparappuḷi (pūta virukṣam) eṉṟa marattukku iruntatiṉāl cittarkaḷ atai upayōkappaṭuttukiṟatākac collappaṭukiṟatu mikapporuttamākat terikiṟatu. nīṇṭa āyuḷuḷḷa immaram nīṇṭa āyuḷai virumpit tavañceyta periyōrkaḷuḷḷa lemūriyāvil atu pūrvamāyiruntirukka vēṇṭum. lemūriyā aḻintupōṉa piṉ atōṭu nilac campantam peṟṟa āppirikkā teṉṉintiyā mutaliya pūpākaṅkaḷil kāṇappaṭuvatu iyaṟkaiyē. potikaimalaiyiṉ pakkattilum kāvēriyāṟṟiṉ pakkaṅkaḷilum iruppatē avaikaḷ maṟṟat tēcaṅkaḷiliruntu varavillai yeṉpataik kāṭṭukiṟatu.
Castes and Tribes of Southern India by E. Thurston Vol. I, Intro. P. XXIV.
“On the evidence of the very close affinities between the plants and animals in Africa and India at a very remote period, Mr. R.D. Oldham concludes that there was once a continuous stretch of dry land connecting South Africa and India.”
“mikappaḻamaiyāṉakālaṅkaḷil āppirikkāvilirunta stāvarajaṅkamaṅ kaḷukkum intiyā vilirunta stāvarajaṅkamaṅkaḷukkum irukkum neruṅkiṉa oṟṟumaiyaikkoṇṭu, ār.ṭi. ōlṭahām eṉpavar, orukālattil teṉ āppirikkāvaiyum intiyāvaiyum orētoṭarcci yāṉa peruntarai iṇaittuk koṇṭiruntatu eṉṟu tīrmāṉikkiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭavarikaḷai nām kavaṉikkaiyil lemūriyākkaṇṭamum teṉ āppirikkāvum vekukālattukkumuṉ toṭarcciyāṉa pūcampanta muṭaiyatāyiruntateṉṟu terikiṟatu. lemūriyā aḻintapiṉ āppirikkāviṉ teṉpākattilum teṉṉintiyāvilum kāṇappaṭum tāvara varkkaṅkaḷiṉ oṟṟumaiyē itaṟkuk kāraṇameṉṟuñ collukiṟār. itaikkoṇṭu papparappuḷi eṉṟa maram āppirikkāviliruntu, koṇṭuvarap paṭavillai, teṉṉintiyāviṟkēyuriya marameṉṟu teḷivākatterikiṟatu.
mēlum patiṉāyira varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē ekiptiyar kaḷiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, avarkaḷ taṇṭacakkaram mutaliya yantiraṅkaḷiṉ utaviyiṉṟik kaiyiṉālēyē maṇpāṇṭaṅkaḷ ceytārkaḷeṉṟum kāṇkiṟōm.
Hutchinson’s History of the Nations,
Early Egyptians making pottery 10,000 years ago.
“The most abundant handwork of the early Egyptians was the finely made pottery entirely formed by hand. It was built up from the base and in form so true that no error is pereeptible. The facing was finished with a coat of red hacmatite, which turned to a brilliant black in the furnace. It is interesting to note that the same materials are used in the same kindof patterns by the hill tribes at the back of Algeria at the present time.”
āti ekiptiyar 10,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ maṇpāṇṭaṅkaḷ ceytal.
“āti ekiptiyar aparimitamāyc ceytuvanta kaittoḻil eṉṉaveṉṟāl, veku nērttiyāy yantira utaviyillāmal kaiyiṉāl ceyta maṇpāttiravēlaiyē, aṭimutal uruvattil yātoru picakum illāmal kaiyālēyē atu ceyyappaṭṭaJ. civappuc ceṅkal poṭiyiṉāl ataṟku mēl pūccuppūcappaṭṭaJ. itai neruppil cuṭṭavuṭaṉ antaccivappu kaṟuppu niṟamāy māṟiṉaJ. itil vicēṣamāyk kavaṉikka vēṇṭiyatu, atē taḷavāṭaṅkaḷiṉāl atēvita maṭpāttiraṅkaḷ ippōtu aljīriyāvukkuppiṉ pakkattiluḷḷa malaivācikaḷāl ceyyappaṭukiṟatu eṉpatē.”
itaṉāl, iṟṟaikkup pattāyira varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē maṉitarkaḷiruntirukkiṟār kaḷeṉṟu terikiṟatu. mēlum ataṟku nīṇṭakālam muṉṉākavē pūrvamāka maṉitarkaḷiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum itaik koṇṭu collalām. ēṉeṉṟāl, maṉuṣar uṇṭāṉapiṉ avarkaḷai aṭaintirukakum tērcciyaikkoṇṭu nāṭkaḷaik kavaṉippōmāṉāl, mutalmutal kāykaḷaiyum paḻaṅkaḷaiyum vittukkaḷaiyum tēṉaiyum cāppiṭṭukkoṇṭirunta kālameṉṟum, iraṇṭāvatu karakarappuḷḷa kiḻaṅkukaḷaiyum kāykaḷaiyum koṭṭaikaḷaiyum cuṭṭuttiṉṉa neruppiṉ upayōkattai aṟintakālameṉṟum, mūṉṟāvatu camaippataṟku aṉukūlamāka maṭpāttiraṅkaḷai yuṇṭākki atil pākappaṭuttiya tāṉiyavakaikaḷaiyum kāy kaṟi kīraikaḷaiyum cāppiṭṭu vantakālameṉṟum nām niṉaikka vēṇṭum. iṉṉum karuvikaḷ campantamākavum vastira campantamākavum vācastala campantamākavum carittira ārāyccikkārar veku pūrvīkattaic collukiṟārkaḷ. avvitamāy nām kavaṉikkaiyil oru ekiptiyaṉ maṇpāttiram ceyta kālam maṉuṣaṉ uṟpattiyāṉa kālattiṟku veku kālattiṟkup piṉṉuḷḷatāyirukkavēṇṭum. aimpatiṉāyiram varuṣaṅ kaḷukkumuṉ putaintirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu coṉṉa maṉita elumpaik koṇṭu maṉitarkaḷ vekukālattiṟku muṉṉālēyuṇṭāyirukka vēṇṭu meṉṟu terikiṟatu. amerikkāvilum ekiptilum kāṇappaṭum poruḷkaḷ teṉṉintiya kaṇṭattilum atāvatu lemūriyāvilum maṉitarkaḷirun tirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu rucuppaṭuttukiṉṟaṉa. ākaiyiṉāl cumār 8,000 varuṅkaḷukku muṉ tolkāppiyariruntāreṉṟum 12,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉpē mutal caṅkam ārampittateṉṟum nām colvatu atikamākāJ. 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉum 12,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkup piṉṉumāṉa mutaṟcaṅkattil akastiyariruntāreṉṟum atil iyal, icai, nāṭaka meṉṉum muttamiḻaippaṟṟiyum ilakkaṇañ coṉṉār eṉṟum colvatu aṟpa āyuḷuḷḷa namakku āccariyamākattōṉṟum. iṟṟaikku 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉiruntu iṟaiyaṉārakapporuḷukku uraiyeḻutiya nakkīraṉār collukiṟatai nām nampāmal irukkalāmō? avar colliya vacaṉaṅkaḷ poyyāyirukkumō? ‘neṟṟikkaṇkāṭṭiṉālum kuṟṟam kuṟṟamē’yeṉṟu paramacivaṉōṭu vātāṭiya oru vitvacirōmaṇi poy colluvārā? avar appaṭic coṉṉālum avar kālattilirunta tamiḻc caṅkappulavarkaḷ maṟukkāmal viṭṭu viṭuvārkaḷā? oruvar etaiyuñ collukiṟatu, tāṭcaṇiyattiṟkāka maṟṟavar kēṭṭukkoṇṭirukkiṟatu eṉṟa ikkālamallavē akkālam. ikkālattai aṭiyārkku nallārum collukiṟār; ākaiyiṉāl ikkāriyam pūrvantoṭṭu uṇmaiyāṉa tākavē tōṉṟukiṟatu. tolkāppiyattil kāṇappaṭum mutal ūḻiyi lirunta tēcattiṉ ellaikaḷum tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉ ciṟanta ilakkaṇamum iṟṟaikku 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē collappaṭṭirukkiṟatē pōtumāṉatākat tōṉṟukiṟatu.
15. teṉṉintiyāviṉ mārkkam
pūrvattiluḷḷōraippaṟṟi nām vicārikka ārampippōmāṉāl, nītitavaṟāmal aracāṭciceyta rājākkaḷaiyum iṭaiviṭāmal teyvattai vaṇaṅkivanta paktarkaḷaiyum paktiyilum kalaikaḷilum ciṟanta cittarkaḷaiyum (riṣikaḷ) paṟṟi vistāramākak kēḷvippaṭuvōm. teṉṉintiyāviṟkut teṉpakkattiluḷḷa lemūriyāvilirunta jaṉaṅkaḷākiya avarkaḷ intu matattiṟkum teyva vaṇakkattiṟkum nākarīkattiṟkum kāraṇamāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟum intu mārkkam avarkaḷālēyē vaṭanāṭṭiṟkuk koṇṭupōkappaṭṭateṉṟum piṉ varum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
District Manual of Madras, Pt. III,
“Mr. Pope in his edition of the Abbe Dubois’ work says that in South India numberless legends relating to devout worship pers of the Linga are current: that some of them are curious, and they are exclusively of southern origin. And Wilson states in his introduction to the catalogue that tradition uniformly Points to an extension of Hinduism and civilization from the extreme south of the Peninsula.”
“teṉṉintiyāvil liṅkattaip pūcittavarkaḷaippaṟṟiya kaṭṭuk kataikaḷ anēkamuṇṭu. avaikaḷil anēkam vinōtamāyum, teṟkē yiruntu uṟpattiyāṉatāyum tōṟṟukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟu pōp turai Abbe Dubois nūliṉ patippuraiyil kūṟukiṟār. intumatamum nākarīkamum intiyāviṉ teṉkōṭiyil uṟpattiyāki vaṭakkē koṇṭupōkappaṭṭatu eṉṟu pārampariyaṅkaḷ oṉṟu pōlak kūṟukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟu ṭākṭar uvilcaṉ (Dr. Wilson) tamatu nūliṉ mukavuraiyil collukiṟār.”
mēlēkaṇṭa vākkiyaṅkaḷaiyum, kiruṣṇapakavāṉ tam jaṉaṅkaḷait tuvārakaiyiliruntu vaṭakiḻakkiluḷḷa intirapirastattil jalappiraḷayat tiṟkut tappippōyk kuṭiyēṟumpaṭi arccuṉaṉukkuc coṉṉataiyum, jalappiraḷayattiṉ aḻiviṉiṉṟu tappiya 300 cōḻiya pirāmaṇarkaḷaip pirāmaṇamatam viruttiyākap paramacivaṉ āviṭaiyārkōyilil vaittuk kāppāṟṟiyataiyum kavaṉikkaiyil, teṟkiliruntu jaṉaṅkaḷ vaṭakkē pōṉārkaḷeṉṟu colluvatu oppukkoḷḷak kūṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. atōṭukūṭa, teṉṉintiyāviṉ mukkiyamāṉa iṭaṅkaḷ ovvoṉṟilum piramāṇṭamāṉa kōyilkaḷ aṟputamāṉa kalvēlaippāṭu kaḷuḷḷavaikaḷāyum peṉṉamperiya tērkaḷuḷḷavaikaḷāyum irukkavum civaṣēttiraṅkaḷum liṅka stāpaṉaṅkaḷum ērāḷamāy irukkavum kāṇkiṟōm. vaṭaintiyāvilō, appērppaṭṭa kōyilkaḷaik kāṇpatu mika ariJ. mēlum teṉmaturaiyiluḷḷōr vicēṣamāka civapaktiyiṟ ciṟantavarkaḷāyiruntārka ḷeṉpatu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. civaperumāṉum avar kumāraṉākiya murukakkaṭavuḷum vikṉēsvararum teṉmaturai yiluḷḷōr koṇṭāṭum tēvarkaḷāyiruntārkaḷ. aḻintupōṉa lemūriyā eṉṉum perum kaṇṭattiluḷḷa tēcaṅkaḷ yāvaṟṟilum civapaktiyē vicēṣittiruntatāka nām kāṇalām. civaperumāṉum murukak kaṭavuḷum teṉmaturai rājyattiṟku mūlapuruṣarkaḷāyiruntu anēka cīrtiruttaṅkaḷ ceytu muṉpiṉ 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷākavirunta mutaṟ caṅkattil talaimai vakittut tamiḻai ātarittuvantavarkaḷāyu miruntārkaḷ. ataṉālum avarkaḷiṭattil viḷaṅkiya teyvīkattiṉālum avarkaḷiṭattil pakti koṇṭu peṉṉam periyakōyil kaṭṭi mūla stāṉattil liṅkastāpaṉam ceytu tērkaḷ amaittu uṟcavaṅkaḷ koṇṭāṭiṉārkaḷ. kōyilkaḷil uṟcavaṅkaḷum tiṉapūjaiyum kiramamāy naṭantu varuvataṟkeṉṟu māṉiyaṅkaḷum kāṇikkaikaḷum koṭuttu vantirukkiṟārkḷa. ulakam muṭintālum oḻintupōkātirukkumpaṭi umpaḷam campaḷaṅkaḷiṉāl ūḻiyam ēṟpaṭutti oḻuṅkupaṭuttiyirukkiṟārkḷa. pakal iravāka 60 nāḻikaiyilum iṉṉiṉṉa pūjai iṉṉiṉaṉa aṉṉapāṉāti kaḷuṭaṉ iṉṉiṉṉarāl naṭattappaṭa vēṇṭumeṉṟu niyamam ceytirukkiṟārkaḷ. kōyilkaḷil teyva cannitiyil āṭukiṟavarkaḷum pāṭukiṟavarkaḷum taṅkaḷukkuk kuṟikkappaṭṭa kālaṅkaḷil taṅkaḷ paṇiviṭaikaḷaic ceyvārkaḷ. teyva paktiyuḷḷa ovvoruvarum taṅkaḷ kaikaḷil kāṇikkaikaḷai eṭuttukkoṇṭu taṅkaḷukku avakācamāṉa enta nērattilum pōy cuvāmi taricaṉañ ceyvārkaḷ. kōyilukku vēṇṭiya puṣpaṅkaḷ tarum nantavaṉaṅkaḷai vaittu vaḷarppatu, atiliruntu atikālaiyil puṣpaṅkaḷ eṭuttukkoṭuppatu, kōyil pirākāraṅkaḷaic cuttañ ceyvatu, viḷakkup pōṭuvatu mutaliya kaiṅkariyaṅkaḷai mika uṟcākattōṭu ceyvārkaḷ. teyva cannitiyil tēvāram tiruvācakam pōṉṟa paṇkaḷiṉāl maṉamurukappāṭi teyvattai ārātippārkaḷ. aimpulaṉkaḷaiyumaṭakki, pakavāṉuṭaiya cannitiyil niṣṭaiyiruntu tiyāṉippārkḷa. irākāti tīkkuṇaṅkaḷukku iruppiṭamākiya uṭalai varuttum virataṅkaḷ aṉuṣṭippārkaḷ. iṉṉum niṉaittaṟkum collutaṟku muṭiyāta anēkavitamāṉa ārātaṉaikaḷālum kāṇikkai kaḷālum āṭal pāṭalkaḷālum ālayam eppoḻutum niṟaintirukkum. itai ālavāy eṉṉum uttaramaturaiyiṉ ālayattilē ammaṉ canniti yilē arai nāḻikai kāttiruntu pārttavarkaḷ naṉṟāy aṟivārkaḷ. cariyai kiriyai yōkam ñāṉam eṉṉum nālu paṭikaḷaiyumuṭaiyavarkaḷ taṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ aṟivukkēṟṟa vitamāy ārātittu varakkūṭiyatāka ālayaṅkaḷ amaikkappaṭṭiruntaṉa.
jalappiraḷayattiṟkut tappiṉa cilar, teṉṉintiyāviṟku vantu kuṭiyēṟit taṅkaḷ kālattiluḷḷa pūrvamāṉa ālayaṅkaḷaippōla ālayaṅkaḷ kaṭṭi, avviṭattil tāṅkaḷ vaṇaṅkiya teyvattaiyēiṅkē vaṇaṅkiṉārkaḷeṉṟum teriyavarukiṟatu.
eppaṭi intumatam teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṟkiluḷḷa vistāramāṉa nāṭukaḷil mikap pūrvīkamāyiruntatō, appaṭiyē apperunāṭṭiṉ pāṣai yākiya tamiḻum evvitamāṉa kalappumaṟṟut taṉippāṣaiyāy jalap piraḷayattukku muṉṉālēyē yiruntateṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil pārppōm.
IV. tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉatu toṉmai.
1. teṉṉāṭṭiluḷḷōr vaḻaṅkivanta tamiḻp pāṣaiyiṉ pūrvīkamum ciṟappum.
itai vācikkum kaṉavāṉkaḷē, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ nuṭpattaiyum ataṉ pūrvīkattaiyum nām aṟiyavēṇṭumāṉāl caṅkītat tamiḻaiyum nāṭakat tamiḻaiyum taṉ aṅkamākakkoṇṭu muttamiḻ eṉṟu peyarvaḻaṅkum tamiḻppāṣaiyaippaṟṟi nām caṟṟuvicārikka vēṇṭiyatu avaciyam. tamiḻppāṣaiyuṇṭāṉa kālamē karnāṭaka caṅkītamum uṇṭāṉa kālamām; tamiḻppāṣaikkuriya iṉimaiyē teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ iṉimaiyām. tamiḻppāṣai eppaṭi aṉṉiya pāṣaikaḷōṭu kalavāta taṉitta pāṣaiyō appaṭiyē teṉṉintiya caṅkītamum maṟṟac caṅkītaṅkaḷōṭu kalavāmal taṉitta vitikaḷuṭaiyatām. aṟiñarkaḷāl uyarntateṉṟu eṇṇappaṭum teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaiyum ataṉ maṟṟum varalāṟukaḷaiyum nām vicārikku muṉ tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉ toṉmai yaiyum ataṉ ciṟappaiyum paṟṟippārkka vēṇṭum. iṅkē maṟṟap pāṣaikaḷōṭu oppiṭṭum maṟṟattēcaṅkaḷōṭu oppiṭṭum maṟṟavarāl tamiḻppāṣaikku uṇṭāṉa naṉmai tīmaikaḷaik kāṭṭiyum aṟiñarkaḷ collum cila vacaṉaṅkaḷai eḻutiṉēṉē yoḻiya maṟṟa enta vitattilum oru pāṣaiyaiyāvatu oru jātiyāraiyāvatu kuṟaicolla vantatāka niṉaiyātirukkumpaṭi mikavum kēṭṭuk koḷḷukiṟēṉ.
ulakac carittiraṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭuvataṟku muṉṉum maṟṟat tēcattār nākarīkamuṭaiyavarkaḷākuvataṟku muṉṉum, aḻintupōṉa lemūriyāk kaṇṭattiluḷḷōr nākarīkamuṭaiyavar kaḷāyiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu carittirakkārar colvataṟkiṇaṅka, akkaṇṭattiluḷḷa teṉmaturaiyum, atil aracāṇṭu vanta pāṇṭiya rājarkaḷum caṅkappulavarkaḷum pēci vanta pāṣaiyākiya tamiḻum, mikuntatoṉmaiyum taṉicciṟappum vāyntavaikaḷā yiruntaṉa. tamiḻ eṉṉum patattiṉ mutal iraṇṭu eḻuttukkaḷ cērnta tami eṉṉum mutaṉilaiyāṉatu oppiṉmai, taṉimai eṉṟum, tamiḻ eṉṉum patamāṉatu iṉimai, maturam eṉṟum arttappaṭumeṉṟu nām aṟivōm. avvarttattiṟkiṇaṅkat tamiḻppāṣaiyāṉatu mēṉmai poruntiyatāy maṟṟap pāṣaikaḷ kalavātāyiruntateṉṟu cila aṟiñar collum apippirāyaṅkaḷai iṅkē pārppōm.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. 42.
“There is little doubt that the Dravidian languages are incomparably older in point of time than the Sanskrit. It is not an unreasonable supposition that they once occupied the whole of Hindustan and have been driven to their present position to the south and along the coast by the encroachment of other languages coming from the North-west.”
“tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷ camaskiruta pāṣaikku veku kālattukku muṉṉuḷḷavai yeṉpataṟkuc cantēkamēyillai. ātiyil avaikaḷ intustāṉam muḻuvatum vaḻaṅkivantateṉṟum, piṟaku vaṭamēṟkiliruntu uṟpattiyāṉa maṟṟap pāṣaikaḷ varavara, avai taṟkālam irukkiṟa kīḻkarai, teṉkōṭi mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷukkut turattappaṭṭaṉa eṉṟum niṉaikka iṭamuṇṭu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, āriyar intiyāviṟku varuvataṟkumuṉ intutēcam muḻuvatilum pēcappaṭṭa pāṣai tamiḻ eṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. mēlum tamiḻāṉatu kālacampavaṅkaḷiṉāl māṟutalai yaṭaintālum ataṉ ilakkaṇam māṟāmal orēmātiriyāyirukkakkūṭiya avvaḷavu tērcciyai anēkamāyiram varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē aṭaintiruntateṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. 112.
“As far as present evidence goes, however, they are indigenous to India, and perhaps specially indigenous to Southern India........ As to their language, no other is known to which it can be affiliated. It stands alone, without any immediate predecessor. In origin, it must be long anterior to the Sanskrit, which has subsequently played so important a political part with regard to it. Its original strength is shown by the great persistence of its grammatical formations through all the vicissitudes of history.”
“taṟkālattil ēṟpaṭum kuṟippukaḷaik kavaṉittāl, avarkaḷ intiyāvilē, mukkiyamāyt teṉṉintiyāvilē uṟpattiyāṉavarkaḷeṉṟu terikiṟatu. avarkaḷ pāṣaiyōveṉṟāl, maṟṟa entap pāṣaikkum contamāṉatākac collakkūṭiyatāka illai. ataṟkumuṉṉuḷḷa maṟṟentap pāṣaiyōṭum atu cērāmal taṉiyē niṟkiṟaJ. uṟpattiyaip pārttāl, atu camaskirutattukku veku kālattukku muṉṉuḷḷaJ. rājāṅka viṣayamāy camaskirutam piṟkālattil veku mukkiyattukku vantapōtilum uṟpattiyil atu pintiṉatu tāṉ, tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉuṭaiya ātipalattai yaṟiyavēṇṭumāṉāl, carittira campantamāṉa māṟutalkaḷ ettaṉaiyō uṇṭākiyum ataṉ ilakkaṇa uṟuppukaḷ māṟāmal ekkālum atē vitamāy iruppataik kavaṉittāl viḷaṅkum.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷai nām kavaṉikkaiyil, epirēya pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu cila kirēkka eḻuttukkaḷum kirēkka pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu pāli pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷum, pāli pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷum avaikaḷi liruntu anēka pirākirata pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷum vantateṉṟum, appāṣaiyiṉ vārttaikaḷ oṉṟukkoṉṟu campantamuṭaiyavarkaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum colluvatu pōla, tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉ iṉṉa pāṣaiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉateṉṟu colla ētuvillāmal taṉitta pāṣaiyāyirukkiṟateṉṟu collukiṟār. mēlum ettaṉaiyō carittira campantamāṉa māṟutalkaḷ uṇṭāṉapōtilum avaikaḷiṉāl māṟṟappaṭāta ilakkaṇa vitikaḷaip pūrvamāy aṭaintirukkiṟateṉṟum collukiṟār. mikappūrvamāyuḷḷa ittamiḻmoḻi ataṉ piṉvanta vēṟu entap pāṣai yālum yātoru māṟutalaiyumuṇṭākka muṭiyāmal taṉippāṣaiyā yiruntu varukiṟateṉpataip piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. 49.
“North Indian Civilization, when it came as far south as the Tamul country, found the people already in possession of the art of writing and of cultivated language. in consequence of this, Sanskrit did not regulate the Tamil phonetic system, and merely held the place of a foreign learned language.”
“vaṭaintiyāviṉ nākarīkamāṉatu, teṟkē tamiḻ nāṭumaṭṭum vantapōtu, eḻuttu, pāṣai mutaliya nākarīkattukkuriya ciṉṉaṅkaḷ teṉṉāṭṭu jaṉaṅkaḷukkuḷ muṉṉamēyirukkak kaṇṭaJ. itaṉāl tāṉ camaskiruta pāṣaiyāṉatu tamiḻp pāṣaiyil evvita māṟutalaiyum uṇṭākkamuṭiyāmal, oru aṉṉiya pāṣaiyākavē iruntuvarukiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil vaṭatēcattiliruntu vanta āriyaraippōlavē appōtirunta tamiḻmakkaḷum nākarīkattilum kalvi yilum ciṟantavarkaḷāyiruntārkaḷ; tāṅkaḷ pōkumiṭaṅkaḷil taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyiṉ nalaṅkāṭṭi aṅkuḷḷa pāṣaiyil pala māṟutalkaḷai yuṇṭākkiṉatu pōla tamiḻmoḻiyai māṟṟa avarkaḷāl iyalāmal pōyiṟṟeṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. mikat toṉmaiyāṉa tamiḻ moḻikkut tirāviṭameṉṟum ataiccērnta pāṣaikaḷ pēcum tēcattukkut tirāviṭatēcameṉṟum jaṉaṅkaḷukkut tirāviṭareṉṟum āriyarkaḷ peyar vaittārkaḷ. tamiḻeṉṟa patattaic ciṟantatāka eṇṇum taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyil eḻutikkoḷḷat tiṟamaiyillāmal ataṟku muṟṟilum campanta millāta vēṟoru peyaraikkoṇṭu aḻaikkap pukuntārkaḷ eṉpataip piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇka.
ciṅkāravēlu mutaliyār eḻutiya apitāṉa cintāmaṇi. pakkam. 489.
“tamiḻ nāṭṭaracar mūvarum cūryacantira vamcattiliruntu pirintavarkaḷ; ivarkaḷ tuvāparayukattiṟku muṉpum aracāṇṭatākat terikiṟatu. oru pāṇṭiyaṉ pārata yuttattil pāṇṭavarkaḷiṉ cēṉaikku aṉṉamiṭṭatākat terikiṟatu. ivaṉ tamiḻp pulavarai ātarittavaṉ. *   *   *   *   *   *   * piṉṉumivvariya pāṣai maṟṟaip pāṣaikaḷpōl vēṟu pāṣaikaḷiṉ tuṇaivali peṟātu tāṉāy viḷaṅkum ēṟṟamuṟṟatu; itaṉai aṟivuṟḷōr palar paḻaiya nūlkaḷiṟ kaṇṭaṟiyalām. āyiṉ tamiḻ eṉpatu tirāviṭam eṉpatiṉṟiripaṉṟōveṉiṉ tiripākāJ. tamiḻ, teluṅku, kaṉṉaṭam, makārāṣṭaram, kūrccaram ivaikaḷaiyun tirāviṭameṉpavākaliṉ itaṟkē yuriyatākāJ. āyiṉum appeyar kūṟavanta vaṭanūlār akkālattiṟku muṉṟāṉē tōṉṟiya pāṣaikkut tāṅkaḷ peyaraṟiyātu annāṭṭil tāṅkaḷ vanta kālattu iṭṭa peyarākavumiruttaliṉ itu vaṭamoḻikkup pintiyatākāJ. vaṭamoḻi vaṭanāṭṭiliruntatupōl teṉmoḻi teṉṉāṭṭi liruntaJ. itaṉāl tamiḻp pāṣai taṉitta pūrva pāṣaiyeṉpatu koḷḷakkiṭantaJ.”
tamiḻmoḻiyeṉṟu collavuṅkūṭa veṟutta āriyar, tirāviṭa pāṣaiyeṉṟu peyar vaittār. tamiḻ eṉṟa patam camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉiṉṟē yuṇṭāṉateṉṟu maṟṟavar eṇṇumpaṭi tirāviṭam eṉṟa vārttai, ṭa, vukku ḷa, varalām eṉṟa vitippaṭi tirāmiḷam eṉṟu vantatākavum tirāmiḷam eṉṟa vārttai iraṇṭām eḻuttuk kuṟukit tiramiḷam eṉṟu vantatākavum, ataṉpiṉ iraṇṭām eḻuttuk keṭṭut tamiḷam eṉṟu āṉatākavum, tamiḷamtāṉē amkeṭṭu tamiḻ eṉṟu vanta tākavum collukiṟārkaḷ. eppaṭiyāṉālum ākaṭṭum vi atāvatu vakara ikarattiṉ keti eṉṉavāyiṟṟu? ataṟkum oru niyāyam coṉṉāl naṉṟāyirukkum. ataṟkum makara irakattiṟkum eṉṉa campantam? ippaṭiyē talaiyaic cuṟṟi mūkkait toṭukiṟavaṉ kataipōla collakkūṭiyatā yiruppatiṉāl allavō coṟpa varikaḷukkum arttanteriyāmal eḷiyanaṭaiyiluḷḷa cāraṅkatēvar eḻutiya caṅkītaratṉākarattiṉ oru culōkattiṟku 20 vitamāy arttañceytum, uṇmaiyaṟiyāmal pōṉārkaḷ. eḻuttu eḻuttāy arttañceyvatai nām kavaṉittāl ellām piramam eṉṟu terintu koṇṭavaṉ mauṉam cātittatu pōlākumē yoḻiya vāytiṟakkamuṭiyātu eṉpatu teḷivāyt terikiṟatu. camaskirutam uyarnta pāṣaiyeṉṟum ariya viṣayaṅkaḷ atil eḻutappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum nām oppuk koṇṭālum pēccuppaḻakkat tiṟku varāta pāṣaiyeṉṟē collavēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu.
tamiḻil vaḻaṅkum aṉēka vārttaikaḷait taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyil mutal mutal iruntatāka maṟṟavar niṉaikkumpaṭi kālākālaṅkaḷil māṟṟi, māṟṟiyataṟ kiṇaṅka nūlkaḷum purāṇaṅkaḷum kaṭṭukkataikaḷuñ ceytu parappivantārkaḷ. nīr eṉṟa patam nīram eṉṟa camaskiruta patattiliruntu vantateṉṟum tamiḻar pēcum vārttaikaḷil nūṟṟukkut toṇṇūṟu camaskirutameṉṟum vāy kūcāmal colluvārkaḷāṉāl vēṟu eṉṉa collamāṭṭārkaḷ! mutal ūḻikku muṉ 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷākac caṅkam vaittu naṭattiya vitvacirōmaṇikaḷum akkālattum ataṟku muṉṉumirunta ērāḷamāṉa tamiḻ makkaḷum taṇṇīr kuṭikkavillaiyō? allatu ataṟku ēṟṟa peyar vaikkat teriyātiruntārkaḷō? āriyar vantapiṉtāṉō avarkaḷ kuḷirnta nīrukku taṇṇīraṉṟum cūṭuḷḷa nīrukku vennīreṉṟum ippaṭiyē āṟṟunīr ūṟṟunīr cēṟṟunīr cennīr ceyanīr kaṭalnīr kuṭinīr iḷanīr kaṇṇīr umiḻnīr eṉṟum colla ārampittārkaḷ? ippaṭiyē ciṟanta ērāḷamāṉa tamiḻ moḻikaḷait taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyiliruntē vantateṉṟu colvataṟku ēṟṟatākac cila eḻuttukkaḷaik kūṭṭiyum cila eḻuttukkaḷaik kuṟaittum neṭil kuṟilākavum kuṟil neṭilākavum māṟṟiyum campantamillāta eḻuttukkaḷākattirittum vaḻaṅkiṉārkaḷ. tamiḻ nāṭṭiluḷḷa cila mukkiyamāṉa malaikaḷukkum āṟukaḷukkum paṭṭaṇaṅ kaḷukkum maṉuṣarkaḷukkum camaskirutap peyarkaḷ vaittup purāṇaṅkaḷum kataikaḷum kaṭṭiṉārkaḷ. tāṅkaḷ ceyta purāṇaṅkaḷaiyum kavikaḷaiyum piṟar aṟiyap piracaṅkittu rājākkaḷaiyum jaṉaṅkaḷaiyum uṇmaiyeṉṟu nampumpaṭi nīr eṉṟa vārttaiyai, nīra nīram nāram eṉṟu māṟṟi avaikaḷiliruntu nāratam nārāyaṇaṉ eṉṟu toṭuttukkoṇṭu piṉ nāratattilum, nīrattiliruntum nīr vantateṉṟu coṉṉatai nampukiṟavarkaḷ, avarkaḷ kaṭṭiya kataikaḷaiyum nampiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷ coṉṉatai yellām nampukiṟavarkaḷ ēṟpaṭṭapiṉ camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉ aṉēka vārttaikaḷ tamiḻil vantu vaḻaṅkattalaippaṭṭaṉa. itai vivēkikaḷ aṟivārkaḷ. atikam colla iṅku avaciyamillai. iyal icai nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻiluḷḷa icait tamiḻākiya caṅkītattait teṉṉāṭṭiliruntē maṟṟavarkaḷ koṇṭupōṉārkaḷeṉpatu paṟṟiyum vaṭapāṣaikkārarum tamiḻ nāṭṭiṉiṉṟē icait tamiḻaik koṇṭu pōyirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu paṟṟiyum iṅku eḻuta nēriṭṭatu. ataṉ viparam itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. tamiḻmoḻiyōṭu vaṭamoḻi kalantavitam piṉ varum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
2. tamiḻil camaskirutam kalanta varalāṟu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. (4.)
“It is to be observed that though the long list of names mentioned in the Pooranas are all Sanskrit, these are only book names. The names of the country reported or ascertained by Aryan travellers and settlers were invariably translated into Sanskrit by the literary caste of the Aryans. It is a very common error to suppose that because none but Sanskrit names are found in the ancient literature of the country, it was therfore a country occupied by an Aryan people, and that all the places mentioned were founded by the Aryans. But in fact as the Aryan visitors to India had the monopoly of literature, the indigenous names could only appear in a Sanskrit form; and no argument is to be thence deduced in one direction or another as to the extent of Aryan coloizations. In later times Aryan influence has undoubtedly given current names to geographical places even in Southern India. *    *    * It will be seen from the next note that Greek literature is analogous to Sanskrit in presenting indigenous Indian names in such a Greek dress that they are not easily recognisable; but the Greeks did not at all to the same extent actually translate Indian names.”
“purāṇaṅkaḷil collappaṭṭirukkiṟa nīṇṭa aṭṭavaṇaip peyarka ḷellām camaskirutap peyarkaḷāyiruntapōtilum avaikaḷellām pustakap peyarkaḷēyoḻiya uṇmaiyāṉa peyarkaḷalla. āriyarāṉa pirayāṇikaḷum putukkuṭiyēṟuvōrkaḷum tāṅkaḷ kaṇṭatāka allatu kēḷvippaṭṭatākac collum tēcaṅkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷellām āriya vitvāṉkaḷāl tappāmal camaskirutattil moḻi peyarkkappaṭṭaṉa. intu tēcattiṉ paḻamaiyāṉa nūlkaḷilellām camaskirutappeyarkaḷē kāṇappaṭuvatāl antat tēcam āriyarāl kuṭiyēṟappeṟṟa tēcameṉṟum atil colliyirukkum iṭaṅkaḷellām āriyarāl stāpikkappaṭ ṭavaiyeṉṟu niṉaippatu mikavum cātāraṇamāṉa tappāyirukkiṟatu. uṇmai eṉṉaveṉṟāl, intiyāvukku vanta āriyartāṉ intiya nūlkaḷ camastattaiyum eḻutiṉavarkaḷāṉatāl aṅkaṅkuḷḷa iṭappeyarkaḷ camaskirutattil māttiram irukkumpaṭiyāyiṟṟu. intap peyarkaḷai vaittukkoṇṭu āriyar kuṭiyēṟiṉa nāṭukaḷaippaṟṟi ippaṭiyāvatu appaṭiyāvatu yātoṉṟai stāpikka muyaluvatu tappu. āṉāl pintiya kālaṅkaḷil āriyarukkuṇṭāṉa celvākkiṉāltāṉ teṉṉintiyāvil kūṭat taṟkālam vaḻaṅkivarum iṭappeyarkaḷ ellām camaskirutattil irukkiṉṟaṉa. piṉ collappōkum viṣayattiṉāl, camaskirutam pōlavē kirēkka nūlkaḷum intiyappērkaḷai kirēkkap pērkaḷpōla māṟṟic colvatiṉāl avaikaḷ intiyap pērkaḷ eṉṟa uruvē teriyāmal pōkiṉṟaṉa eṉṟa caṅkati veḷiyākum. āṉālum āriyar ceytatu pōla kirēkkar avvaḷavu tūram intiyap pērkaḷaik taṅkaḷuṭaiyavaipōl māṟṟikkoḷḷavillai.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil tamiḻ moḻikaḷ āriyarāl camaskirutattil māṟṟappaṭṭup purāṇaṅkaḷil eḻutappaṭṭatākavum tāṅkaḷ kaṭṭāta paṭṭaṇattiṟkuṅ kuṭiyirāta vīṭṭukkum camaskirutap peyar vaittuk kēḷvippaṭṭatai māttiraṅkoṇṭu purāṇakkataikaḷ kaṭṭuvatil cilar tērntavarkaḷ eṉpatākavum terikiṟatu. tāṅkaḷ kēḷvippaṭṭatai māttiramalla, uttēcamāy niṉaippataiyum uḷḷatupōl eḻutivaikkum cāmarttiyam āriyarukkuc cupāvamākavē amaintirukkiṟaJ. avarkaḷiṟ cilar nūtaṉamāy ulakattil oṉṟu uṇṭāṉāl, atuvum eṅkaḷ paḻaiya purāṇaṅkaḷi lirukkiṟateṉṟu collakkūṭiya vitamāyk kaṟpaṉai ceytuvaippārkaḷ. intak kaṟpaṉaikaḷukkuḷ intiyarkaḷ. kaṭṭuppaṭa vaḻakkappaṭṭuviṭṭārkaḷ. nūtaṉamāy intiyāvil piravēcitta āriyar, itaṉmuṉ intiyāvil vacitta jaṉaṅkaḷaiyum avarkaḷ pāṣaiyaiyum appāṣaiyiluḷḷa nūlkaḷaiyum aṅkuḷḷa rājākkaḷ periyōrkaḷiṉ carittiraṅkaḷaiyum muṟṟilum maṟaittu, taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyaiyum taṅkaḷ nūlaiyum taṅkaḷ periyōraiyumē colla ārampittārkaḷ. intiyāviṟku vanta kirēkkarkaḷ intiyāviluḷḷa cilapeyarkaḷait taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyil māṟṟiyapōtu avaikaḷ intiyappeyarkaḷeṉṟu uruvē teriyāmal pōyiṟṟeṉṟum ataippārkkilum atikamāy āriyarkaḷ intiyapeyarkaḷaic camaskirutattil māṟṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟār. intiyā āriyarālēyē kuṭiyēṟṟappaṭṭa tēcameṉṟum atiluḷḷa iṭaṅkaḷ ellām āriyarāl stāpikkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum niṉaippatu mikavum cātāraṇamāṉa tappāyirukkiṟateṉṟu maklīṉ paṇṭitar collukiṟatu pōlavē, camaskirutattiliruntu tamiḻ uṇṭāṉateṉṟum, camaskiruta vārttaikaḷ kalavāmal tamiḻp pāṣai vaḻaṅkamuṭiyāteṉṟum colvatum tappeṉṟu collavēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. mutal ūḻiyil mutal tamiḻccaṅkamum accaṅkattiṟkuriya nūlkaḷum attēcamum aḻintu pōṉapiṉ atil tolkāppiyam oṉṟē miñciṉatākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. ataṉpiṉ iṭaiccaṅkattār kālattu uṇṭākiya pala nūlkaḷum iraṇṭāvatu ūḻiyil aḻintupōyiṉa. avaṟṟil miñciyirunta cila nūlkaḷum camaskiruta moḻikkalappālum camaskirutattiliruntu vantateṉṟu collum vacaṉattālum kaṟaippaṭuttappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil terikiṟatu. 
tamiḻp paṇṭitar cūriyanārāyaṇa cāstiriyār B.A.,eḻutiya tamiḻ moḻiyiṉ varalāṟu, pakkam 14, 15.
“vaṭamoḻiyāḷar tamiḻarkaḷatu oḻukkavaḻakkaṅkaḷai yuṇarntu avaṟṟiṟkēṟpa vaṭamoḻiyil nūlkaḷ vakuppāṉ pukuntaṉar. avarkaḷellām āṉma nūṟpayiṟci mikkuṭaiyārāyum, kalaiyuṇarcci cāṉṟavarāyu miruntamaipaṟṟit tamiḻaratu tivviya stalaṅkaḷukkup purāṇaṅkaḷ vakuttaṉar; tamiḻarkaḷiṭattillātirunta ‘antaṇar, aracar, vaṇikar, vēḷāḷar’ eṉṟa nālvakaiccāti muṟaiyai mella mella nāṭṭiviṭṭaṉar.
		‘muṟpaṭaippataṉil vēṟākiya muṟaimaipōl
		nālvakaic cātiyin nāṭṭiṉīr nāṭṭiṉīr!
eṉṟu āriyarai nōkki muḻaṅkuṅ kapilarakavalaiyuṅ kāṇka. iṉṉum avar tam puntinalaṅ kāṭṭit tamiḻaracarkaḷiṭam amaiccarka ḷeṉavum mēlatikārap pirapukkaḷeṉavum amaintukoṇṭaṉar; tamiḻariṭat tirunta pala ariya viṣayaṅkaḷaiyum moḻipeyarttut tamiḻar aṟiyumuṉṉarē avaṟṟait tāmaṟintaṉa pōlavum vaṭamoḻiyiṉiṉṟumē tamiḻiṟku avai vantaṉapōlavum kāṭṭiṉar. *  *  *  *  *  *
tāṅkaḷ cellumiṭaṅkaḷukkut takkapaṭi putiya putiya ilipikaḷ ēṟpaṭuttikkoḷḷu miyalpuṭaiya āriyar tamiḻnāṭṭiṟkēṟṟapaṭi, tamiḻilipiyai yoṭṭik ‘kirantam’ eṉṉum peyariṟ putuvatōr ilipi vakuttaṉar; tamiḻarai vacīkarikkumāṟu avvilipiyiṟ pala nūlkaḷ varaintaṉar. tamiḻppulavarāvār etaṟkum acaiyātu taṅkaḷ tamiḻ moḻiyiṉ pōkkaiyē taḻuvic celvārāyiṉār.”
mēṟkāṭṭiya vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil camaskiruta pāṣai yilēyē mikunta paṟṟuḷḷa oru āriyar colvatai nām oppukkoḷḷa vēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. tamiḻariṭattirunta pala ariya viṣayaṅkaḷaiyum moḻi peyarttut tamiḻar aṟiyumuṉṉamē avaṟṟait tām aṟintaṉa pōlavum vaṭamoḻiyiṉiṉṟumē tamiḻiṟku avai vantaṉapōlavum kāṭṭiṉāreṉṟu colvatai nām kavaṉikkaiyil, tamiḻiluḷḷa pala nūlkaḷ vaṭamoḻiyil māṟṟappaṭṭatākavum taṟkālamvaḻaṅkum tamiḻ nūlkaḷil camaskirutattiṟkup piṉṉē ivaikaḷuṇṭāṉavaiyeṉṟu tōṉṟumpaṭi cila tittuppāṭukaḷ aṅkaṅkē ceytatākavum niṉaikka iṭantarukiṟaJ. vaṭamoḻic campantamuḷḷa cila pērakattiyac cūttiraṅkaḷum, tolkāppiyac cūttiraṅkaḷum, cila vaittiya, vāta, yōka, ñāṉa, cōtiṭa nūlkaḷum cila tittuppāṭukaḷaiyuṭaiyaṉavāyirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collattakkatāka aṅkaṅkē cila vārttaikaḷum cūttiraṅkaḷum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. akattiyaruṭaiya māṇākkaril oruvarākiya kaḻārampar iyaṟṟiyatāka veḷi vantirukkum pēricaic cūttiram eṉṉum nūlai nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl, ivvuṇmai naṉku pulappaṭum. appustakattil mutalāvatu oli vaṭivāyirunta kālameṉṟum iraṇṭāvatu aṭcarakālameṉṟum mūṉṟāvatu ilakkaṇa kālameṉṟum nālāvatu caṅkakālameṉṟum nām aintāvatu maṭātipatikaḷiṉ ātīṉakālameṉṟum āṟāvatu camaṇakālameṉṟum ēḻāvatu purāṇakālameṉṟum eṭṭāvatu atamakālameṉṟum oṉpatāvatu tiruviḷaiyāṭaṟ kālameṉṟum pattāvatu taṟkālameṉṟum collukiṟār. ivvoḻuṅkaic cumār 34 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ vīracōḻiyattiṟkup patippurai eḻutiya ci.vai. tāmōtarampiḷḷai avarkaḷ vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇkiṟōm. ākaiyāl vicu varuṣattiṟkup piṉṉē ippēricaic cūttiram putitāka eḻutap paṭṭirukkavēṇṭum. atilum aintāvatu anātārakālameṉṟu tāmōtarampiḷḷai avarkaḷ coṉṉatai māṟṟi
		“cittelā niṟaintu cittā yamarnta
		tēcikar marapil ciṟantu viḷaṅkum
		maṭāti patikaḷā māṇpamai ñāṉiyar
		aḷaviṟ paṭuvatav vatīṉa kālam.”
eṉṉum cūttiram aintāvatāka eḻutappaṭṭirukkiṟaJ. ivaikaḷai nām kavaṉikkaiyil, akattiyaruṭaiya māṇākkar paṉṉiruvaruḷ kaḻārampar eḻutiyirukka māṭṭāreṉṟu tiṭṭamāyt terikiṟatu. tamiḻ naṭaiyum atai rucuppaṭuttukiṟaJ. mēlum akattiyar kālattilirunta kaḻārampar taṟkālam varaikkum tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉ kālavarttamāṉattai eppaṭic coṉṉār? appaṭic coṉṉatu uṇmaiyāṉāl taṟkālattukku mēlvarum piṟkālattaippaṟṟi ēṉ yātoṉṟum collavillai? itu camas kirutattil pērapimāṉamuḷḷa oruvar kaṟpaṉaiyē eṉpōm. itu pōlavē pērakattiya cūttiraṅkaḷilum tolkāppiyac cūttiraṅkaḷilum tamiḻ vaṭamoḻikkup pintiyateṉṟu kāṭṭumpaṭi ceytirukkum cila eḻuttu māṟutalkaḷaiyum vārttai māṟutalkaḷaiyum cūttiramāṟutalkaḷaiyum itu mutaṟkoṇṭāvatu tamiḻ makkaḷ ūṉṟippārppārkaḷāka. ippaṭiyē tamiḻil vaḻaṅkiya caṅkītanūlum atāvatu icaittamiḻum ātiyil camaskirutatti liruntē tamiḻarukku vantateṉṟu yāvarum eṇṇum paṭiyākiviṭṭatu. āṉāl camaskiruta nūlkaḷil vaḻaṅkivarum caṅkīta muṟaikkum teṉṉāṭṭil vaḻaṅkum karnāṭaka caṅkīta muṟaikkum mikunta vittiyāca mirukkiṟateṉṟu aṟivāḷikaḷ kāṇpārkaḷ. vaṭapāṣaiyil eḻutappaṭṭa curuti muṟaikaḷuḷḷa kāṉam teṉṉāṭṭil vaḻaṅkāmalpōṉālum vaṭapāṣaiyiluḷḷa peyarkaḷē caṅkītattil vaḻaṅkivarukiṉṟaṉa. ivviparam yāvum itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. mēlum kaṭaiccaṅka kālattil tōṉṟiya jaiṉarālum āriyarālum camaskiruta vārttaikaḷ tamiḻ moḻiyōṭu kalakka ārampittateṉṟum tamiḻ moḻikaḷ maṟṟappāṣaikaḷōṭu kalantateṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. (41-42.)
“The greater number of the Sanskrit and Pracrita words in the Dravidian languages were introduced by the Jaina writers. Some tatsamas, however, were introduced by the three comparatively modern philosophic schools: the sheiva Siddhaunta, the School of Sankaracharya and the School of Ramanoojacharya. Sanskrit words are said to have been introduced even before the time of the Jains, but it is doubtful whether these are not ancient words common to both Aryan and Dravidian languages.”
“tirāviṭapāṣaikaḷil varum camaskiruta pirākiruta vārttaikaḷil mikutiyāṉavai jaiṉa vittuvāṉkaḷāl mutal mutal tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷil upayōkikkappaṭṭaṉa. āṉālum cila taṟcamaṅkaḷ ciṟitu kālaṅkaḷukku muṉṉēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa caivacittāntam caṅkarācāriyam rāmāṉujācāriyam eṉṟa mūṉṟu camayavātikaḷālum mutal mutal ippāṣaikaḷil upayōkik kappaṭṭaṉa. jaiṉaruṭaiya kālattukku muṉṉēyē camaskiruta vārttaikaḷ upayōkikkappaṭṭu vantatākac collappaṭukiṉṟaJ. āṉāl avaikaḷ āriya tirāviṭapāṣaikaḷukkup potuvāṉa vārttaikaḷāyirukkappaṭātō eṉṟu cantēkikka iṭamirukkiṟatu.”
itil, akkālattil vaḻaṅkivanta cila moḻikaḷ āriya tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷukkup potuvāṉa vārttaikaḷāyirukkappaṭātōveṉṟu cantēkikka iṭamirukkiṟateṉkiṟār. camaskiruta pāṣaiyil mikunta vairākkiyamuḷḷa āriyar tāṅkaḷ cellumiṭaṅkaḷiluḷḷa pāṣaikaḷil vaḻaṅkum vārttaikaḷaiyum karuttukkaḷaiyum putitu putitāka amaittu nūl uṇṭākkiṉārkaḷ. ākaiyiṉāl tamiḻmoḻikaḷum camaskirutattil pala cērkkappaṭṭateṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. camaskiruta pāṣaiyōṭu cērntu pūrva culōkaṅkaḷil amaintapiṉ avvārttaikaḷ iraṇṭukkum potuvāyirukkalāmōveṉṟu cantēkikka iṭantaruvatu kāla iyalputāṉē. iraval vāṅkiṉatait tirumpak koṭukkakkūṭāteṉṟa eṇṇam vantapiṉ atai iṉanteriyāmal paṇṇuvatu ulaka iyaṟkaitāṉē. ‘iraval uṭaimai, eṉak kicaivāyirukkiṟatu, eṉ appā āṇai nāṉ koṭukka māṭṭēṉ’ eṉṟatupōla ituvumāyiṟṟu. ippaṭit tamiḻmoḻikaḷ palavum camaskirutattil kalanta piṉpum, anēka tamiḻ nūlkaḷ camaskirutattil tiruppappaṭṭapiṉpumtāṉ, camaskirutattiliruntē tamiḻmoḻikaḷ palavum, nūlkaḷ palavum vantaṉaveṉṟu collat tuṇintārkaḷeṉpataip piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell
“Professor Wilson observes that the spoken languages of the South were cultivated in imitation and privalry of the Sanskrit, and but partially aspired to an independent literature; that the pricipal compositions in Tamil, Telugu, Canarese, and Malayalam are trainslations of paraphrases from Sanskrit works; and that they largely borrow the phraseology of their originals. This representation is not perfectly correct, in so far as the Tamil is concerned; for the compositions that are universally admitted to be the ablest and finest in the language, viz., the Cural and the Chintamani, are perfectly independent of the Sanskrit, and original in design as well as in exccution.”
“teṟkē pēcappaṭukiṟa pāṣaikaḷ camaskirutattaip pārttu ataṟku māṟāka uṇṭākkappaṭṭavaikaḷeṉṟum, avaikaḷ cuya kirantaṅkaḷai uṇṭākkap pirayattaṉam paṇṇiyum palikkavillaiyeṉṟum, tamiḻ teluṅku kaṉṉaṭam malaiyāḷa pāṣaikaḷiluḷḷa mukkiyamāṉa nūlkaḷellām camaskiruta nūlkaḷiṉ moḻi peyarppukaḷēyeṉṟum, avaikaḷ camaskiruta moḻikaḷaiyum toṭarmoḻikaḷaiyum vācakappōkkaiyum naṭaiyaiyu muṭaiyavaikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟum uvilcaṉ paṇṭitar kūṟukiṟār. ippaṭi avar kūṟuvatu, tamiḻppāṣaiyiṉ viṣayattil muṟṟilum cariyalla. ēṉeṉil, antappāṣaiyil mikavum nērttiyāṉavaikaḷum ciṟantavaikaḷumeṉṟu ulakattiluḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷ yāvarālum oppukkoḷḷappaṭukiṟa tirukkuṟaḷum cintāmaṇiyum camas kirutak kalappēyiṉṟi muḻutum tamiḻmayamākavē viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, camaskiruta pāṣaiyē muntiyuṇṭāṉateṉṟum ataṟku etiriṭaiyākat tamiḻum tamiḻaiccērnta pāṣaikaḷu muṇṭākkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum tirāviṭa nūlkaḷ camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉ moḻi peyarppukaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum avaṟṟiṉ naṭai camaskirutattiṉ naṭaiyaiyē ottirukkiṟateṉṟum uvilcaṉ paṇṭitar cantēkikkiṟār. itupōlavē taṟkālattiluḷḷa camaskirutapaṇṭitar ovvoruvarum collikkoḷḷukiṟārkḷa. āriya pāṣaiyiṉ vārttaikaḷ kalantapiṉpum tamiḻ moḻikaḷai āriyapāṣai vārttaikaḷākac cērttukkoṇṭa piṉpum camaskirutattiliruntē tamiḻ vantateṉṟu collāmal vēṟē eṉṉa colvārkaḷ? ceṉṟa 800 varuṣaṅkaḷukkuḷ pala purāṇaṅkaḷum cila vētānta nūlkaḷum camaskirutattiliruntu tamiḻil moḻipeyarkkappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa eṉpatu uṇmaiyē. āyiṉum atukoṇṭu ataṟku muṉṉuḷḷa tamiḻ nūlkaḷellām camaskirutattiliruntē vantaṉa eṉṟu cātittal eṅṅaṉam poruntum? camaskirutapāṣaikaḷellām muḻutum tamiḻ vārttaikaḷāl amaintiruppataṟkut tirukkuṟaḷum cintāmaṇiyum iṅkē utāraṇamākak kāṭṭappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. ivaṟṟil vaḻaṅkivarum cila tamiḻ vārttaikaḷaic camaskiruta vārttaikaḷeṉṟu collum pētaikaḷumuṇṭu. tirukkuṟaḷ tamiḻ nāṭum tamiḻ vēntarum palavitattilum cīrkulaintu-tēyntakālam ceyyappaṭṭateṉṟu nām aṟivōm. itilum mēlāṉavaikaḷāka iyal icai nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻukkum eḻutiya nūlkaḷum nīti nūlkaḷum mutaṟ piraḷayattālum iraṇṭām piraḷayattālum aḻintupōyviṭṭaṉa. āriyarum camaṇarum tamiḻnāṭṭil kalantapiṉpum mūṉṟāvatu tamiḻc caṅkattiṉ iṟutiyilum tirukkuṟaḷ mutaliya tamiḻ nūlkaḷ veḷivantaṉa. ataṉ muṉṉuḷḷa tamiḻ moḻikaḷ mikacciṟantavakaiyeṉṟum kalappaṟṟavai yeṉṟum ciṟanta ilakkaṇamuṭaiyavai yeṉṟum tolkāppiyattāl aṟivōm. tamiḻ moḻiyiṉ toṉmaiyaik kavaṉittaṟinta evarum camaskirutattiṉiṉṟu tamiḻ vantateṉṟu collamāṭṭār.
3. tamiḻē tāyppāṣaiyāyiruntatu eṉpatu.
tamiḻppāṣaiyāṉatu vekukālaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē mikac cīrtirutta maṭaintirunta teṉpatum ilakkaṇaṅkaḷaiyuṭaiyatā yirunta teṉpatum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇpōm.
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell, P.29.
“No person who has any acquaintance with the principles of comparative philogy and who has carefully studied the Grammars and vocabularies of the Dravidian Languages, and compared them with those of the Sanskrit, can suppose that the grammatical structure and inflexional forms of those languages and the greater number of their more important roots are capable of being derived from the Sanskrit by any process of corruption whatsoever.”
“pāṣātattuva cāstiravitikaḷaik koñcamāvatu aṟintu, tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇaṅ kaḷaiyum nikaṇṭu tivākaram piṅkalantai akarāti mutalāṉavaikaḷaiyum kavaṉamāyppaṭittu, avaikaḷaic camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉ amaram mutaliya nikaṇṭukaḷōṭum akarātikaḷōṭum oppiṭṭup pārtta evarum, antappāṣaikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇavaṭivaṅkaḷum patarūpa pētaṅkaḷum vēṟṟumaikaḷum avaikaḷiṉ atika mukkiyamāṉa pakutikaḷil mikutiyāṉavaikaḷum, camaskirutat tiṉiṉṟu maruviyēṉum allatu vēṟu evvitamāyākilum citaintēṉum vantirukkumeṉṟu niṉaikkamāṭṭārkaḷ.”
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell, P.4
“This language (Tamil) being the earliest cultivated of all the Dravidian idioms, the most copious, and that which contains the largest portion and the richest variety of indubitably ancient forms, it is deservedly placed at the head of the list.”
“tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷellāvaṟṟilum tamiḻē atipūrvakālattilēyē cīrppaṭuttappaṭṭu virttiyaṭainta pāṣaiyāyum vistāramāṉa pāṣai yāyum yātoru cantēkamumiṉṟip paṇṭaiyuruccoṟkaḷai ērāḷamāyum niṟaivāyumuṭaiya pāṣaiyāyumiruppatiṉālē, atai niyāyamāy mutal mutal vaittirukkiṟaJ.”
“From the various particulars mentioned above, it appears certain that the Tamil language was of all the Dravidian idioms the earliest cultivated; it also appears highly probable, that in the endeavour to ascertain the characteristics of the primitive Dravidian speech, from which the various existing dialects have been derived, most assistance will be furnished by the Tamil.”
“mēlē kūṟiya paṟpala viṣayaṅkaḷiṉālum tirāviṭapāṣaika ḷellā vaṟṟilum tamiḻppāṣaiyē atikap pūrvīkamāṉateṉṟu niccayamāyt tōṉṟukiṟatu. taṟkālattilirukkum pala kiḷaippāṣaikaḷukku mūla pāṣaiyāyirunta tirāviṭapāṣaiyiṉ ātirūpattai aṟiya muyaṟci ceytāl, tamiḻē muṉṉiṉṟu atika utavi ceyyumeṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, tirāviṭapāṣaiyeṉṟu āriyar aḻaikkum pāṣaikaḷukkut tamiḻē tāyp pāṣaiyeṉṟum, ciṟanta ilakkaṇamuṭaiyateṉṟum, pūrvakālattatu eṉṟum, camaskirutattiṉiṉṟu muṟṟilum vēṟāṉa pāṣaiyeṉṟum teḷivākat terikiṟatu. mēlum, tolkāppiyam mikap paḻamaiyāṉateṉṟum, nuṭpamāṉa ilakkaṇa vitikaḷaiyuṭaiya teṉṟum, avar mēṟkōḷkaḷaikkoṇṭu avarukku muntiya kālattilēyē tamiḻ vitvāṉkaḷiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatai aṟiyalāmeṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency. Vol. I, P (56)
“Tamil literature is the oldest among the Dravidian languages. To the sage Agastiya (of unknown date) are attributed not only the formation of the alphabet and first treatise upon grammar, but also a number of treatises on various sciences. But nothing authentic survives from such an ancient time. The oldest extent Tamil grammar is called the “Tolgauppiam’ that is to say ‘The ancient book’: Such a work must have been preceded by centuries of literary culture as it lays down rules for different kinds of poetical compositions, duduced from examples furnished by the best authors whose works were then in existence. Its date cannot, however, be fixed.”
“tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷil eḻutappaṭṭa nūlkaḷil atikap paḻamai yāṉavai tamiḻppāṣaiyil uḷḷavai. aṟiyamuṭiyāta atipūrvakālat tirunta akattiyamuṉi, tamiḻukku neṭuṅkaṇakku ēṟpaṭutti, mutal ilakkaṇa nūlaiyum vaittiyam, cōtiṭam, ñāṉam mutaliya pala cāstira nūlkaḷaiyum eḻutiṉatākac collappaṭukiṟatu. āṉāl avvaḷavu paḻamai yāṉa kālattiliruntu niccayamāṉa yātoṉṟum namakku kiṭaikkavillai. ippōtirukkiṟa paḻamaiyāṉa tamiḻ ilakkaṇa nūlukkut “tolkāppiyam” (allatu āti pustakam) eṉṟu peyar. āṉāl antap pustakattil palavakaippākkaḷ iyaṟṟuvataṟku vēṇṭiya palavitikaḷ nuṭpamāyc collappaṭṭiruppatāl atu eḻutappaṭṭa kālattukku ati muṉṉēyē jaṉaṅkaḷ nūl eḻutuvatil atika nākarīkamaṭaintirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu veḷiyākiṟatu. yāppiyalukku vēṇṭiya vitikaḷukku utāraṇaṅkaḷ akkālattukku muṉṉuḷḷa vitvāṉkaḷiṉ nūlkaḷiliruntu eṭukkap paṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. ākilum tolkāppiyam eḻutappaṭṭa kālattai niccayikka muṭiyāJ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vākkiyaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, tamiḻppāṣaikku eḻuttukkaḷ amaittu ilakkaṇam vakuttu ilakkiyam ceytavar akastiyareṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. avarirunta kālam teriyavillai. āṉāl tolkāppiyar kālattiṟku muṉṉālēyē anēka vitvāṉkaḷirun tiruppatākat terikiṟateṉkiṟār. akattiyar kālattiṟku muṉpē tamiḻp pāṣaiyiruntateṉṟum avarkaḷ tamiḻppāṣaiyil ciṟanta nūlkaḷ ceytiruntārkaḷeṉṟum akattiyar tāmē colliyirukkiṟār. atu piṉvarum cūttiraṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
pērakattiyam
		“ilakkaṇa meṉpatilakkiya muṟaiyuṟa
		vaittateṉṟu vaḻaṅkappaṭumē.”
		“ilakkiya miṉṟi yilakkaṇamiṉṟē
		eḷḷiṉṟāki leṇṇeyumiṉṟē
		eḷḷiṉiṉṟeṇ ṇeyeṭuppatu pōla
		ilakkiyattiṉiṉ ṟeṭupaṭu milakkaṇam.”
itaṉāl akattiyarukku muṉṉālēyē tamiḻ mikat tērcci peṟṟiruntateṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. tiruviḷaiyāṭaṟpurāṇamum itaṉai valiyuṟuttukiṟaJ. akkālattirunta tamiḻiṉ tērccikkut takuntapaṭi iyal icai nāṭakam eṉṉum muttamiḻukkum ilakkaṇam vakuttāreṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu.
		“vaṭavēṅkaṭan teṉkumariyāyiṭait
		tamiḻkūṟu nallulakattu
		vaḻakkuñ ceyyuḷumāyirumutaliṉ
		eḻuttuñ collum poruḷunāṭic
		centamiḻiyaṟkai civaṇiyanilattoṭu
		muntunūlkaṇṭu muṟaippaṭaveṇṇip
		pulantokuttōṉē. - - - -  ”
itil tolkāppiyar tamakku muṉṉuḷḷa tamiḻnūlkaḷai ārāycci ceytu, tamiḻ kūṟum nallulakattu vaḻakkum ceyyuḷumākiya iruvaḻiyilum vaḻaṅkum eḻuttu, col, poruḷkaḷukku ilakkaṇam coṉṉatākac collukiṟār. itaikkoṇṭu tamiḻiṉ pūrvamum ataṉ pūraṇa nilaiyum teḷivāka aṟiyalām.
4. tamiḻpāṣaiyiṉ vārttaikaḷai varttaka campantamuḷḷa maṟṟa tēcattavar vaḻaṅkivantārkaḷeṉpaJ.
mēlum teṉṉintiyāvōṭu varttakañ ceyta kirēkkarum piṉīciyarum viyāpārap poruḷākak koṇṭupōṉa vastukkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷait taṅkaḷ pāṣaiyil appaṭiyē vaḻaṅkivantārkaḷeṉṟu piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary
“It is said that the Language of the Mountaineers of Rajah Mahal abounds in terms identified with Tamil and Telugu. What is more singular, the names by which the ivory, apes, and peacocks, conveyed by Solomon’s ships of Tarshish were known, are the same with those still used in Tamil; seeming to imply that the traders visited Ceylon, or India, and obtained with these novelties their Tamil names, Danta, Kapi, and Togai, as found in the Hebrew Bible.”
“rājāmahāl malaittēcavācikaḷ pāṣaiyil, tamiḻ teluṅku moḻikaḷ ērāḷamāyiruk kiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu. cālōmō ṉuṭaiya tarṣīsiṉ kappalkaḷ koṇṭupōṉa tantam, kuraṅku, mayil mutalāṉavaikaḷiṉ peyarkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, avaikaḷ taṟkālattilum tamiḻppāṣaiyil vaḻaṅki varukiṟa peyarkaḷeṉṟum, aṉṉiya tēcatti liruntu viyāpārikaḷ ilaṅkai intiyā mutalāṉaviṭaṅkaḷukku vantiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, apporuḷkaḷōṭu epirēya paipiḷil kāṇappaṭum tantam, kapi, tōkai eṉṉum avaikaḷiṉ tamiḻp peyarkaḷaiyum kēṭṭaṟintu koṇṭu pōṉārkaḷeṉṟum teriyavarukiṟatu.”
mēṟkāṭṭiyapaṭi intiyāvil viyāpārañceyta kālam kiṟistuvukkuc caṟṟēṟakkuṟaiya 1,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṟpaṭṭaJ. cumār 3,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ teṉṉintiyāvōṭu viyāpāra mūlamāyc campantappaṭṭavarkaḷ intiyāviṟku māttira muriyatāṉa viyāpārac carakkukaḷiṉ peyarkaḷait tamiḻilēyē colli vantirukkavēṇṭum. āṉāl varttakarkaḷ entac carakkukaḷai intiyāvil iṟakkumati paṇṇiṉārkaḷeṉṟu teriyavillai. intiyāviṟku nūtaṉamākat tōṉṟum kōlikaḷ pīṅkāṉkaḷ viḷaiyāṭṭup poruḷkaḷ mutaliyavaikaḷait tavira vēṟu etai iṟakkumati ceytiruppārkaḷ? ākaiyiṉāl  kirēkku, epirēyu mutaliya aṉṉiyapāṣaikaḷiṉ moḻikaḷ, tamiḻōṭu atikamāyk kalantirukkamāṭṭāveṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P (48,)
“The Phoenicians were the first to adopt a purely alphabetic system. The general voice of antiquity gives them this credit and the facts agree with the rumour. The Indo-Arabian alphabet is held to represent the Himyarite of South Arabia and the alphabets of India as shown in the Asoka’s inscriptions. It will be seen later that this scheme does not provide for the original alphabet of the Dravidian nations, which remains thus unaffiliated in the same way as are the Dravidian languages themselves.”
“eḻuttukkaḷai mutalmutal upayōkittavarkaḷ piṉīciyar. pūrva nūlkaḷellām itaippaṟṟic cāṭci koṭukkiṉṟaṉa. viṣayaṅkaḷum avaikaḷukku ottirukkiṉṟaṉa. acōkaṉuṭaiya cilai eḻuttukkaḷaip pārttāl, intiya arapiyaruṭaiya eḻuttukkaḷellām teṉ arapiyāviluḷḷa himayāriṭi eḻuttukkaḷiṉiṉṟum intiyāviṉ eḻuttukkaḷiṉiṉṟum uṇṭāṉavaiyeṉṟu terikiṟatu. inta muṟaiyil tirāviṭaruṭaiya eḻuttu muṟai uṟpattiyaippaṟṟi yātoṉṟum collavillai. tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷ eppaṭi oṉṟōṭum cērāmal taṉimai yāy niṟkiṉṟaṉavō appaṭiyē tirāviṭa eḻuttukkaḷum niṟkiṉṟaṉa.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil, āciyātturukkiyil mattiya taraikkaṭalai yaṭutta piṉīciya nāṭṭilēyē mutalmutal eḻuttukkaḷ uṇṭāyiṉaveṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu. acōkaṉuṭaiya cilai eḻuttu, teṉ arēpiyāviṉ cittira eḻuttukkaḷālum intiya eḻuttukkaḷālumuṇṭāṉa teṉṟu collukiṟār. āṉāl tirāviṭa pāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷ avaikaḷōṭu cērāmal taṉittu niṟkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu colvatai nām kavaṉikkavēṇṭum.
5. tirāviṭapāṣaiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷ piṉīciya pāṣaiyiliruntum camaskiruta pāṣaiyiliruntum uṇṭākavillaiyeṉpaJ.
intiya pāṣaiyiṉ aṭcaraṅkaḷ piṉīciya pāṣaiyiliruntu uṇṭā yirukkamāṭṭāveṉṟu aṭiyil varum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Sanskrit-English Dictionary by Monier Williams, M.A., Preface, P. XVI.
“According to Mr. Edward Thomas (Prinsep’s Indian Antiquities, Vol. II. Page 42) the theory by which Professor Weber has sought to establish a Phoenician origin for the Indian alphabets is untenable. There are, however, two sets of Buddhist inscriptions, and that of Kapurdigiri is decidedly traceable to a Phoenician source. Those on the rock of Girnar (Giri-nagara) in Kathywar, Gujarat, which are said to be most important in their relation to the present Indian alphabets, are not so clearly traceable. Mr. Thomas appears to have good ground for thinking that many of the Nagari letters were derived from the Dravidians of the south.”
“eṭvarṭ tāmas eṉpavar (intiya purātaṉaṅkaḷ 2-m pākam, pakkam 42.) collukiṟatāvatu intiya pāṣaiyiṉ aṭcaram piṉīciya pāṣaiyeḻutti liruntu vantateṉṟu uvepar paṇṭitar collukiṟatu niṟkakkūṭiya ātāramuṭaiyatalla. eṉṟālum, iruvitamāṉa puttacilācācaṉaṅkaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟuḷ kappurṭikiriyiluḷḷa cilaiyeḻuttukkaḷ piṉīciya akṣarattiliruntu uṇṭāyiruppatākat teriya varukiṟatu. kujarāttiluḷḷa kattiyavāril kirinakarā eṉṉum pāṟaiyil kāṇappaṭum mika mukkiyamāṉavaiyum taṟkāla intiya pāṣaikaḷiṉ akṣaraṅkaḷukkuc campantamuṭaiyavaiyumāṉa eḻuttukkaḷ eṉṟu collappaṭupavai avvaḷavu teḷivāṉa oṟṟumaiyillātavaikaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. nākari eḻuttukkaḷil anēkam teṉṉintiyāviluḷḷa tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷiliruntu vantateṉṟu tāmas turai ūkikkiṟataṟku ēṟṟa mukāntaraṅkaḷirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉak kūṟukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil cilācācaṉaṅkaḷaikkoṇṭu intiyapāṣai piṉīciyapāṣaikku avvaḷavu  ottirukkavillaiyeṉṟu collukiṟatākak kāṇkiṟōm. ākaiyiṉāl intiya pāṣai piṉīciya pāṣaiyi liruntuṇṭāṉatalla veṉpatu terikiṟatu. tamiḻppāṣai camaskiruta pāṣaiyiliruntuṇṭāṉa pāṣaiyalla. ataṉ ilakkaṇam camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉ ilakkaṇattiṟku vittiyācamuṭaiyatā yirukkiṟateṉṟu aṭiyilvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil pārppōm.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“Unlike several of the vernaculars of India, it is not, as some have supposed, a daughter of the Sanskrit. Its Alphabet differs not only in character, but in sound; and is more limited. Its grammar, though conformed to the Sanskrit, as far as the genius of the Language would allow, is still very different. It has not article, no relative pronoun, no dual number, no optative mood. It differs in its numerals, in many nouns, and adverbs, and in technical terms in grammar. In the declension of its nouns, the conjugation of its verbs, and the arrangement of its sentences, it more resembles the Latin.”
“cilar niṉaikkiṟapaṭi, intiyāvil vaḻaṅkivarum anēka cutēca pāṣaikaḷaippōl tamiḻppāṣai camaskirutattiliruntu uṇṭāṉatalla. atiṉ akṣaram vaṭivattil māttiramalla, ōcaiyilum vittiyācappaṭukiṟaJ. curukkamāyumirukkiṟaJ. atiṉ ilakkaṇam, pāṣaip pōkku iṭaṅ koṭukkiṟa maṭṭukkum camaskiruta pāṣaiyiṉ ilakkaṇattai aṉucarittuc ceyyappaṭṭi runtālum, perum vitkkiṟaJ. ya, yan, vāna eṉṉum (yacavaiuṭanaḷa) pōṉṟa kuṟippiṭaic coṟkaḷāvatu piratip peyarc coṟkaḷāvatu tuvivacaṉam eṉṉum irumaikāṭṭum coṟkaḷāvatu atil kiṭaiyātu. eṇṇuccoṟkaḷilum, anēka peyarccoṟkaḷilum, viṉaic coṟkaḷilum, viṉaiyuriccoṟkaḷilum, ilakkaṇa paripāṣaiccoṟkaḷilum vittiyāca muṭaiyatāyirukkiṟatu. peyarccoṟkaḷ viṉaiccoṟkaḷiṉ rūpa pētaṅkaḷilum, vākkiya amaippilum atu lattīṉ pāṣaiyai ottirukkiṟaJ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil, camaskiruta pāṣaiyaiyum tamiḻp pāṣaiyaiyum ilakkaṇattil oppiṭṭukkāṭṭi tamiḻ lattīṉ pāṣaikku ottirukkiṟateṉṟum ataṉ coṟkaḷum eḻuttukkaḷum curukkamāṉavai yeṉṟum ōcaiyilum vaṭivattilum camaskirutattiṟku vittiyācamāṉavai yeṉṟum collukiṟār. ciṟu kuḻantaiyiṉ vāyiṉāl colvataṟkēṟṟa mataḷaic (maḻalai) coṟkaḷum, tērnta vitvāṉkaḷ collat takunta uyarnta vārttaikaḷum cātāraṇa jaṉaṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ karuttaip piṟarukkut terivikkap pōtumāṉa eḷitāṉa vārttaikaḷumuṭaiyatu tamiḻppāṣaiyē. yāvarum mikacculapamāy uccarikkavum uccarittatiṉpaṭi eḻutavum eḻutiya eḻuttukkaḷ yāvum tirumpa uccarikkakkūṭiyatākavum viḷaṅki niṟpatu tamiḻē. camaskirutapāṣaiyiṉ vārttaikaḷ kalavāmal tamiḻ taṉiyāy pēcap paṭakkūṭiya pāṣaiyeṉṟu aṭiyilvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷilkāṇalām.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“It is evident that there was an early literature in Tamil independent of Sanskrit; it is certain that Tamil could do without Sanskrit much better than English without Latin. *  *  *  *  *
The reason why Tamil is more independent of Sanskrit than the Northern Languages, and even than the other Dravidian tongues, is, that it has not been left, like those, principally to the cultivation of the Brahmans.”
“pūrvatamiḻnūlkaḷ camaskirutak kalappillāmalirukkiṉṟaṉa. lattīṉ kalavāmal iṅkilīṣai eḻutakkūṭiyataippārkkilum atika naṉṟāyc camaskirutam kalavāmal tamiḻp pāṣaiyai yeḻutalām. *  *  *  *  *
tamiḻil camaskiruta pāṣai atikamāyk kalavātiruppataṟkuk kāraṇameṉṉaveṉṟāl vaṭapāṣaikaḷaippōlum, maṟṟattirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷaip pōlum, pirāmaṇa nūlāciriyarkaḷ anēkar tamiḻppāṣaik killāmaṟpōṉatē.”
mutal iraṇṭu caṅkaṅkaḷilumirunta icai nūlkaḷum nāṭakanūlkaḷum aḻintu kuṟaivu paṭṭa kālattil teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītattai pūraṇamāykkaṟṟum ataṟkuriya cila nūlkaḷ eḻutiyum taṟkālam varaiyum nīṭittirukkum nilaikkup perumpālum āriya vitvacirōmaṇikaḷ kāraṇamāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟu collavum naṉṟipārāṭṭavum tamiḻmakkaḷ maṟantu pōkakkūṭāJ.    
6. tamiḻ pala ciṟanta kalaikaḷaiyuṭaiya pāṣai eṉpatu.
tamiḻppāṣai mika nērttiyāṉa pataṅkaḷuṭaiya pāṣaiyeṉṟum, pūrvīka nūlkaḷaiyum pulavarkaḷaiyumuṭaiyateṉṟum, piṉvarum vacaṉattil nām kāṇpōm.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“A native author of repute, well versed in English, as well as his own vernacular, has said, adopting the words of Mr. Taylor before mentioned ‘it is one of the most copious, refined, and polished languages spoken by man’. This author has added, what may admit of doubt, ‘few nations on earth can perhaps boast of so many poets as the Tamils’. As, however, all their carlier literature was in poetry, even Dictionaries and Grammars, and works on Medicine, Law, Architecture and Theology, the Number of poets, so called, must have been great.”
“āṅkilēya pāṣaiyait tīrakkaṟṟavarum, tammuṭaiya cuyapāṣaiyiṉ tiṟamai peṟṟavarumāṉa ōr pērpōṉa cutēca nūlāciriyar, misṭar ṭēylar colvataṟku oṟṟumaiyāka, maṉitar pēcum pāṣaikaḷuḷ tamiḻppāṣai anēka apippirāyaṅkaḷait terivikkakkūṭiyatum, mika nērttiyum ērāḷamumāṉa coṟkaḷaiyuṭaiyatumāṉa pāṣaiyeṉṟu terivikkiṟār. piṉṉum avar tamiḻp pāṣaiyil tērnta pulavarkaḷ ērāḷamāyiruppatu pōl ulakattil maṟṟa entappāṣaiyilumillai eṉṟu kūṟukiṟār. avarkaḷiṉ pūrvīka kāviyaṅkaḷ, akarātikaḷ, ilakkaṇa nūlkaḷ, vaittiya cāstiraṅkaḷ, caṭṭacāstiraṅkaḷ ciṟpacāstiraṅkaḷ, vētacāstiraṅkaḷ mutalāṉavaikaḷellām ceyyuḷilēyēyiruppatāl, avaikaḷiṉ nūlāciriyarkaḷākiya pulavarkaḷum ērāḷam.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, oru pāṣaikkum oru tēcattukkumuriya kalaikaḷ yāvum tamiḻiliruntatākak kāṇkiṟōm. aṟupattunālu kalaikaḷaiyum ovvoṉṟum oru tiruviḷaiyāṭṭākap paramacivaṉ naṭattikkāṭṭiyatu tamiḻ nāṭṭilallavō. ākaiyiṉāl vēṇṭum kalaikaḷ yāvum pūrva tamiḻ nāṭṭiliruntaṉaveṉṟum piṉ aḻintupōyiṉaveṉṟum nām niṉaikkavēṇṭum.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“It is not perhaps extravagant to say, that, in its poetic form, the Tamil is more polished and exact than the Greek, and in both dialects, with its borrowed treasures, more copious than the Latin. In its fulness and power it more resembles English and German than any other living language. Its prose style is yet in a forming state, and will well repay the labor of accurate scholars in moulding it properly. Many natives who write poetry readily, cannot write a page of correct prose.”
“tamiḻp pāṣaiyāvatu ceyyuḷ naṭaiyil kirēkka pāṣaiyaip pārkkilum atika paḷapaḷappum tiruttamum poruntiyatāy, karuttukkaḷai atikattiṭṭamāyk kāṭṭakkūṭiyatāyirukkiṟateṉṟu colvatu atai maṭṭukku (aḷavukku) miñcippukaḻvatākāJ. atu maṟṟap pāṣaikaḷiliruntu cērttukkoṇṭa moḻikaḷuḷpaṭa ceyyuḷ naṭaiyilum vācaka naṭaiyilum ataṟkirukkum moḻikaḷ lattīṉ moḻikaḷaiviṭa atika ērāḷamām. ataṉ niṟaivilum pelattilum taṟkālattilirukkum maṟṟentap pāṣaiyaiyum viṭa iṅkilīṣ jarmaṉ pāṣaikaḷai ottirukkiṟaJ. ataṟku vacaṉa naṭai paṭippaṭiyāy ēṟpaṭṭukkoṇṭu varukiṟatu: ataic cariyāṉa naṭaiyil oḻuṅkupaṭutta muyalukiṟavarkaḷ taṅkaḷ pirayācattiṉ palaṉaiyaṭaiyāmaṟ pōkamāṭṭārkaḷ. coṉṉavuṭaṉē kavi yeḻutakkūṭiya anēka tamiḻarkaḷ piḻaiyillāmal vacaṉam oru pakkaṅkūṭa eḻuta muṭiyātavarkaḷā yirukkiṟārkaḷ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil tamiḻppāṣaiyāṉatu āṅkilēya jermāṉiya pāṣaikaḷaippōlap pūraṇamum ciṟappum peṟṟu pēccu vaḻakkuṭaiya uyirppāṣaiyāy viḷaṅkukiṟa teṉkiṟār.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary
“The Tamil is not a vulgar dialect. Before the principal basis of the English had a written character, it was a highly polished language. Its name signifies sweetness, and though not so musical as the Telugu, in its poetic form especially, it is not without its claim to cuphonic charms, and ‘linked sweetness.”
“tamiḻppāṣai koccaiyāṉa pāṣaiyalla. āṅkilēya pāṣaikku eḻuttu vaṭivam ēṟpaṭuvataṟku muṉṉamē tamiḻ paḷapaḷappum maḻamaḻappum mikuntu tērnta ciṟanta pāṣaiyāyiruntaJ. tamiḻ eṉpataṟku maturam eṉṟu arttamām. teluṅkaippōl avvaḷavu iṉiya ōcai yuḷḷatalla veṉṟālum kēṭpavarkaḷukku ānantattai viḷaivippatilum toṭarcci yāṉa iṉimai payappatilum tamiḻppāṣaikkuc caktiyillāmaṟ pōka villai.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷai nām kavaṉikkaiyil, iṅkilīṣpāṣaikku eḻuttukkaḷuṇṭāvataṟku muṉṉālēyē tamiḻppāṣai ciṟantu viḷaṅkiṉateṉṟu terikiṟatu.
7. camaskirutapāṣai yuṇṭāṉataippaṟṟiya cila kuṟippukaḷ.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“In the opinion of the Rev. William Taylor, the able Editor of Dr. Rottler’s Dictionary, ‘there was originally one simple homogeneous dialect, spoken by rude, simple aborigines from the Himalaya to Cape Comorin ‘ Mr. Taylor thinks that, ‘the earliest probable refinement of it was the Pali of the North, and the Tamil of the extreme South,’ and that, ‘the Sanscrit assumed its own form by engrafting numerous Chaldaic terms of science and others of common use in the old Pali.’ It is evident from their names, that the Pali must have been anterior to the Sanscrit, the former signifying root or original, and the latter finished or polished. It is stated by Colonel Sykes that very ancient inscriptions on rocks and coins, are found in Pali and Pracrit four hundred years earlier than in Sanscrit.”
“rāṭlar akarātiyaip piracurañceytavarāṉa villiyam ṭēylar eṉpavar, ātiyil imaya parvata mutal kaṉṉiyākumarimuṉaivarai, nākarīkamillātavarkaḷum pētamaiyuḷḷavarkaḷumāṉa pūrvīkak kuṭikaḷukkuḷ yātoru kalappillāta orēpāṣai pēcappaṭṭu vantateṉṟum, atu mutaṟ cīrtiruttattai yaṭaintu vaṭa tēcattil pāliyākavum teṉkōṭiyil tamiḻākavum vaḻaṅkiyateṉṟum, paḻaiya pālipāṣaiyil ērāḷamāṉa kaltēya cāstira campantamāṉa paripāṣaikaḷaiyum cātāraṇa vaḻakkatti luḷḷa anēka moḻikaḷaiyum cērttukkoṇṭu camaskirutapāṣaiyai yuṇṭākkiṉār kaḷeṉṟum apippirāyappaṭukiṟār. intap pāṣaikaḷiṉ pērkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil pālipāṣai camaskirutattiṟku muntiṉateṉṟu teḷivākiṟaJ. ‘pāli’ eṉṟāl mūlam allatu muntiṉatu eṉṟum, ‘camaskirutam’ eṉṟāl tiruttam peṟṟatu allatu cemmaiyākkappaṭṭatu eṉṟum arttam. kaṟpāṟaikaḷilum nāṇayaṅkaḷilumuḷḷa atipūrvīka cāsaṉaṅkaḷil pāliyilum pirākirutattilum eḻutappaṭṭavaikaḷ camas kirutattil eḻutappaṭṭavaikaḷukku 400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muntiṉavaikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu karṉel caiks kūṟukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷai nām kavaṉikkaiyil, intiyāviṉ pūrvattōr imayamalai mutal kaṉṉiyākumari varaiyum kuṭiyiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, avarkaḷuḷ yātoru kalappumaṟṟa orē pāṣai pēcappaṭṭu vantateṉṟum, appāṣai vaṭapākattil pāliyeṉṟum teṉpākattil tamiḻ eṉṟum collappaṭṭateṉṟum collukiṟār. pāli pāṣaiyiliruntum aṉēka kaltēya pataṅkaḷiṉiṉṟum kalantu eṭukkappaṭṭu camaskirutameṉṟa oru pāṣaiyuṇṭāṉateṉṟu collukiṟār. pūrvīkattil kallil eḻutappaṭṭa eḻuttukkaḷaikkoṇṭum nāṇayaṅkaḷiluḷḷa eḻuttukkaḷaikoṇṭum pāli pāṣaiyē pūrvamāṉateṉṟu terikiṟatu. pālipāṣaiyaippōla pirākiruta pāṣaikaḷum camaskirutattaiviṭa 400-varuṣaṅkaḷukku muntiṉatu eṉṟum pālipāṣaiyai ātimūlapāṣai yeṉṟum camaskirutam atiliruntu eṭukkappaṭṭu muṭivu peṟṟirukkiṟa pāṣaiyeṉṟum collukiṟār. itaikkoṇṭu pāli pāṣaiyiliruntum pirākiruta pāṣaiyiliruntum naṉṟāyuṇṭākkappaṭṭa pāṣai camaskiruta meṉṟu terikiṟatu. camaskirutam eṉṟa peyarukkē ‘naṉṟāyc ceyyappaṭṭatu’ eṉṟu arttamām. pāli eṉpatu mūlameṉṟum pirākirutiyeṉpatu mutal eṉṟum arttamām. ivvārttaikaḷiṉ arttaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, āti yākavum mutalākavumuḷḷa pala pāṣaikaḷiliruntu moḻikaḷ cērkkappaṭṭu ataṟkiṇaṅka ilakkaṇamuñ ceyyappaṭṭu naṉṟāyt tiruttappaṭṭa pāṣai camaskirutameṉṟu viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. āriyarkaḷ tāṅkaḷ pirayāṇam paṇṇivanta tēcaṅkaḷiluḷḷa pāṣaiyiṉ palamoḻikaḷaiyum oṉṟu cērttu, camaskirutameṉṟa putuppāṣai oṉṟu ceytārkaḷeṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. appaṭiyē kirēkku, lattīṉ, epirēyu, cīttiyam, pāli, pirākirutam, tamiḻ mutaliya pāṣaikaḷ itil kalantirukkiṟatait taṟkāla aṉupōkattāl kāṇkiṟōm. mēlum iṭukuṟippeyarkaḷē āti vārttaikaḷā yirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum ataṉ piṉpē kāraṇappeyarkaḷ vaḻaṅkakkūṭiyateṉṟum aṟivāḷikaḷ yāvarum oppukkoḷvārkaḷ. caṅkīta cāstirattilum pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷ vaḻaṅkivanta iyaṟkaiyāṉa moḻikaḷ niṟka, ovvoṉṟiṟkum kāraṇappeyarkaḷ amaittu vaḻaṅki varuvataik kavaṉittāl, camaskirutattiluḷḷa caṅkīta cāstiraṅkaḷ yāvum piṟkālattiluṇṭāṉavaiyeṉṟē niṉaikka ētuvirukkiṟatu.
8. epirēya cīttiya airōppiya camaskirutapāṣaikaḷil tamiḻ moḻikaḷ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpataṟkuc cila tiruṣṭāntam.
pāṣaikaḷil ciṟantatākac collappaṭum camaskiruta pāṣaiyum cila vārttaikaḷait tamiḻppāṣaiyiliruntu eṭuttiruppatākap piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“While nearly all the vernaculars of India have been greatly enriched from the Sanscrit, that wonderful language has condescended to borrow even from the Dravidian group, of which the Tamil is the oldest, and the principal. Dr. Caldwell in his learned Dravidian Comparative Grammar, instances 31 words in Sanscrit taken from Dravidian tongues, and 25 borrowed by both from some common source. He is of opinion that the Sanscrit derived its cerebral consonants from the Dravidian.”
“intiyāviluḷḷa maṟṟellāppāṣaikaḷum kaṭaṉvāṅkikkoḷḷum paṭiyāka avvaḷavu ciṟanta tāy camaskirutamiruntālum, atuvum tirāviṭa pāṣaiyiliruntu kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyirukkiṟatu. tirāviṭa pāṣaikaḷil atikap pūrvīkamāṉatum mukkiyamāṉatum tamiḻtāṉ. ṭākṭar kālṭuvel tammuṭaiya ciṟanta tirāviṭapāṣai ilakkaṇattil, tirāviṭapāṣaiyi liruntu camaskirutattil cērkkap paṭṭirukkiṟataṟku utāraṇamāka 31 vārttaikaḷum, camaskirutamum tirāviṭamum vēṟoru potuppāṣaiyi liruntu kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyiruppataṟku utāraṇamāka 25 vārttaikaḷum kāṭṭiyirukkiṟār. camaskirutapāṣaiyāṉatu neñciliruntu varakkūṭiya cila meyyeḻuttukkaḷai (ṭa ṇa ḷa ra) tirāviṭa pāṣaiyiliruntu cērttukkoṇṭatāka apippirāyappaṭukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil pala tamiḻ moḻikaḷ camaskiruta pāṣaiyil cērntiruppatākavum camaskirutattiṟkum tamiḻukkum potuvāṉa vēṟu pala vārttaikaḷ camaskirutattil varuvatākavum tamiḻukkuriya ṭa ṇa ḷa ra eṉṟa eḻuttukkaḷ tamiḻiliruntu camaskirutat tiṟku eṭuttuk koḷḷap paṭṭaṉaveṉṟum collukiṟār. mēlum, oru kālattil oru iṭattiliruntē jātikaḷ yāvarum taṉittaṉi pirintupōyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum, avarkaḷ pirintu pōkumuṉ tamiḻp pāṣaiyaiyē pēcivantārkaḷeṉṟum, pala kāraṇaṅkaḷaikkoṇṭu ūkikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. ulakaccarittiram uṇṭāvataṟku muṉṉālēyē ippērppaṭṭa oru perum pirivu uṇṭāyirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu. appaṭippirintupōṉavarkaḷ tāṅkaḷ pūrvamāyp pēcivanta pāṣaiyaiyē araikuṟaiyāyp pēciyirukkavēṇṭum. ēṉeṉṟāl, jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻikkappaṭum jaṉaṅkaḷil, aṉēka aṟivāḷikaḷum celvavāṉkaḷum tappittukkoḷḷat tiṟamaiyaṟṟirukka, cātāraṇa kūli vēlaiceykiṟavaṉum nīntat terintavaṉumē tappip piḻaikka muṭiyum. cātāraṇa maṉitaṉukku 200, 300 vārttaikaḷ terintirukkavēṇṭumāṉāl maṟṟoruvaṉ kūṭṭuṟavum vēṇṭum. illaiyāṉāl maṟantupōm. maṟantapiṉ taṅkaḷukkuppiriyamāṉatum lēcāṉatumāṉa nūtaṉa vārttaikaḷaik koṇṭē ulakakkāriyaṅkaḷ pēccuppaḻakkattiṟku varavēṇṭum. ippaṭi vekukālam ceṉṟu viṭṭāl, taṅkaḷ pūrva vārttaikaḷ vekutūram māṟi, mutal eḻuttu nīṇṭum kuṟukiyum iṉa eḻuttu vantum vaḻaṅkum. appaṭiyē kaṭai eḻuttukkeṭṭum nīṇṭum kuṟukiyum vēṟoru eḻuttu vantu viraviyum vaḻaṅkum. ippaṭip pala pāṣaikaḷilum vaḻaṅkum vārttaikaḷai oruṅku cērttup pārppōmāṉāl, 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta tolkāppiyar kālattil 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷāka irunta mutaṟcaṅkattār ātarittu vanta tamiḻppāṣaiyē pūrvamāṉa pāṣaiyeṉṟum, avarkaḷirunta lemūriyā aḻintapiṉ taṅkaḷukku etirppaṭṭa karaikaḷukkut tamiḻar kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum, tiṭṭamākac collalām. avarkaḷ citaṟippōṉa piṉ tēca cītōṣṇa nilaikkut takuntapaṭiyum ākārātikaḷukkut takuntapaṭiyum kaittoḻilukkum kalvikkum takuntapaṭiyum vevvēṟu pāṣaikkārarākavum tēcattārākavum aḻaikkappaṭṭārkaḷ eṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. mēṟpaṭi jātiyār pēcum pāṣaikaḷil tamiḻ vārttaikaḷ kāṇappaṭuvatē ataṟkup pōtumāṉa attāṭci. camaskiruta pāṣaiyil anēka vārttaikaḷum cila eḻuttukkaḷum tamiḻiliruntu eṭukkappaṭṭu vaḻakkattilirukkiṉṟaṉa.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“But from affinities traced out by him, in addition to those hereafter given, it would seen that we may go farther back for many roots and forms in these tongues, to some common fountain both for them and for the languages of the Indo-European family, including Sanscrit; nearer to the time when ‘the whole earth was of one language.’ He specifies 85 words in the Dravidian, as having Seythian affinities, 31 as Semitic, and 106 connectedwith the west Indo European family, distinct from those in Sanscrit..”
