“ellāc caṅkītattukkum ātāram taṉiccuraṅkaḷai oṉṟiṉpiṉ oṉṟāyc colluvatutāṉ (melody). itu intiya caṅkītattil pūraṇa aḷavāy irukkiṟatu eṉṟu ellōrum oppuk koḷḷukiṟārkaḷ. anēka āyira varuṣaṅkaḷāy intiya caṅkītam iṉimaiyāyiruppataṟkuk kāraṇam ituvē. itu kāraṇam paṟṟiyē varappōkiṟa varuṣaṅkaḷilum atu veku piracittamāyirukkum eṉpataṟkuc cantēkamillai.”
ivvākkiyaṅkaḷil rākam pāṭuvatu mika iṉimaiyuṭaiyateṉṟu collukiṟār. iṉi varappōkuṅ kālaṅkaḷilum itu mikavum mēṉmai yuṭaiyatāka eṇṇappaṭumeṉṟum niccayañ collukiṟār. uṇmai yilēyē avaikaḷ mēṉmaiyuṭaiyavaikaḷā yiruppataṟkuk kāraṇam piṉvarum vākkiyattil kāṇalām.

The Indian Empire by W.W. Hunter. P. III.
“It is, indeed, impossible to adequately represent the Indian system by the European notation; and the full range of its effects can only be rendered by Indian instruments a vast collection of sound-producers, slowly elaborated during 2,000 years to suit the special requirements of Hindu music. The complicated structure of its musical modes (rags) rests upon three separate systems, one of which consists of five, another of six, and the other of seven notes. It preserves in a living state some of the early forms which puzzle the student of Greek music, side by side with the most complicated developments.”
 “intiya irākaṅkaḷai airōppiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum kōṭukaḷ nōṭṭukkaḷ mūlamāy curappaṭuttuvatu kūṭātakāriyam. anta irākaṅkaḷiṉ muḻu iṉimaiyaiyum pirastārattaiyum aṟiya vēṇṭu māṉāl intiya caṅkīta vāttiyaṅkaḷ mūlamāyttāṉ aṟiyalām. inta vāttiyaṅkaḷ oṉṟiraṇṭalla, nēṟṟu muṉṟānāḷ uṇṭāṉavaiyumalla. avaikaḷiṉ tokai atikam. avaiyuṇṭāṉa kālamum 2,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉākum. avai orē kālattil uṇṭāṉavaiyumalla. antantak kālattukkum avaciyattukkum takkapaṭi paṭippaṭiyāy 2,000 varuṣaṅkaḷākac cōtikkappaṭṭum pirastārattukkuk koṇṭuvarappaṭṭavai. intiya caṅkītattukku ātāramāyirukkappaṭṭa ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷ mūṉṟu vakaiyuḷḷaṉa. oṉṟu 5 curaṅkaḷum, oṉṟu 6 curaṅkaḷum, oṉṟu 7 curaṅkaḷumuḷḷaṉa. ātiyil ēṟpaṭṭuḷḷa inta ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷ avvaḷavu cikkumukkāy iruppatāl kirēkka caṅkītam aṟintavarkaḷkūṭa avaikaḷaik kaṇṭāl yāteṉṟaṟiyāmal tikaippārkaḷ.”
ivvacaṉaṅkaḷai kavaṉikkaiyil itai eḻutiyavar intiya caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ nuṭpaṅkaḷai naṉṟāy aṟintavareṉpatu viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. mutal mutal airōppiyac curattaik kōṭukaḷil kuṟikkum vaḻakkampōla kuṟikkak kūṭāta vitamāka nuṭpamāṉa curaṅkaḷirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collukiṟār. annuṭpamāṉa curaṅkaḷaiyum intiya caṅkīta vāttiyaṅkaḷiṉ mūlamāyttāṉ aṟiyalāmeṉṟum annuṭpamāṉa curaṅkaḷē intiya rākaṅkaḷiṉ iṉimaiyuṭaiyaṉaveṉṟum collukiṟār. innuṭpamāṉa curamē intiya caṅkītattai alaṅkarittuk koṇṭirukkiṟateṉṟu aṟintālum ilakuvāyc collikkāṭṭavum iṉṉiṉṉa aḷavil varukiṟateṉṟu eḻutikkāṭṭavum aṟiyātirukkiṟārkaḷ. mēlum intiyāvil vaḻaṅki varum vāttiyak karuvikaḷ 2,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē uṇṭākkap paṭṭavaiyeṉṟum oḷaṭava, cāṭava, campūraṇam eṉṉum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷil pāṭum oru rākam maṟṟavarkaḷ aṟintukoḷḷak kūṭāta avvaḷavu tērcci peṟṟavaiyeṉṟum collukiṟār. mēṟṟicai caṅkītattiṟkum kīḻtticai caṅkītattiṟkumuḷḷa vittiyācattaiyum taṉittuppāṭum intiya caṅkītattiṉ mēṉmaiyaiyum atu viruttiyaṭaiyāmal pōṉataṟkuk kāraṇattaiyum Capt. Daypiṉvarumāṟu collukiṟār.
Vide Hindhustani Sangita Paddhati. P. 329, 330.
“The wide divergence of thaste in the matter of music between European and Asiatic nations has doubtless arisen from the fact that while the western nations gradually discarded the employment of mode, and clothed the melody with harmony, the Eastern nations in this respect made little or no progress; and now, in India, the employment of authentic modes and melody types (or ragas) is still jealously adhered to.
Speaking of this, Capt. Willard remarks “To expect an endless variety in the melody of Hindustan would be an injudicous hope as their authentic melody is limited to a certain number, said to have been composed by professors universally acknowledged to have possessed not only real merit but also the original genius of composition, beyond the precinets of whose authority it would be criminal to trespass. What the more reputed of the moderns have done is that they have adopted them to their own purposes, and found others by the combination of two or more of them. Thus far they are licensed, but they dare not proceed a step further. Whatever merit an entire modern composition might possess, should it have no resemblance to the established melody of the country, it would be looked upon as spurious. It is implicitly believed that it is impossible to add to the number of these one single melody of equal merit. So tenacious are the natives of Hindustan of the ancient practices.”
The continued employment of mode combined with the almost entire absence of harmony, has prevented Indian music from reaching any higher pitch of development such as has been attained elsewhere. It stands to reason also that this is the chief cause of the monotony which causes Indian music to be little appreciated by, if not repellent to, European ears.
Since the early periods of India history, music would seem to have been cultivated more as a science than an art. More attention seems to have been paid to elaborate and tedious artistic skill than to simple and natural melody. Hence arose technical rules that marred the pristine sweetness of melody, the very life of all real music. To a great extent this must be attributed to the art falling into the hands of illiterate “Virtuosi.” Their influence which caused music to suffer both in purity of style and simplicity is being felt less and less. The great aim of music - “Rakti” or the power of affecting the heart-now asserts itself more and more, and is slowly but surely bringing about a return to the early type of sweet, simple melody.”
“airōppiya jātiyārukkum āciyāviluḷḷa jātiyārukkum caṅkīta viṣayattil irukkum vitti yācattiṟkuk kāraṇameṉṉa veṉṟāl, mēṟṟicai jātiyār varavara ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇa viṣayamāy ēṟpaṭum irākaṅkaḷai viṭṭuviṭṭu ēka kālattil pala aṉucuraṅkaḷai amaittup pāṭuvataiyē vicēṣamāka virutti ceytārkaḷ. kīḻtticaiyār itu viṣayattil yātoru muyaṟciyum ceyyavillai. taṟkālattilum intiyāvil (authentic modes) ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷaiyum irākap pirastāraṅkaḷaiyumviṭa caṅkītam vēṟillai eṉṟa koḷkai mēmpāṭuṭaiyatāyirukkiṟatu.
itu viṣayattaippaṟṟi Captain Willard eṉpavar colluvatu yāteṉil, intu tēcattiluḷḷōr upayōkikkum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷ (authentic modes) coṟpamāṉavaiyātalāl, intiyaruṭaiya irākavittiyācaṅkaḷ kaṇakkilaṭaṅkātavaiyāyirukkum eṉṟu eṇṇuvatu picaku. appaṭi eṇṇuvatum niyāya virōtamāyirukkum. inta ārōkaṇaṅkaḷ (authentic modes) caṅkītattil vitvat tiṟamaiyaiyum uruvākkum tiṟamaiyaiyum uṭaittāyirunta pala vitvāṉkaḷāl amaikkappaṭṭavaiyeṉṟu yāvarum oppukkoḷvatāl,avarkaḷuṭaiya varaiyaik kaṭantu nām celvatu kuṟṟamāyirukkum. taṟkāla vitvāṉkaḷ anta ārōkaṇaṅkaḷait tāṅkaḷum aṅkīkarittu upayōkattukkuk koṇṭu vantatumallāmal avaikaḷil iraṇṭoṉṟai oṉṟu cērttatāl putitāṉa cilavaṟṟaik kaṇṭupiṭittu mirukkiṟārkaḷ. ivvaḷavu tūram ivarkaḷ ceyyalāmē yoḻiya itaṟku mēl nūtaṉamāy oṉṟaiyum avarkaḷ ceyya atikāramēyillai. taṟkālam uṇṭu paṇṇappaṭṭa caṅkītamāṉatu evvaḷavu matippai uṭaittāyiruntapōtilum, tēcattil paramparaiyāy vaḻaṅkappaṭum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷukku ottirāviṭṭāl kuṟṟameṉṟu koḷḷappaṭum.  ippōtu irukkiṟa rākaṅkaḷōṭu putitāy ētāvatu oṉṟai yāvatu cērppatu kūṭāta kāriyam eṉṟu intu vitvāṉkaḷ niccayamāy nampukiṟārkaḷ. paḻamaiyai viṭāppiṭiyāyp piṭittal eṉkiṟa vaḻakkam ivarkaḷukkuḷ iruppatāl ippaṭi niṉaikkiṟārkaḷ. taṉi curaṅkaḷālāṉa irākaṅkaḷaiyē virutti ceytu pala aṉucuraṅkaḷai ēka kālattil cērttuc colvataik (Harmony) kaṭṭōṭē viṭṭuviṭṭatutāṉ intiya caṅkītamāṉatu maṟṟa tēca caṅkītam viruttiyaṭaintatupōla aṭaiyātataṟkuk kāraṇam. airōppiyaruṭaiya kātukku intiya saṅkītam iṉimaiyāyirāmal orē rītiyuṭaiyatāyk kāṇappaṭuvataṟkum ituvē kāraṇam.
intutēca carittirattiṉ ārampakāla mutal intiya caṅkītamāṉatu cāstira campantamāṉa viṣayattil tērcciyai aṭaintiruntatēyoḻiya pāṭutal, vāttiyaṅkaḷil vācittal mutaliya viṣayaṅkaḷil atika virutti yaṭaintiruntatākat teriyavillai. irākaṅkaḷaic cuttamāy yāvarukkum viḷaṅkakkūṭiya muṟaiyil colvataip periyatāy niṉaikkāmal irākaṅkaḷaik kaṣṭamāy ākkik kāṇpippatē vittaiyeṉa niṉaittārkaḷ. ākaiyāltāṉ putitu putitāṉa muṟaikaḷ tōṉṟi ātiyil irākaṅkaḷukku irunta aḻakaik keṭuttuviṭṭaṉa. caṅkītattiṉ aḻaku yāvarukkum eḷitil viḷaṅkum atiṉ lēcāṉa muṟaiyaṉṟō? ippaṭic caṅkītam keṭṭuppōṉataṟku mukkiyak kāraṇam yāteṉṟāl, paṭippaṟiyāta pāṭakar, vāttiyakkārar mutaliyavarkaḷ kaiyil caṅkītam akappaṭṭuk koṇṭatutāṉ. āṉāl caṅkītamāṉatu ippaṭic cuttamiṉṟik kalappāvatiṉālum lēcāṉa muṟaikaḷai viṭṭu viṭuvatiṉālum keṭṭup pōvataṟkuk kāraṇamāyiruntavarkaḷ ippōtu varavara oḻintupōkiṟārkaḷ. ākaiyāl ippōtu caṅkītamāṉatu muṉṉirunta ātit taṉinilaimaikkut tirumpi varukiṟatu eṉpataṟkuc cantēkamillai. ellāc caṅkītattiṉuṭaiya mukkiya amcam eṉṉaveṉṟāl, kallaiyum karaiyappaṇṇumpaṭiyāṉa avvaḷavu iṉimaiyaik koṭuppatutāṉ. taṟkāla caṅkītam ivvaḻiyil tirumpukiṟatu eṉkiṟataṟkum pōtumāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ uṇṭu.”
itil mēṟṟicaiyār oru ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇattil ēṟpaṭum rākam oṉṟai ataṟkuriya aḻakōṭu pāṭuvatai viṭṭuviṭṭu ṣaṭjamam, kāntāram, pañcamam, mēlṣaṭjamam mutaliya aṉucuraṅkaḷai vaittuk koṇṭu nālu pākamākap paṭikkum aḻakaiyē virutti ceytārkaḷeṉṟum, intiyarkaḷ appaṭic ceyyāmal rākattaiyē paṭippatu mēleṉṟu niṉaittārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟār. ivvuṇmaiyai nāmum aṅkīkarikka vēṇṭiyatē. pūrvattil iraṇṭu mūṉṟu nālu curaṅkaḷōṭu vētattaik kāṉam ceyta kālattil, cilar ṣaṭjamattilum cilar mēl ṣaṭjamattilum tuvakkip pāṭuvatu vaḻakkam. ivvaḻakkam vētapārāyaṇam paṇṇum ovvoru kūṭṭattārukkum nāḷatuvarai paḻakkamāyirukkiṟateṉṟum ituvē palar cērntu pāṭum muṟaiyāyiruntateṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. ivvaḻakkattiṉiṉṟē maṟṟa tēcattār cērntu pāṭum muṟaiyai viruttikkuk koṇṭu vantārkaḷeṉṟum, āṉāl immuṟai mikap pūrvamāy intiyāvilēyē vaḻaṅki vantateṉṟum ovvoru intiyaṉum perumaip pārāṭṭakkūṭiyatākavumirukkiṟaJ. uṟcava kālaṅkaḷil svāmi veḷippuṟappaṭumpoḻutu svāmiyiṉ piṉṉāl vētakāṉam paṇṇuvatai nām kavaṉittāl ivvuṇmai viḷaṅkum. āṉāl sapta curaṅkaḷu muṇṭāki avaikaḷiṉ pirastārapētattiṉāl anēka rākaṅkaḷ uṇṭāṉatai aṟinta piṉ, oṉṟu cērntu pāṭum vaḻakkattil taṉittup pāṭum aḻaku uṇṭākamāṭṭāteṉṟu aṟintārkaḷ. ṣaṭjamattil ārampittuc caṅkarāparaṇa rākañcollum oruvarukku, kāntārattil ārampittuppāṭuvatu aṉumattōṭi rākamākavum, pañcamattil ārampittup pāṭum maṟṟavarukku harikāmpōti rākamākavum varum. āka mūṉṟu rākaṅkaḷaiyum oṉṟu cērppatiṉāl kātukku iṉimaiyaiyum maṉatukku oṭukkattaiyum tarāJ. āṉāl muṭiyum iṭaṅkaḷil allatu cila curaṅkaḷ oṉṟu cērumiṭattil caṟṟu iṉimaiyāyirukkum. mey maṟanta uṉṉata nilaikku aṉucuraṅkaḷ cērnta caṅkītam utaviyāyirukkamāṭṭāJ. āṉāl maṉataip palavaḻippaṭuttic carīrattai uṟcākappaṭutti vaikkum. iv vuṇmaiyai aṟintē taṉittup pāṭum muṟaiyai intiyāvil taṉittut tapacu ceytu koṇṭirunta periyōr virutti ceytu koṇṭu vantārkaḷeṉṟu niccayamāyc collalām. intiyāvaip pōlotta uṣṇa piratēcattiluḷḷōr karuvi, karaṇaṅkaḷ oṭuṅki niṟka nīṇṭa jīvaṉaiyum virintu niṟkak kuṟukiya āyuḷaiyum uṭaiyavarāvārkaḷ. āṉāl airōppāvaip pōṉṟa kuḷirnta piratēcaṅkaḷiluḷḷōr karuvi, karaṇaṅkaḷ oṭuṅki niṟkil kuḷiriṉāl viṟaittu āyuḷ kuṟukip pōvārkaḷ. taṅkaḷai uṣṇappaṭuttuvataṟku vēṇṭiya ākāramum, uṭaiyum aṉalum, āṭṭamum avarkaḷukku mikavum avaciyamē. itaṟkēṟpa uṣṇap piratēcattil kuḷirnta nīrūṟṟukkaḷum cītaḷap piratēcattil ven nīrūṟṟukkaḷum amaintiruppatu iṅkuk kavaṉikkattakkaJ. ivviyaṟkaiyai aṉucarittē ellōrum pāṭavum āṭavum kūṭiyavitamāka kuḷirnta tēcattiṉ caṅkītam amaintirukkiṟaJ. intiyāvil pakirmukappaṭṭa maṉatai oruvaḻippaṭuttit tiyāṉa nilaikkuk koṇṭu varakkūṭiya vitamākavum, neṭunēram camāti kūṭum vitamāy rākavistāramuṭaiyatākavum mikavum ciṟanta amaippuṭaiyatākavum aḻakuṭaiyatākavum ceyyappaṭṭirukkiṟaJ. āṭavum pāṭavum virumpuvōṉ mēṟṟicaic caṅkītattil piriyappaṭuvāṉ. amaintu maṉamaṭaṅki tiyāṉa nilaiyil niṟka virumpuvōṉ teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ oru rākattai āṟumātamāṉālum toṭarntu pāṭikkoṇṭum kēṭṭuk koṇṭumiruppāṉ. itu iyaṟkai amaippē. kuḷir kālamākiya mārikkālattil kāytta kāykaḷ tittippillāmalum paḻukkāmalum neṭunāḷ marattil niṟpataiyum, puṣpaṅkaḷ vācaṉaiyiṉṟic cilanāḷ utirāmaliruppataiyum nām aṟivōm. āṉāl uṣṇa kālattil veku cīkkirattil kāykaḷ atika maturamuṭaiyavaiyāki viṭuvataiyum puṣpaṅkaḷ atika vācaṉaiyuṭaiyaṉavāy cīkkiram utirntu viṭuvataiyum nām kāṇalām. atikak kuḷircciyāṉa tōppukaḷuḷḷa āṟṟōraṅkaḷilum malaikaḷiṉ kukaikaḷilum tapacu ceytu koṇṭirunta periyōr taṅkaḷ tapacukkaṉukūlamāka vīṇai eṉṉum ciṟanta vāttiyattai vaittukkoṇṭu oru rākattai anēka kālam appiyācittu ālāpaṉai ceytu taṉittup pāṭum vaḻakkattai virutti ceytu vantirukkiṟārkaḷ eṉṟu nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. mālaikkālattil utayamum utayakālattil mālaiyum iruppatupōl uṇarakkāṭṭum rākattiṉ aḻaku, aṉucuraṅkaḷaic cērttup pāṭuvatiṉāl uṇṭākamāṭṭāteṉpatu tiṇṇam. ākaiyiṉāl ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇattil oḷaṭava, cāṭava, campūraṇam eṉṉum curappaṭi vakkira varjiya kālakkiramam aṟintu oru rākattaippāṭi ataṉ iṉimai terintu koṇṭavarkaḷ ataiyē virumpuvārkaḷ eṉpatu niccayam.
paḻamaiyai viṭāppiṭiyāy piṭikkiṟa vaḻakkam intukkaḷuk kuḷḷiruppatāl putitāka oru rākam uṇṭākkuvatu kūṭāta kāriyameṉṟu caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ niccayamāy nampukiṟārkaḷ eṉkiṟār. ivviṣayattil ivar apippirāyattai muṟṟilum oppukkoḷḷukiṟōm. pūrvattil intiyā vilirunta periyōr ovvoru rākattaiyum vistāram ceyvataṟku vēṇṭiya kirāmattai kītamākac ceytu vaittārkaḷ. akkītamē taṟkālattiluḷḷa vitvāṉkaḷukku rākattai vistāram paṇṇuvatil ūṉṟu kōl pōl utavukiṟaJ. ippaṭippaṭṭa kītam ceyyakkūṭiya vitimuṟaikaḷ aḻintupōyiṉa. ākaiyiṉāl muṉṉōr ceytu vaitta kītaṅkaḷ ettaṉaiyō attaṉai rākaṅkaḷ māttirantāṉ ippōtu pāṭappaṭṭu varukiṉṟaṉa. putitāka oru ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇattil oru rākam oruvar pāṭuvārāṉāl, atil anēka tappitaṅkaḷ ēṟpaṭṭu itaip pāṭāmal viṭṭuviṭuvatē nallateṉṟu avarukkē tōṉṟum. tiṟavu kōlillāta pūṭṭait tiṟakkavum piṉ pūṭṭavum muṭiyātirukkiṟatu eppaṭiyō appaṭiyē intiya caṅkītattiṉ nilaiyumirukkiṟaJ. itu tavira “cāstira campantamākat tērcci yaṭaintiruntārkaḷēyoḻiya, pāṭuvatil avvaḷavu tērcciyaṭaiyavillai”  eṉṟu collukiṟār. aṉupōkam vēṟu col vēṟāyirunta cila caṅkīta cāstiraṅkaḷiṉāl ippaṭic colla nēriṭukiṟatē yoḻiya cāstirattiṉ marmam teriyumāṉāl ivvitam niṉaikka māṭṭōm. mēlum avar colliyatu pōlavē, iḻivāṉa toḻil uḷḷavarkaḷālum aṟiyāmai yuḷḷavarkaḷālum caṅkītam taṉ matippai iḻantateṉṟu nāmum collukiṟōm. ātmalāpam peṟa uṇṭāṉa kītam artta nimittam viṟkappaṭuvatai aṟinta periyōr aruvaruppatu niyāyantāṉē.
kirīṭātipatikaḷālum avatāra mūrttikaḷālum riṣikaḷālum āṭik koṇṭāṭappaṭṭu vanta caṅkītam, kaḻaikkūttāṭikaḷālum kūttāṭukiṟavar kaḷālum naṭaṉañceypavarkaḷālum arai kuṟaiyāy appiyācikkappaṭṭum, caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷeṉṟu taṅkaḷaik kāṭṭik koḷḷukiṟavarkaḷāl taṟ kālattil perumpālum paṭikkappaṭṭum varuvatē caṅkītam kuṟaivāṉa nilaikku vantataṟku kāraṇameṉṟu colla iṭantarukiṟaJ. intiya caṅkītattiṟku mukkiyamāṉa mārkkaviti tavaṟi, perumpālum tēcikamāṉataṟkuk kāraṇam intiya tēcattup pirapukkaḷiṉ kavaṉakkuṟaivē yoḻiya vēṟalla. intiyāvil caṅkītam mika uyarvākak koṇṭāṭappaṭṭa teṉṟum vāymoḻiyāl paramparaiyāyp pāṭappaṭum kītaṅkaḷai nām ātāramākak koḷḷa vēṇṭumeṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil terikiṟatu.
Doctor Coomarasawmy’s Foreword to “The Study of Indian Music” by E. Clements.
“Long anterior to this, however, music was a most highly cultivated-perhaps the most highly cultivated of India arts, and to the present day it has remained the most continuously vital and most universally appreciated art of India.”
“kāḷitācaṉ paratar kālattukku muṉṉēyē caṅkītamāṉatu ellā intiya cāstira vittaikaḷilum atikamāyp payilappaṭṭu vantatu eṉṟum ātikālamutal taṟkālam varai toṉṟutoṭṭu uyiruḷḷatāyum, intiyāvil ellā vittaikaḷilum cirēṣṭamāṉatāyum koṇṭāṭap paṭṭateṉṟum teriya varukiṟatu.”
“It is far better that the method of oral transmission should be maintained.”
“vāymoḻiyāl paramparaiyāy varum muṟaiyē vicēṣamākak koḷḷappaṭa vēṇṭum.”
vāymoḻiyāy paramparaiyāy varum muṟaiyaiyē vicēṣamāyk koḷḷa vēṇṭum. ivvārttaiyai nām naṉṟāy iṅkē kavaṉikka vēṇṭum. intiyāviṉ mukkiyamāṉa vaḻakkam oṉṟu itaṉāl aṟivōm. intiyāviṉ arumaiyāṉa anēka kalaikaḷum toḻilkaḷum takutiyuḷḷa oruvaṉukkuk karṇaparamparaiyāy atāvatu taṉ antiya kālattil veku rakaciyamāy kātil collappaṭṭu vantatāka aṟivōm. cila mantiraṅkaḷiṉ rakaciyaṅkaḷum vētāntattiṉ rakaciyamum vātattiṉ rakaciyamum muppūviṉ muṟaiyum pōṉṟa cila arumaiyāṉa vittaikaḷ oṉṟu allatu iraṇṭu vārttaikaḷāl pūrttipeṟakkūṭiyavaiyāyiruntaṉa. appaṭippaṭṭa rakaciya moḻi allatu kurumoḻiyiṉṟi eḻutappaṭṭa pustakaṅkaḷ ērāḷamā yiruntum oru pirayōjaṉattaiyum tarāJ. itōṭu cila ariya vittaikaḷ toṭṭukkāṭṭa atāvatu ceytu kāṭṭa vēṇṭiyavaikaḷākavum iruntaṉa. icceymuṟai toṭṭukkāṭṭāta varaiyum viḷaṅkuvatu kaṭiṉamākum. vaittiyam vātam mutaliya toḻilkaḷil varum iṭaipākam pañca pūta iṉapākamum vāṉa cāstiram yōka cāstiram mutaliyavaikaḷil varum kaṇitamuṟaikaḷum vētānta cāstirattil varum pañcīkaraṇa tattuvac ceyalkaḷum mukkuṇa tattuvac ceyalkaḷum pōṉṟa anēka rakaciyam collappaṭāmal cāstiraṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu nām aṟivōm. “ellārkaṇ muṉṉiṟkum eṭutturaikkum kuru aruḷillāmaṟ pōṉāl collālum vārātu” eṉṟa vākkiyattiṉpaṭi mika virivāṉa nūlkaḷukkum periyōr toṭṭukkāṭṭa vēṇṭiya cila rakaciyaṅkaḷ vaitteḻutiyirukkiṟārkaḷ. akkurumoḻiyillāmal pōṉāl cāvi yillāta oruvaṉ pūṭṭiya vīṭṭai eppaṭic cuṟṟic cuṟṟi mayaṅkuvāṉō appaṭiyē kurumoḻiyillāta cāstiraṅkaḷum viḷaṅkāmal aṉarttam viḷaivikkum. oru neruppukkucciyiṉ veḷiccam eppaṭi iruṭṭiṉ antakārattai nīkki uḷḷataikkāṭṭukiṟatō appaṭippōl periyōrkaḷ collum oru vārttaiyiṉ piṉ vācikkum oru cāstiram mikat teḷivāka arttamākiṟaJ. ippaṭiyē teṉṉintiya caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ curuti muṟaiyilum oru rakaciyam maṟaikkappaṭṭirukkiṟa teṉpatai itaṉpiṉ pārppōm.
ic curuti muṟaikkuriya tiṟavukōl (rakaciyam) teriyumāṉāl teṉṉintiya caṅkītam māttiram cāstira yuktamuṭaiya teṉṟum maṟṟavai kuṟaivuḷḷavaiyeṉṟum nām ilakuvāy aṟintu koḷvōm. teṉṉintiyāvil pāṭappaṭṭu varum rākaṅkaḷum avaikaḷukkuriya curaṅkaḷum aṇuppiramāṇamum tavaṟāmal paramparaiyāy nāḷatu varaiyum kōyil ūḻiyakkārarkaḷāl pēṇappaṭṭu vantirukkiṟateṉṟum atuvē karnāṭaka caṅkītam cuttamāyirukkiṟataṟkuk kāraṇam eṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. ikkarnāṭaka muṟaiyiṉ rakaciyam terintu koḷḷāmai yiṉālēyē curutiyaip paṟṟip palar palavāṟāka pustakaṅkaḷ eḻutavum vātañ ceyyavum nēriṭṭatu.
mēl kāṟṟiṉāl kiḻakkē pōṉa paṭcikaḷum carukukaḷum kīḻ kāṟṟiṉāl maṟupaṭi mēṟkē pōvatupōla maṟṟavar kītamum kālatarumat tiṟkut takuntapaṭi appaṭiyum ippaṭiyum pala māṟutalkaḷaiyaṭaintālum tamiḻnāṭṭiṉ caṅkītam oṉṟu māttiram māṟāmal niṟkiṟateṉṟu aṟivōm. tamiḻ nāṭṭil vaḻaṅki varum caṅkītattiṟkuriya pūrva nūlkaḷ muṟṟilum aḻintu pōṉālum paramparaiyāy vaḻaṅki varum pāṭamuṟaikaḷ caṅkītattiṉ rakaciyam yāvum aṟintu koḷvataṟkup pōtumāṉavai yeṉṟum ammuṟaiyaik koṇṭu caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ mukkiyamāṉa tattuvaṅkaḷaik kaṇṭupiṭikkavum atupōlavē maṟṟoṉṟu nūtaṉamāy uṇṭākkavum kūṭiya vitamāyirukkiṟateṉṟu nām niccayam colluvōm. rākaṅkaḷaip paṟṟi eḻutik koṇṭirukkum iraṇṭām puttakattil itaṉ viparam yāvum teḷivākak kāṇalām. oru collāl virinta pala col maṟainta oru collai viḷakki niṟpatu pōla caṅkītattil virinta pala uruppaṭikaḷ collāmal viṭṭa curuti rakaciyattai viḷakkik kāṭṭik koṇṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. karṇa paramparaiyāy vaḻaṅki varum karnāṭaka rākaṅkaḷiṉ aḻakē avvirākaṅkaḷiṉ rakaciyattaiyum kāṭṭik koṇṭu niṟkiṟateṉṟu itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. erikiṟa viḷakkai viṭṭil pūcci viḻuntu keṭuttut tāṉum keṭṭatupōlākāmal putitāṉa tēcika rākaṅkaḷaik kēṭṭu cila avāntara curaṅkaḷai karnāṭaka caṅkītattil kalantu vaḻaṅkum keṭutalkaḷai nīkkik karnāṭaka muṟaippaṭi taṅkaḷ kāṉattaik kāppāṟṟik koḷḷumpaṭi karnāṭaka caṅkītam payilum kaṉavāṉkaḷ yāvaraiyum mikavum kēṭṭuk koḷḷukiṟēṉ.
piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil intiya caṅkītattiṉ iṉimaiyai maṟṟavarkaḷ aṟintu koḷḷātataṟkuk kāraṇam kūṟukiṟār.
The Indian empire by W.W. Hunter. P. III.
“Melodies which the Indian composer pronounces to be the perfection of harmony, and which have for ages touched the hearts and fired the imagination of Indian audiences, are condemned as discord by the European critic. The Hindu ear has been trained to recognise modifications of sound which the European ear refuses to take pleasure in. Our ears on the other hand, have been taught to expect harmonic combinations of its own. The Indian musician declines altogether to be judged by the few simple Hindu airs which the English ear can appreciate.”
“irākaṅkaḷil atika iṉimaiyuṭaiyavaiyeṉṟum, cirēṣṭa curap poruttamuḷḷavaiyeṉṟum, nīṇṭa kālaṅkaḷāka intiyaruṭaiya maṉatai urukac ceytu paravacappaṭuttiṉavai eṉṟum yāvarālum oppukkoḷḷap paṭṭavaikaḷ, curapporuttam aṟṟavaiyeṉṟu airōppiyarāl taḷḷiviṭap paṭukiṉṟaṉa. intiyaruṭaiya kātukku iṉimaiyeṉṟeṇṇappaṭum nuṭpamāṉa ciṟu curaṅkaḷ airōppiyaruṭaiya kātukaḷukku veṟuppāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. nammuṭaiya kātukaḷō airōppiyaruṭaiya curaṅkaḷukku māṟupaṭṭa oṟṟumaic curaṅkaḷaiyē kēṭṭu āṉantikkum paḻakkattai yaṭaintirukkiṉṟaṉa. intu caṅkītattiṉ cila ilēcāṉa tuṇṭukaḷai airōppiyar iṉimaiyāyiruntatāka oppuk koḷvārkaḷ. āṉāl intu vitvāṉkaḷ intu caṅkītattiṉ iṉimaikku intac ciṟu tuṇṭukaḷ tāṉ cāṭci eṉṟu oppuk koḷḷat tayārāyillai.”
intiya caṅkītam vekunāḷaikku muṉṉuḷḷateṉṟum ovvoru curamum mummūṉṟu pirivuṭaiya tāyiruntateṉṟum teṉṉintiya caṅkītam vētaṅkaḷai ōtuvataṟku mika aṉukūlamāy irukkiṟateṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, P. ii P. 36 (Kunte)
“It is now positively established by documentary evidence that at least 7,000 years before Christ. India had developed a system of musical notation, that the seven notes were scientifically arranged, each note being divided into three the sharp, flat, and the proper note itself. All the inscriptions as yet discovered, and the Sanskrit literature that has been brought to light, place this statement beyond doubt. This fact has a scientific side. Though all the world over, notes, the elements of music, are seven only, yet the great variety of modes and melodies differ in European and Indian music, which is either ancient or modern, Southern or Northern. The Southern or the Dravidian system is more Vedic than the Northern or Hindustani Dhanga. There is what is called a constant mode in Maharastra. This is the remnant of the system of singing vedic psalms. It consists of opening modulation, soft, steady and slow in its progress. this is followed by notes the pitch of which is high, the modulation is strong, varied and rapid in its flow. This is followed by a combination of both leading to agreeable cadences. In a treatise on music, which is at any rate as ancient as the third century before Christ, a connection between physiological condition of human blood in the course of a day, and the changes of temper which these conditions necessitate are explained.”
“kuṟaintapakṣam kiṟistu piṟakka 7,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ intiyāvil caṅkīta curaṅkaḷ eḻutum muṟai uṇṭāyiruntateṉṟum capta curaṅkaḷum cāstira vitippaṭi oḻuṅkāyk kaṇṭupiṭikkap paṭṭiruntaṉa eṉṟum, ovvoru curamum mūṉṟu pirivuḷḷatāyiruntatu. atāvatu curamum curattukkuk koñcam kūṭiya curutiyum curattukkuk koñcam kuṟainta curutiyumāka mūṉṟu eṉṟum eḻuttu ātāraṅkaḷ mūlamāy nirūpikkappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. ituvaraiyum kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭirukkiṟa cila eḻuttukaḷum, kiranta nūlkaḷum itai oṭṭiyē stiramāyp pēcukiṉṟaṉa. intak koḷkaiyāṉatu cāstira campantamuḷḷatāyum irukkiṟaJ. ulakameṅkum kāṉattukku ātāramākiya curaṅkaḷ ēḻu eṉṟa cattiyam vaḻaṅki vanta pōtilum, intiya caṅkītattilum airōppiya caṅkītattilum vaḻaṅkappaṭum ārōkaṇaṅkaḷum irākaṅkaḷum anēkaṅkaḷāyum oṉṟukkoṉṟu aṉanta vittiyācamuḷḷavai kaḷāyum irukkiṉṟaṉa. itu viṣayattil, ātimuṟaikaḷō, taṟkāla muṟaikaḷō, vaṭatēcamuṟaikaḷō, teṉtēcamuṟaikaḷō evaiyāṉālum oṉṟukkoṉṟu vittiyācaṅkaḷ anēkamuḷḷavaikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉa. teṉtēca allatu tirāviṭa caṅkītamāṉatu, vaṭatēca intustāṉiṭaṅkāvaiviṭa vētaṅkaḷai ōtuvataṟku atika pirayōcaṉamuḷḷatāyirukkiṟatākat terikiṟatu. makārāṣṭirattil māṟāta ārōkaṇam (constant mode) eṉṟa ōr muṟaiyuṇṭu. vētattiluḷḷa caṅkītaṅkaḷai ōtum muṟaiyiṉiṉṟu mīnta ōr muṟaiyām iJ. atu ārampattil metuvāṉa ēṟṟat tāḻcciyāṉa pētaṅkaḷāy ārampittu varavara kampīrittuppōvatāṉa ōrvita kōṣṭikāṉam. piṟaku varavara uyarnta curaṅkaḷukkup pōy atituritamāka vittiyācam vittiyacamāṉa cura pētaṅkaḷuḷḷa peruṅkāṉamāki viṭukiṟaJ. ataṟkup piṉ iraṇṭuvita kāṉaṅkaḷum oṉṟu cērntu oru aṭi kāṉa amaitalāṉa muṭivukku varukiṟatu. kiṟistuvukku muṉ mūṉṟāvatu nūṟṟāṇṭil eḻutappaṭṭa ōr caṅkīta nūlāṉatu ōr nāḷiṉ pala nimiṣaṅkaḷuḷ maṉita tēkattil irukkum iratta ōṭṭattiṉ nilaikkum itaṉ mūlamāy maṉitakuṇam māṟum nilaikkum campantappaṭuttik kāṇpikkiṟaJ.”
itil nām kavaṉikka vēṇṭiya toṉṟuṇṭu. sapta curaṅkaḷum mummūṉṟu pirivukaḷuṭaiyaṉa vāyiruntaṉaveṉṟum, atāvatu curamum, curattiṟkuk koñcaṅkūṭiya curutiyum, curattiṟkuk koñcam kuṟainta curutiyumāka mūṉṟu eṉṟum collukiṟār. itaik kavaṉippōmēyāṉāl, evvaḷavu kūṭiyirunta teṉṟum evvaḷavu kuṟaintiruntateṉṟum oru kēḷviyuṇṭākum. curutikaḷaip paṟṟiya cantēkam iṉṉum atikappaṭumēyoḻiya oru niccayattiṟkum varātu. curaṅkaḷ ēḻukkum mummūṉṟāka 21 curutikaḷākiṉṟaṉa. 22 eṉṟu coṉṉa curutikaḷukku iru virōtappaṭumē. ippaṭi niccayamillāta vārttaikaḷaic colluvatē curutikaḷaip paṟṟip palapala apippirāyaṅkaḷ uṇṭāvataṟkuk kāraṇam. mēlum teṉtēca allatu tirāviṭa caṅkītamāṉatu vētaṅkaḷai ōtuvataṟku atikap pirayōcaṉa muḷḷatāyirukkiṟatu eṉkiṟār. vaṭaticai cāstira viṟpaṉṉar oruvar teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītam vētam ōtuvataṟku ēṟṟatāyirukkiṟateṉṟu colvatai nām iṅkē kavaṉikka vēṇṭum. teṉṉintiyāvil rāvaṇēsvaraṉ sapta curaṅkaḷaik koṇṭum cāmavētattaik kāṉam paṇṇum muṟaikku muntiṉavaṉāyiruntāṉeṉṟum avaṉ teṉṉintiyāviṉ camīpattiluḷḷa ilaṅkaikkaracaṉeṉṟum nām aṟivōm. sapta curaṅkaḷōṭu cērntu vaḻaṅkiya pala curutikaḷum pūrvamuḷḷa tamiḻ nūlkaḷil collappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. itaik koṇṭu, pūrvamāṉa vētakāṉattiṟkum maṟṟum kāṉattiṟkum teṉṉintiyāvē muntiṉatu eṉṟu colla niyāyamirukkiṟaJ.
2. teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil tēcikak kalappu vanta vitam.
teṉṉintiya caṅkītamāṉatu vaṭatēcattiṟkup palarālum koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭateṉṟum, maṟṟavarkaḷāl atu kaiyāḷappaṭuṅ kālattil avaravarkaḷ pāṣaikkum tēcattukkum takuntapaṭi pala pētaṅkaḷaiyum aṭaintateṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Oriental Music, P. 82 P. 30. Chinnaswami Moodr., M.A.
“Considering the prodigious number of nationalities and the diversity of provincial dialects in existence throughout the length and breadth of the Indian Empire, it should be no matter for astonishment if there be found any number of heterogeneous systems, as well as incongruous classifications in standard works forming the musical literature of the land. The primary distinction is into two classes, Marga (celestial) and Desi (terrestrial); the latter is now broadly divided into Hindustani and Karnata, the former representing the school established by Hanuma, and the latter the much more ancient and authentic system introduced by Narada, the inventor of all arts and sciences. It is clear however that local tastes and methods of training have considerably upset the theories originally propounded.
In the extreme north, there is a system of six Ragas and thirty-six Raginis which are grouped together diferently by different authors; in the west of India the divergencies are still wider, through the origin is traceable to the same source; in the extreme south only thirty-two are recognised as principal Ragas, of which 8 are classed as Purusha and 24 as Stri, while a few more are designatred by other fanciful names. Another classification is into 32 ancient and 42 recent Ragas. All these are manifestly incomplete. Of late the Hindustani element (which has itself much deteriorated owing to foreign admixture) has been ingrafted on the Dravidian modes to an alarming extent, so that it is a matter of no small difficulty to distinguish the purely classical from the adulterated systems. The tendency is at present to demolish all system and to sail clear of all trammels rules and regulations imposed by the ancient framers of the science; but it is evident that this is not the proper method of effecting reform or insuring progress. Each system should be taken up separately by itself, and while its true original and individual character is jealously maintained, it should be divested of all useless encumbrances and incrustations which obstruct or retard improvement.”
kīḻtticai caṅkītattiṉ vicēṣa amcaṅkaḷum ataippaṟṟiya pala apippirāyaṅkaḷum.
“intiya rājyamāṉatu anēka jātiyār niṟaintuḷḷatāyum, mākāṇaṅkaḷ tōṟum vevvēṟu pāṣaikaḷ vaḻaṅkuvatāyum iruppatāl, caṅkīta cāstira viṣayattil palavita vitatiyācaṅkaḷuḷḷa palamuṟaikaḷum caṅkīta cāstirap pustakaṅkaḷai aṭṭavaṇai muṟaiyāy oḻuṅkupaṭutta muyaṉṟa mukkiya nūlkaḷil anēka vittiyācaṅkaḷum ēṟpaṭṭu yāvum kuḻappamāyk kāṇappaṭuvatu avvaḷavu āccariyamāṉa kāriyamalla. ātiyil caṅkītamāṉatu iraṇṭu pirivuḷḷatākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. avai mārkkam allatu tēvakāṉam, tēcikam allatu ivvulaka kāṉam eṉpavaikaḷām. ivaikaḷil tēcikamāṉatu intustāṉi, karnāṭakam eṉṟa iraṇṭu pirivukaḷākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. avaṟṟuḷ muntiṉatu haṉumārāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa muṟaiyeṉṟum, pintiṉatu ellā cāstirattukkum āti kāraṇarākiya nāratarāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa muṟaiyeṉṟum koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. intap pintiṉamuṟai muntiṉataiviṭa pūrvīkameṉṟum, muṟṟilum cariyeṉṟu nampakkūṭiyateṉṟum koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. āṉālum, ātiyil stāpitta muṟaikaḷ, iṭapētattiṉālum collik koṭukkum muṟai pētattiṉālum atika vittiyācamuḷḷavaikaḷāyp pōyiṉa eṉṟu naṉṟāy veḷiyākiṟatu.
vaṭatēcattil, āṟu irākaṅkaḷum 36 rākiṉikaḷum aṭaṅkiya orumuṟai iruppatākavum, avai pala vitavāṉkaḷāl palavitamāy aṭukkic collappaṭuvatāyum terikiṟatu. mēṟkut tēca caṅkītamō itē muṟaiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉa pōtilum, vittiyācaṅkaḷ iṉṉum aṉantam uṭaiyaṉa. teṉṉāṭṭil tāy rākaṅkaḷ 32 tāṉ eṉṟum, avaikaḷil 8 puruṣarākaṅkaḷeṉṟum, 24 stirī rākaṅkaḷeṉṟum, colvatuṭaṉ maṟṟavaikaḷukku anēka vēṭikkaip peyar koṭuttum aḻaikkiṟārkaḷ. iṉṉoru muṟaiyeṉṉaveṉṟāl, pūrvarākaṅkaḷ 32 eṉṟum, taṟkāla rākaṅkaḷ 42 eṉṟum pirippatu. āṉāl ivaiyellām muṭivaṟṟa vakuppukaḷēyeṉṟu pariṣkāramāyt terikiṟatu.
taṟkālattil vēṟṟuc caṅkītattiṉ kalappāl atika vittiyācamaṭaintirukkiṟa intustāṉi caṅkītam tirāviṭa caṅkītattōṭu aparimitamāyk kalantiruppatāṉatu, tirāviṭa caṅkītattukku nēriṭakkūṭiya apāyattaiyum kalappillāta pūrvacaṅkītattukkum kalappuḷḷa taṟkāla caṅkītattukkumuḷḷa vittiyācattaiyum kaṇṭupiṭikka muṭiyāmaikku ētuvāyirukkiṟatu. ivvitak kaṣṭaṅkaḷiruppatāl taṟkālattil yāvarum ceykiṟateṉṉaveṉṟāl, ellā muṟaiyaiyum tavirttu muṟkālattu vitvāṉkaḷāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa oḻuṅkukaḷ ellāvaṟṟaiyum oḻittu viṭuvatē. āṉāl ippaṭic ceyvatu cīrtiruttañ ceytalukkum virutti ceytalukkum aḻakalla. āṉāl ceyya vēṇṭiyatu eṉṉaveṉṟāl, ovvoru muṟaiyaiyum taṉittaṉiyē eṭuttuk koṇṭu, ataṉuṭaiya vicēṣitta āti amcattaiviṭṭu nilaipeyara viṭāmal, ataṉ viruttikku iṭaiyūṟāyirukkakkūṭiya pirayōcaṉamaṟṟa vaḻivakaikaḷ yāvaiyum vilakki, avaikaḷaic cīrtirutta vēṇṭiyatēyām.”
itaik kavaṉikkaiyil mārkkam tēcikam eṉkiṟa pirivukaḷ iraṇṭum caṅkītattiliruntateṉṟum mārkkam eṉpatu tēvakāṉameṉṟum, tēcikam eṉpatu intustāṉi karnāṭakameṉṟa iraṇṭu pirivukaḷuḷḷateṉṟum, muntiṉarākiya intustāṉi aṉumārāl ceyyappaṭṭateṉṟum, pintiṉatākiya karnāṭakam nāratarāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭateṉṟum collukiṟār. ellāc cāstiraṅkaḷukkum ātikāraṇarākiya nāratarāl stāpikkappaṭṭa muṟai cariyāṉateṉṟum, pūrvīkamāṉateṉṟum, iṭapētaṅkaḷiṉāl māṟutal aṭaintateṉṟum collukiṟār. āṉāl mārkkam eṉṟa muṟaiyē riṣikaḷāl uṇṭākkappaṭṭateṉṟum, ataṉaiyē anumār, nāratar, rāvaṇēsparaṉ mutaliyavarkaḷ kāṉam ceytateṉṟum, atuvē cariyāṉa muṟaiyeṉṟum, teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītamākiya karnāṭakam cuttam eṉṟu aḻaikkappaṭuvataṟkut takutiyuḷḷateṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. anumārāl intustāṉi uṇṭāyiṟṟeṉṟu colluvatu muṟṟilum ovvāJ. nāratarāl ceyyappaṭṭa mārkka muṟai vaṭa tēcattil ceṉṟu tēcaṅkaḷtōṟum vevvēṟu curaṅkaḷ kalakkap peṟṟatiṉāl tēcikameṉṟu peyar peṟṟatu. curañāṉamillāta ciṟuvarkaḷum stirīkaḷum piṟarum tēcika muṟaiyai aṉucarikkalāmeṉṟum collappaṭukiṟatu. tēcikam eṉpatu caṅkīta ilakkaṇam tavirntu vaḻaṅkiya vaḻuvamaitiyāṉa oru muṟaiyeṉṟu nām ñāpakattil vaikka vēṇṭum. mēlum teṉṉāṭṭil pūrvakālattilēyē 11,991 paṇkaḷiruntatākac cilappatikārattiṉ paḻaiya uraikārar colliyirukkiṟār. teṉṉāṭṭil piratimattimattōṭu pāṭappaṭum kalyāṇi vaṭa nāṭṭiṟkup pōy kiramam mīṟi piratimattimattōṭu vēṟu iraṇṭu mattimaṅkaḷaiyum appaṭiyē kāntāra niṣātaṅkaḷilum iraṇṭu iraṇṭu curaṅkaḷaiyum cērttuk koṇṭu teṉṉāṭṭukkuvara, teṉṉāṭṭiluḷḷōr atai mikavum aḻakāyirukkiṟateṉṟu paḻakukiṟārkaḷ. ippaṭiyē āṉanta pairavi, kāmpōti, tōṭi mutaliya rākaṅkaḷum varavarak kalappuṟṟatāy mārkka muṟaiyai iḻantu, muṟṟilum tēcikamāki, karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṉ uyarvaik keṭukkum nilaikku vantirukkiṉṟaṉa. ippaṭic curaṅkaḷ kalakkak kalakka, karnāṭaka rākaṅkaḷ yāvum orē rākam pōl tōṉṟumpaṭiyākiviṭum. intustāṉi mutaliya kītaṅkaḷil taṟkālattil nām collum kuṟaikaḷaiyē karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṟkum colla nēriṭum.
karnāṭaka caṅkītam mikavum oḻuṅkuḷḷa mēlāṉa caṅkītameṉṟuma, pautta caṅkītamum makammatiya caṅkītamum tēcikameṉṟu vaḻaṅki vantaṉaveṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.


Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, P. I. P. 8.
“The Margi System, although preserved still in Sanskrit works on Music, owing to want of cultivation, political influence, and other adventitious circumstances has almost become extinct. Desi with its numerous ramifications is the system now obtaining in India. Music is divided into Nibadha and Anibadha, that set in words and that not, the former being Margi and the latter Desi. The Desi System first acquired importance from the Buddhist musicians, and received fuller development from Mussulmans who introduced khyal from the Hindu Dhruvapada system and from that the Tappa. Besides these, there is the southern Indian system, distinct in itself, and constituting an important section of the Indian musical system, termed the Carnataka system.”
“mārkkam’ eṉṉum muṟaiyāṉatu, caṅkīta kiranta nūlkaḷil innāḷ varaikkum kāṇappaṭṭa pōtilum, appiyācam, turaittaṉattāriṉ ātarippu mutaliya viruttikkuriya ētukkaḷ illāmal perumpālum arukip pōyviṭṭatu. āṉāl tēcikamum atil kiḷaitta anēka muṟaikaḷuntāṉ taṟkālam intiyāvil vaḻaṅkappaṭṭu varukiṉṟaṉa. caṅkītamāṉatu nipatam, anipatam eṉṟu iruvakaippaṭum. nipatam cākittiyamuṭaiyaJ. anipatam cākittiyamaṟṟaJ. muntiṉatu mārkkam. pintiṉatu tēcikam. tēcikamuṟaiyāṉatu mutal mutal pautta caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷāl perumaiyaṭaintu piṟaku makamatiyarāl pūraṇam peṟṟatu. makammatiyar intu turuvapata muṟaiyiṉiṉṟu kiyāl eṉṉappaṭṭa muṟaiyaiyum, kiyāl eṉṉum muṟaiyiṉiṉṟu ṭappā eṉṉum muṟaiyaiyum ēṟpaṭuttiṉārkaḷ. inta muṟaikaḷaiyallāmal teṉṉintiya karnāṭaka muṟaiyeṉṟu oṉṟum uṇṭu. itu maṟṟavaika ḷōṭu kalavāmal intiya caṅkīta muṟaikaḷil veku mukkiyamāṉa ōr muṟaiyāy viḷaṅkik koṇṭirukkiṟaJ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil, mārkkam eṉṉum muṟai appiyācippārillāmal nūl aḷavākavē niṉṟuviṭṭateṉṟum, tēcikam palavāṟākak kiḷaittu viruttiyāki viṭṭateṉṟum teriya varukiṟatu. teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaip pūrṇamāyaṟiyāta pauttarumavarkkup piṉ makammatiyarum tēcikattaip paṭittu vantārkaḷ. curañāṉamillāmal pala curaṅkaḷ kalanta tēcikam atikamāy vaḻaṅkum vaṭanāṭṭiluḷḷa oruvar, maṟṟavaikaḷōṭu kalavāta karnāṭaka caṅkītam eṉṉum oru mukkiya muṟaiyirukkiṟateṉṟu colluvatai nām kavaṉikka vēṇṭum. mēlum, avarkaḷ vaḻaṅki varukiṟa turpat, tillāṉā, kiyāl, ṭappā, ṭōmari mutaliya kīta muṟaikaḷ mikac culapamāṉavaiyeṉṟu nām aṟivōm. āṉāl karnāṭakattil vaḻaṅki varum kītam, tāṉavarṇam, caukkavarṇam, kīrttaṉam, pallavi, rākamālikai mutaliyavai, caṅkīta cāstirattukkup poruntumpaṭi ceyyappaṭṭu mikunta tāḷa amaippuṭaṉ tērcciyaṭaintiruppavai eṉṟu nām aṟivōm.
makammatiyarāl viruttikkuk koṇṭu varappaṭṭa intustāṉi eṉṉum tēcikamuṟaiyāṉatu, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉiṉṟē uṇṭāṉa muṟaiyeṉṟum, teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭanāṭṭukkup pōṉateṉṟum, teṉṉintiya caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ allāvuṭīṉ eṉpavarāl ciṟaikaḷāka vaṭanāṭṭukkuk koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭārkaḷeṉṟum, avvāṟu koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭavarkaḷil takṣaṇattaic cērnta caṅkīta vitvāṉ nāyak kōpāl eṉpavar oruvareṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇkiṟōm. itil kaṇṭa nāyak kōpāl eṉpavar cumār 400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ tāḷārṇavam, rākakatampam, pirapantam mutaliya nūlkaḷ ceytavareṉṟu cāraṅka tēvar nūlukku uraiyeḻutiya kallinātar collukiṟāreṉṟu cupparāmatīkṣatar kūṟukiṟār. ivar curutiyaip paṟṟi naṉṟāyt terintavareṉṟu caturtaṇṭi pirakācikaiyeḻutiya veṅkaṭamaki collukiṟār. ivar tīpakam eṉṉum rākam pāṭi oruvarum ēṟṟāmalē oru viḷakkai eriyumpaṭic ceytāreṉṟum collappaṭṭirukkiṟatu.
Universal History of Music, P. 84.
“The Mahomedans as a ruling nation came in contact with the people of India for the first time in the 11th century, and since then a change has been worked into the music system of the country. The Mahomedans did not encourage the theory of the art, but they patronized practical musicians and were themselves instrumental in composing and introducing several styles of songs or devising new forms of musical instruments. It is related by Mahomedan historians of the period that when Dacca was invaded by Allaudin in 1294 and the conquest of the south of India was completed (1310) by his Mogul general Malik Kafer, music was in such a flourishing condition, that all the musicians and their Hindu preceptors were taken with the armies, and settled in the North. It is said that the celebrated Persian poet and musician Amir Khosru came to India during the rule of Allaudin and defeated in a contest the musician of the South, Nayak Gopal, who had come to Delhi with a view to challenge the musicians of the court. Amir Khosru is reported to have given the name of Safar to the Tritantri Vina of the classic days and to have divided the Rags into twelve Mokams which were subsequently subdivided by other Mahomedan musicians into 24 Sobha and 48 Guswas.”
“makammatiyar rājāṅkattiṟkuriya ōr jātiyārāy intukkaḷuṭaṉ mutal mutal kalantatu 11-m nūṟṟāṇṭiltāṉ. atu mutal intiya caṅkītattil ōrvita māṟutal uṇṭāka ārampittaJ. makammatiyar caṅkīta cāstirattai atikamāy apivirttikkuk koṇṭu varāmal, caṅkīta vittiyāp piyācattai ātarittu vantatākat terikiṟatu. avarkaḷ tāṅkaḷē cākittiyam, kavikaḷ ceytatākavum anēka putuvitamāṉa pāṭṭukaḷai uṇṭu paṇṇiṉatākavum, anēka putumātiriyāṉa vāttiyaṅkaḷai uṇṭu paṇṇiṉatākavum terikiṟatu. 1,294-m varuṣattil ṉuyauuya nakaram allāvuṭiṉ eṉpavarāl muṟṟukai pōṭappaṭṭu, 1,310-m varuṣattil teṉṉintiyāvāṉatu avaruṭaiya taḷakarttaṉākiya mālik kāpar eṉpavaṉāl pūraṇamāy jeyikkappaṭṭa pōtu, caṅkītamāṉatu veku tirupti yāṉa nilaimaiyil iruntatākavum, ellāc caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷōṭu avarkaḷukkup pāṭam collik koṭutta intu vitvāṉkaḷaiyum ciṟaipiṭittuk koṇṭupōy vaṭakkē kuṭiyēṟṟiṉatākavum collap paṭukiṟatu. pērpōṉa pāracīkak kaviyum caṅkīta vitvāṉumāṉa amīrkusru eṉpavaṉ allāvuṭiṉ aracāṇṭa camayattil intiyāvukku vantatākavum, camastāṉa vitvāṉkaḷiṉ tiṟamaiyaic cōtikka vēṇṭumeṉṟu ṭillikku vantirunta takṣaṇattaic cērnta nāyak kōpāl eṉpavaṉai jeyittatākavum collappaṭukiṟatu. amīrkusru eṉpavaṉ ātikālattu tiritantiri vīṇaikku Satar eṉṉum pērkoṭuttatākavum avaṉ irākaṅkaḷai 12 mōkaṅkaḷākap pirittatākavum, avaikaḷ piṟpāṭu maṟṟa makammatiyarāl 24 cōpaṅkaḷākavum 48 kusvaṅkaḷākavum pirikkappaṭṭa tākavum collappaṭukiṟatu.”
itaikkoṇṭu vaṭanāṭṭil teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaip pōl uyarvuḷḷa caṅkīta muṟai illaiyeṉṟu kaṇṭa maṟṟavar teṉṉāṭṭi liruntu vitvāṉkaḷaik koṇṭu pōy vaṭanāṭṭil kuṭiyēṟṟi, caṅkītattai virutti ceytārkaḷeṉṟu eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟatu. intiyā viṟkup paṭaiyeṭuttu vanta makā alekcāntarum intiyāviliruntu caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷaiyum maṟṟum cila vitvāṉkaḷaiyum kūṭṭik koṇṭu pōṉāṉ eṉṟu paramparaiyāyc collappaṭukiṟatu.
vaṭa intiyāvil vaḻaṅki vanta intiya caṅkītam makammatiyaruṭaiya kālattil mikavum īṉastitiyaṭaintu intustāṉ eṉṟa peyaruṭaṉ vaḻaṅki vantatu eṉpataip piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil aṟiyalām.
The Music and the Musical Instruments of Southern India, by C.R. Day, P. 5.
“In later years music became a distinct trade, especially under Musalman rulers and passed into the hands of the lower orders and the unlearned; and to this cause operating through a long succession of years, the differences between the Hindustani and Karnatic systems must be in a great measure attributed.”
“piṟkālattil caṅkītamāṉatu vicēṣamāy makammatiya aracāṭciyiṉ kālattil, oru viyāpāram pōla ākiya, kīḻ jātiyāruṭaiya kaiyilum paṭippaṟiyātavarkaḷ kaiyilum pōyc cērntaJ. inta vitamāy anēka āṇṭukaḷ kaḻiyavē anēka māṟutalkaḷ uṇṭāyviṭṭaṉa. inta māṟutalkaḷē anēkamāy karnāṭaka caṅkītattukkum intustāṉ caṅkītattukkumuḷḷa vittiyācattiṟku ātikāraṇam.”
akpar kālattilirunta caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷaiyum avarkaḷ upayōkitta curaṅkaḷiṉ nuṭpattaiyum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil aṟiyalām.
The Indian Empire, by W.W. Hunter, P. 110-111.
“Hindu music, after a period of excessive elaboration sank under the Mahomedans into a state of arrested development. Of the 36 chief musicians in the time of Akbar, only 5 were Hindus. Not content with tones and semitones, the Indian musicians employ a more minute subdivision, together with a number of tonal modifications, which the Western ear neither recognises nor enjoys. Thus they divide the octave into 22 subtones instead of the 12 tones and semitones of the European scale. This is one of several fundamental differences, but it alone suffices to render Indian music barbaric to us; giving it the effect of a Scotch ballad in a minor key, sung intentionally a little out of tune.”
“vekutūram viruttiyaṭaintirunta intiya caṅkītamāṉatu makammatiyar aracāṇṭa kālattil virutti kuṉṟat toṭaṅkiṟṟu. akpar kālattil irunta 36 cirēṣṭa caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷuḷ intiyar aintē aintu pērtāṉ. muḻu curaṅkaḷum arai curaṅkaḷum pōtumeṉṟirāmal intiya caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ, kāl, araikkāl mutaliya nuṭpamāṉa vittiyācamuḷḷa ciṟu curaṅkaḷaiyum upayōkikkiṟārkaḷ. inta aṇuppiramāṇamāṉa curaṅkaḷ airōppiyarukkut terikiṟatumillai, appērppaṭṭa curaṅkaḷ ēṟpaṭum rākaṅkaḷil avarkaḷ piriyappaṭukiṟatumillai. airōppiya caṅkītattil iruppatupōl oru stāyiyai muḻutum araiyumāka 12 curaṅkaḷāyp pirikkāmal, 22 curaṅkaḷākap pirikkiṟārkaḷ. iraṇṭu caṅkītattiṟkumuḷḷa anēka vittiyācaṅkaḷil itu oṉṟu. āṉāl inta oru kāraṇattiṉā lēyē intiya caṅkītam nammuṭaiya kātukaḷukku anākarīkamuḷḷatākat tōṟṟukiṟaJ. atu tāḻnta curattil (āinṭica manala il) vēṇumeṉṟu curam kūṭṭik kuṟaittup pāṭappaṭukiṟa Scotch ballad -ai ottirukkiṟa teṉṟu collalām.”
intiyāvai mikunta nērmaiyuṭaṉ (1556-1605) cumār 50 varuṣaṅkaḷ āṇṭu koṇṭirunta, akpar cakkiravarttiyiṉ capaiyil 36 cirēṣṭa caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷiruntārkaḷ eṉṟum, atil intiya caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ aintē aintu pēr māttiram iruntārkaḷeṉṟum terikiṟatu. tañcāvūr camastāṉattaic cērnta ariyalūril irunta oru jākīrtār kacciyuvareṅka pūpati eṉpavar taṉ kālattil 365 tērnta karnāṭaka caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷai vaittu ātarittār eṉṟum, avarkaḷāl kacciyuvareṅkapūpati eṉṟa muttiraiyuṭaṉ anēka varṇaṅkaḷum kīrttaṉaikaḷum pāṭappaṭṭiruk
kiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum nām aṟikaiyil vaṭatēcattil intiya caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ evvaḷavu coṟpamāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟu kāṇpōm. mēlum, avarkaḷ mika nuṭpamāṉa kāl, araikkāl, mutaliya curaṅkaḷaiyum cērttu upayōkappaṭuttik koṇṭu vantārkaḷeṉṟum aṟikiṟōm. kāl araikkāl curaṅkaḷai intiya caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ upayōkittuk koṇṭu vantārkaḷ eṉṟu coṉṉavar oru stāyiyil 22 curaṅkaḷākap pirikkiṟārkaḷ eṉṟu collukiṟār. itu muṉṉukkup piṉ ovvātirukkiṟatu. ēḻu curaṅkaḷum 22 curaṅkaḷākap pirintatu eṉṟum, avaikaḷ kāl araikkāl eṉṟa aḷavil varavillai yeṉṟum itiṉ piṉ pārppōm.
3. teṉṉintiya caṅkītam vēṟu, vaṭa intiya caṅkītam vēṟu eṉpataip paṟṟi.
itu varaiyum nām pārttavaikaḷaik koṇṭu, teṉṉintiyāvil nāratar akastiyar mutaliyavarkaḷāl stāpikkappaṭṭa teṉṉintiya caṅkītamāṉatu vaṭanāṭṭiṟkuc ceṉṟu cila curaṅkaḷaic cērttukkoṇṭu tēcikamāki intustāṉi caṅkītam eṉṟa peyaruṭaṉ vaṭaintiyāvil vaḻaṅki vantateṉṟu teḷivāyt terikiṟatu. mārkkam eṉṟa tēvakāṉam teṉṉintiyāvilum, intustāṉiyeṉṟa tēcikakāṉam vaṭaintiyāvilum vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟateṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷāl aṟiyalām.
The Music and the Musical Instruments of Southern India, by C.R. Day, P. 12.
“Of the two systems practised in Southern India at the present time, the Hindustani is somewhat akin to that of Northern India and Bengal. It is practised mostly by Mussalman musicians while the Karnatic is confined more to those of the Southern races. The latter which may be called the national music of the South, is far more scientific and refined than the Hindustani and its professors arc, as a rule, men of much better education, a fact that is not without influence upon their music and seems apparent in all their melodies but particularly in the renderings they give of them.”
“taṟkālam teṉṉintiyāvil vaḻaṅkum iraṇṭu muṟaikaḷil intustāṉi caṅkītamāṉatu, vaṭa intiyāvilum paṅkāḷattilum vaḻaṅkum caṅkītattiṟkuc caṟṟu ottirukkiṟaJ. atu vicēṣamāy makamatiya caṅkītakkārarālum karnāṭaka caṅkītamāṉatu teṉṉintiya jātiyārālum muṟaiyē upayōkikkappaṭukiṉṟaJ. teṉṉintiya jātiyāriṉ caṅkīta meṉṟaḻaikkappaṭum karnāṭaka caṅkītamāṉatu intustāṉi caṅkītattaiviṭa atika cāstirōktamāṉatum atika cuttamāṉatu māyiruppatu mallāmal ataippaṭikkum vitvāṉkaḷum intustāṉi caṅkītattaip paṭippavarai viṭak kalviyaṟivil tērntavarkaḷāyirukkiṟārkaḷ eṉpatu perumpālum uṇmaiyē. ippaṭik kalviyil apiviruttiyillai yeṉṟu colluva tāṉatu intustāṉi caṅkītattil vaḻaṅkum irākaṅkaḷaiyum vicēṣamāy avaikaḷai avarkaḷ pirastāram ceytu kāṇpippataiyum kavaṉittāl naṉṟāy viḷaṅkum.”
mēl vacaṉaṅkaḷil teṉṉintiya jātiyāriṉ caṅkīta meṉṟaḻaikkappaṭum karnāṭakamāṉatu intustāṉi caṅkītattaiviṭa cāstirayuktamāṉateṉṟum kalappillāmal cuttamāyirukkiṟateṉṟum kalvi aṟivil tērntavarkaḷāl appiyācikkappaṭṭu varukiṟateṉṟum ciṟanta rākap pirastāramuṭaiya tāyirukkiṟa teṉṟum collukiṟār.
teṉṉintiyāvil teṉpākattil aḻintupōṉa teṉṉintiya kaṇṭattil teṉ maturaiyirunta kālattaiyum atilirunta caṅkap pulavarkaḷaiyum atil caṅkītam mikavum tērcci peṟṟirunta teṉpataiyum nām itaṉ muṉ pārttirukkiṟōm. avarkaḷil caṅkītattaip paḻakiyavarkaḷum vīṇai vācikkat tērntavarkaḷum teyva cannitiyil narttaṉam paṇṇit teyvattait toḻutu koṇṭavarkaḷum yāreṉṟu vicārippōmēyāṉāl avvirājyattaiyāṇṭa muṭimaṉṉarkaḷum avatāra mūrttikaḷum iḷavaracarkaḷum pirapukkaḷumāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟu nām perumai pārāṭṭik koḷvōm. kirīṭātipatikaḷākiya taṅkaḷ aracarkaḷ caṅkītattilum paratattilum mikunta piriyamuḷḷavarkaḷāyirukkiṟārkaḷeṉṟu aṟinta maṟṟavarum teṉṉintiyāviṟku vanta piṟarum caṅkītattaiyum paratattaiyum mikavum appiyācittut tērntavarkaḷāṉārkaḷ. mēlum, rājāṅkattai viṭṭu tapacu ceyyac ceṉṟa rājariṣikaḷum avarkaḷ ciṣyavarkkaṅkaḷāṉa teṉṉāṭṭu antaṇarum taṅkaḷ tapacukku aṉukūlamākac caṅkītattai upayōkappaṭuttik koṇṭu vantārkaḷ. ivvitamāka jaṉaṅkaḷil uyarntavarkaḷāka matikkappaṭum tapacikaḷālum kaṟṟaṟintavarkaḷālum āriyarālum caṅkītam pēṇappaṭṭu mika arumaiyuṭaiyatāka eṇṇappaṭṭatu. ippaṭiyāvarālum arumaiyākak koṇṭāṭappaṭṭa caṅkītam kōvil kaḷilum vaitīka karumaṅkaḷilum kaliyāṇaṅkaḷilum upayōkikkappaṭṭu vantatu.
teṉṉāṭṭil pāṇṭiya rājyam nātaṉaṟṟup pōṉapiṉ caṅkītamum tēṭuvāraṟṟuppōka tamiḻnāṭṭu mūvēntarkaḷiloruvarāṉa cōḻa rājākkaḷāl viruttiyaṭaintu vantatu. tamiḻnāṭukaḷil oṉṟākiya cōḻa nāṭum cōḻa aracarkaḷai iḻantapiṉ vaṭuka rājākkaḷālum avarkaḷukkup piṉ makārāṣṭira rājākkaḷālum iṟṟaikku 60 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ varaiyum āḷappaṭṭu vantatu. atil cōḻa rājākkaḷālum nāyakka rājākkaḷ cilarālum, makārāṣṭira rājākkaḷ cilarālum oruvāṟu caṅkītamum paratamum pēṇappaṭṭu vantaṉa. āti kālantoṭṭut toṭarcciyāyt teṉṉintiyā vilēyē caṅkītam vaḷarntu vantirukkiṟateṉpatai piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
The Music and the Musical Instruments of Southern India, by C.R. Day, P. 5.
“Music has almost without interruption flourished there (in Southern India) from very remote ages.
The higher branches of musical profession were formely confined to either Brahmins (Bhagavatars) or to men of very high caste. Music being of divine origin was regarded as sacred, and it was considered impious for any but men of the caste to wish to acquire any knowledge of its principles. It was and still is called the fifth Veda. Hence the ancient Brahmins of the country would have excommunicated any of their number who would have so far presumed as to betray the sacred writings to any but the elect, whose mouths only were esteemed sufficiently holy to utter words so sacred. Indeed it was the knowledge of which they were possessed that was the chief cause of the reverence and adoration paid to the Brahmins of old and which gave them power and influence they prized so much. It was thus that the ancient musicians sang their own composition.”
“caṅkītamāṉatu teṉṉintiyāvil āti kālantoṭṭu toṭarcciyāy vaḷarntu vantirukkiṟatu.
muṟkālattil caṅkītattai uyarnta toḻilākak koṇṭavarkaḷ pirāmaṇa pākavatarkaḷāyāvatu uyarkulattaic cērntavarkaḷāyāvatu iruntārkaḷ. caṅkītamāṉatu kaṭavuḷiṭamiruntu jaṉittapaṭiyāl atu vaitīka muṟaiyaic cērntatāka eṇṇappaṭṭatu māttiramalla. ataṉ muṟaikaḷai uyarkulattār māttiram paṭikkalām maṟṟavar paṭittāl atu paktikku virōtameṉṟum eṇṇappaṭṭatu. caṅkītamāṉatu āti mutal aintām vētamāyiruntatu māttiramalla, ellārālum appaṭiyē oppukkoḷḷappaṭṭu antap pērāl aḻaikkappaṭukiṟatāyumirukkiṟaJ. ākaiyāl intiyāviṉ āti pirāmaṇarallāta maṟṟavarukku yārāvatu collik koṭukkat tuṇiyumpaṭcattil avarkaḷai jātiyiliruntu vilakkip pōṭṭatu mallāmal, vētaviṣayaṅkaḷai eṭuttuc colvataṟkut taṅkaḷ vāykaḷ māttiramē paricuttamāṉavaiyeṉṟu eṇṇiyum vantārkaḷ. āti pirāmaṇarukkuc celuttappaṭṭa vaṇakkamum mariyātaiyum antastum atikāramum veku mēṉmaiyāka eṇṇivanta avarkaḷ celvākkum intak kāraṇattiṉālēyē. ākaiyāl āti caṅkītavitvāṉkaḷ taṅkaḷuṭaiya conta cāhittiyaṅkaḷaiyē pāṭi vantārkaḷ.”
ivvākkiyaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil caṅkītamāṉatu kaṭavuḷiṭatti liruntuṇṭāṉateṉṟum atai mikunta mēṉmaiyāy ataṉ rakaciyaṅkaḷai maṟṟavar aṟiyāvaṇṇam vaittiruntārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟār.
āriyar teṉṉintiyāviṟku varum muṉṉatākavē caṅkītamum ataic cērntatāṉa paratam, tāḷam mutaliya aṅkaṅkaḷum mika vistāramaṭaintu vīṇai, pullāṅkuḻal, mirutaṅkam mutaliya vāttiyak karuvikaḷuṭaṉ appiyācikkappaṭṭu vantateṉṟum, avaikaḷ kōyilkaḷilum rāja araṇmaṉaikaḷilum mukkiyamāy upayōkappaṭṭu vantateṉṟum itaṉ muṉ pārttirukkiṟōm. āriyar teṉṉāṭṭiṟku vantapiṉ teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaik kaṟṟuk koṇṭu atil tērntavarkaḷāṉārkaḷ. vēta culōkaṅkaḷai eppaṭi nāḷatu varaiyum piṟarukkuc collik koṭukkak kūṭātu eṉṟa eṇṇamuṭaiyavarkaḷāyirukkiṟārkaḷō atupōlavē maṟṟavariṭat tiliruntu kaṟṟukkoṇṭa teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaiyum piṟarukkuc collik koṭukka maṉamaṟṟavar kaḷāṉārkaḷ. aṉṟiyum pūrvamāyc ceyta tamiḻ uruppaṭikaḷai nīkkip putitākat taṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ paramparaikkeṉṟu camaskirutam teluṅkupōṉṟa pāṣaikaḷil uruppaṭikaḷ ceytu avaṟṟaiyē vaḻaṅkiṉārkaḷ. avaikaḷ muṟṟilum pūrvatamiḻ naṭaiyaiyē aṉucarittu irukkiṟatēyoḻiya vēṟillai. itaṉāl kālakkiramattil teṉṉintiyāviṉiṉṟuṇṭāṉa caṅkītam vaṭanāṭṭiṉ camaskiruttiliruntuṇṭāṉatāka eṇṇumpaṭiyāyiṟṟu.
mēlum karnāṭaka caṅkītamum intustāṉ caṅkītamum vevvēṟu eṉpataiyum intustāṉi caṅkītattaiviṭa kaṭavuḷiṭamiruntuṇṭāṉatāka eṇṇappaṭum karnāṭaka caṅkītattil kaṭavuḷukkirukkum tējacum pirakācamum cūḻntukoṇṭikkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum paktikkuriya nūlkaḷ yāvum ic caṅkītattil campantappaṭṭēyirukkiṟatākavum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
The Music and the Musical Instruments of Southern India, by C.R. Day, P. 2,3 and 4.
“Since the Sangita Parijata which is believed to be one of the latest of these Sanskrit works, had been written by Ahobila, two separate schools or systems of music have arisen and are now known by the names of Hindustani and Karnatic. The Karnatic appears to have been elaborated as a distinct system subsequent to the advent of the Aryans to the south of India. The two systems although sprung from the same origin have since undergone independantly considerable changes and are now totally distinct from each other.
Of Hindu music in Southern India, since the fall of the Hindu Empire of Vijayanagar Tanjore has been the only school and from it those of Travancore and other places have doubtless been founded.
Mahomedan music taken as a while, has little t recommend itself even at the present day. The ideans professed by Hindus offer a curious contrast for music from a Hindu standpoint. It is associated with all that is bright and sweet in life; its origin ascribed directly to divine providence causes it to be regarded as surrounded by a halo of sanctity. Almost all the religious literature of the Hindus breathes music.”
“samaskiruta nūlkaḷil coṟpakālattukku muṉtāṉ eḻutappaṭṭatu eṉṟu oppuk koḷḷappaṭṭavaikaḷil oṉṟākiya caṅkīta pārijātam (Sangita Parijatam) akōpilar eṉpavarāl eḻutappaṭṭa kāla mutal iraṇṭu vittiyācamāṉa caṅkīta muṟaikaḷ atāvatu intustāṉi, karnāṭakam eṉpavai uṇṭāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. āriyarkaḷ intiyāviṉ teṉpākattil kuṭiyēṟiṉa kālattiṟkup piṟpāṭu karnāṭaka caṅkītamāṉatu pirattiyēkamāṉa ōr muṟaiyāy ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭatākat terikiṟatu. iraṇṭu muṟaikaḷum orē uṟpattiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉapōtilum taṉittaṉiyē oṉṟaṟkoṉṟu campantamillāmalē pala māṟutalkaḷai yaṭaintu oṉṟaṟkoṉṟu muṟṟum pētamāṉavaikaḷāyk kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
vijayanakara rājyam viḻunta kālamutal teṉṉintiya caṅkīta muṟaikaḷil tañcainakar muṟai oṉṟutāṉ vicēṣamāṉatākat terikiṟatu. in nakariliruntutāṉ tiruvaṉantapurattukkum maṟṟa iṭaṅkaḷukkuc caṅkīta muṟaikaḷ paraviṉa.
makammatiya caṅkītattait tokaiyāka eṭuttukkoḷḷum paṭcattil, taṟkālam yāvarālum piriyappaṭṭu oppukkoḷḷakkūṭiya amcaṅkaḷ atil atikam kiṭaiyātu. āṉāl intu caṅkītattaip pārttālō, intukkaḷuṭaiya caṅkīta amcaṅkaḷukkum yāvarum āccariyappaṭakkūṭiya cila vittiyācaṅkaḷ ēṟpaṭukiṉṟaṉa. intukkaḷuṭaiya caṅkītamāṉatu ētētu makimaiyāyum namatu jīviyattil iṉpamāyum uḷḷatu eṉṟu niṉaip pōmō avaikaḷōṭellām campantappaṭṭēyirukkiṟatu. caṅkītamāṉatu kaṭavuḷiṭamiruntu uṇṭāṉatu eṉkiṟa eṇṇam pūrvamāyirukkiṟapaṭiyāl kaṭavuḷukkirukkum oruvitamāṉa tējacum pirakācamum atai eppōtum cūḻntu niṟkiṟatākak koṇṭāṭappaṭukiṟaJ. intukkaḷuṭaiya paktikkuriya nūlkaḷellām caṅkītavācaṉaiyuḷḷatākavēyirukkiṉṟaṉa.”
ivvākkiyaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil caṟṟēṟakkuṟaiya 300 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta pārijātakkārar, akkālattiṟku cumār 400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta caṅkīta ratṉā karattiṉ curuti muṟai teṉṉintiya kāṉattil vaḻaṅki vanta curutikaḷukku ottu varavillai yeṉṟa apippirāyattaiyuṭaiyavarāy ippōtu vīṇaiyil vaḻaṅkivarum 12 curaṅkaḷai eṭuttuk koṇṭu mītiyāṉa pattu ciṟu curaṅkaḷaiyum taḷḷi aḷavu collukiṟār. avar vākkiyaṅkaḷil mītiyāṉa pattaiyun taḷḷi rāka laṭcaṇam collukiṟēṉ eṉkiṟār. itaikkoṇṭu avar kālattilēyē caṅkīta ratṉākarar curutimuṟai karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṉ curuti muṟaikku ottatallaveṉṟa vivātam iruntatākat terikiṟatu. “tuvāvimcati curutikaḷ” eṉṟa col eppaṭi vantateṉpataip paṟṟi itaṉ piṉ pārppōm. karnāṭaka caṅkītam pārijātakkārarukkup piṟakuṇṭāṉateṉṟu tavaṟutalākac coṉṉavarkaḷiṉ apippirāyattaik koṇṭu Capt. Day ippaṭi eḻutiṉārē yoḻiya maṟṟapaṭiyalla. avarukkut teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ uṇmai terintavarkaḷ viparam colliyiruppārkaḷāṉāl intiya caṅkītattiṉ ātāra niyāyaṅkaḷai veku nuṭpamāka ulakattukkuc colliyiruppār. intiyāviṉ pūrvattaiyum ataṉ kalaikaḷaiyum vicārikka varum mēṟṟicai kaṉavāṉkaḷil anēkar cila cila camayaṅkaḷil mikavum coṟpa aṟivuṭaiyavarkaḷāl vaḻikāṭṭappaṭṭu avviṭattiluḷḷa kāriyaṅkaḷai avarkaḷ mūlamāy aṟintukoṇṭu pōkiṟārkaḷ. oru ciṟiya mūkkaṇām kayiṟṟil kaṭṭuppaṭṭa māṭu akkayiṟṟiṉ mūlamāka naṭattappaṭuvatupōla ituvum kāla iyalpākiṟaJ. itaṉāl vivēkikaḷ cila camayaṅkaḷil tappāṉa apippirāyam koḷvatai aṭikkaṭi nām kāṇkiṟōm. intiya caṅkītattaip paṟṟiya nuṭpam iṉṉateṉṟaṟiyāmal cumār 2,500 varuṣaṅkaḷākat tavaṟutalāṉa apippirāyamuṇṭāki vivātaṅkaḷ naṭantu varukiṟateṉpatai nām aṟivōmāṉāl itaip peritāka niṉaikka māṭṭōm.
āriyarkaḷ intiyāviṉ teṉpākattil kuṭiyēṟiṉa kālattiṟkup piṟpāṭu karnāṭaka caṅkītamāṉatu pirattiyēkamāṉa ōr muṟaiyāka ēṟpaṭuttap paṭṭa teṉpatākat terikiṟateṉkiṟār. itu uṇmaiyē. āriyarkaḷ kaṭaic caṅka kālattilum ataṟkup piṉṉum tamiḻ nāṭṭil vantu tamiḻaikkaṟṟu tamiḻ ilakkaṇa ilakkiyaṅkaḷukku uraiyeḻutavum nūlkaḷ iyaṟṟavum ārampittārkaḷeṉṟum, ataṉāl camaskiruta moḻikaḷ aṅkaṅkē kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum, tamiḻiṉ pūrvīkattai cantēkikkumpaṭiyāṉa vārttaikaḷum kalantirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum, aḻintupōṉa tamiḻ nūlkaḷiṉ cārattai camaskirutattil ceytirukkalāmeṉṟum itaṉmuṉ coṉṉōm. atupōlavē karnāṭaka caṅkītameṉṟa pirattiyēkamāṉa oru muṟaiyum intustāṉi eṉṟu taṟkālattil vaḻaṅkum vaṭanāṭṭiṉ caṅkīta muṟaiyeṉṟu eḻutappaṭṭatāka iṅkē kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. ivviraṇṭum orē uṟpattiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉatākavum appaṭi uṇṭāṉapōtilum taṉittaṉiyē oṉṟaṟkoṉṟu campantamillāta pala māṟutalkaḷaiyum uṭaittāyirukkiṉṟateṉṟum teḷivākac collukiṟār. itaṉāl veku pūrvamāyuḷḷa teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ curuti muṟaiyiṉ rakaciyam teriyāmal tuvāvimcati curutikaḷeṉṟu eḻutiya camaskiruta muṟai yoṉṟum ataṉpiṉ teṉṉintiya kāṉattil pāṇṭittiyamaṭainta vitvāṉkaḷ teṉṉintiya caṅkītattai aṉucarittu eḻutiya pārijātam pōṉṟa camaskiruta nūlkaḷum āka iraṇṭumuṟaiyuṇṭāṉatākac collukiṟār. atil teṉṉintiya caṅkītamuṟai tañcainakar oṉṟiltāṉ vicēṣamāyiruntatākavum ataṉpiṉ maṟṟiṭaṅkaḷukkup pōṉatākavum collukiṟār. cumār 300 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ tañcai nakariliruntu vēṅkaṭamaki eḻutiya “caturtaṇṭippirakācikai”yum ataṉpiṉ ataippōṉṟa pala ciṟu nūlkaḷum camaskirutattil eḻutappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum avaikaḷ karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṉ uṇmaiyaiyē terivippavaikaḷeṉṟum itaṉ muṉ colliyirukkiṟōm. nālu pālaikaḷiliruntuṇṭākum 103 paṇkaḷum 12,000 rākaṅkaḷum  iṉṉaveṉṟu viparañcollum tamiḻ nūlkaḷ aḻintapiṉ avaṟṟiṟku iraṇṭāvatāṉa āyappālaiyiṉ 12 curaṅkaḷaiyum avaikaḷāl uṇṭākum 72 mēḷaṅkaḷaiyum oḻuṅkupaṭutti ivvaḷavāvatu karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṟku eḻuti vaittārkaḷēyeṉṟu mikavum cantōṣappaṭa vēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. āṉāl pūrvamāy vaḻaṅki vanta rākaṅkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷaiyum cila cantēka coṟkaḷaiyum muṟṟilum māṟṟi camaskiruta aṭcaraṅkaḷaik koṇṭu lakkaṅkaḷ kaṇṭupiṭikkum pataṅkaḷaiyum cērttu curaṅkaḷukkum rākaṅkaḷukkum kāraṇap peyariṭṭu jāti vakuttu camaskirutattiṟkuriya cila vicēṣa laṭcaṇaṅkaḷellām atōṭu cērttu eḻutiyirukkiṟārkaḷ. intiyāviṉ pūrvamāyuḷḷa iṭaṅkaḷukkum paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷukkum rājarkaḷukkum vevvēṟu putiya camaskiruta peyarkaḷ koṭuttu camaskirutattil vaḻaṅkuvatupōla caṅkītattilum nūl eḻuti vaittārkaḷ. pūrvantoṭṭu teṉṉintiyāviṉ kōyilkaḷil nāḷatuvaraiyum collikkoṇṭu varum tēvāram, tiruvācakam, tiruvāymoḻi mutaliya teyva stōttiraṅkaḷ ōtuvārālum maṟṟum paktarkaḷālum collappaṭukiṟatai nām kēṭṭirukkiṟōm. avaikaḷil iṉṉiṉṉa rākaṅkaḷil pāṭavēṇṭumeṉṟu apputtakaṅkaḷil kuṟikkappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. ammuṟaippaṭiyē nāḷatu varaiyum kāṉam ceytu koṇṭu varukiṟārkaḷeṉṟum terikiṟatu. appaṭiyiruntum atil vaḻaṅki vanta paṇ intaḷam, paṇkāntāram, paṇ kolli, paṇ cīkāmaram, paṇ takkēci, paṇ kuṟiñci, paṇ naṭṭapāṭai, paṇ kuṟuntokai, paṇ tiruttāṇṭakam ivaipōṉṟa pūrvap peyarkaḷ camaskiruta nūlkaḷil vaḻaṅkātiruppataip pirattiyaṭcamāyp pārkkiṟōm. āṉāl pūrvamāyp paṭikkappaṭṭu vanta tēvāraṅkaḷē nūtaṉamāṉa camaskiruta peyarkaḷiṉāl aḻaikkappaṭum rākaṅkaḷākap peyar māṟi vaḻaṅkukiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. peyar māṟṟi vaḻaṅkukiṟa iyalpaip paṟṟi itaṉ muṉ maṟṟavarāl kēṭṭirukkiṟōm. ivaikaḷ yāvaṟṟaiyuṅkoṇṭu pūrva kālattil vaḻaṅki vanta teṉṉintiya caṅkīta muṟai paitākaras (Pythagoras) pōṉṟa tattuva kirēkka cāstiriyiṉāl 2/3, 3/4 eṉṟum, paratar, caṅkīta ratṉākarar pōṉṟa camaskiruta cirōmaṇikaḷāl 22 eṉṟum, pōcāṉkē (Bosanquet) pōṉṟavarkaḷāl 53 eṉṟum, vēṅkaṭamaki, caṅkītapārijātakkārar mutaliyavarkaḷāl 12 eṉṟum vevvēṟuvitamāṉa apippirayāṅkaḷai yuṭaiyatāyp pala nūlkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭaṉa. ‘itutāṉ yāṉai’ yeṉṟu cātippavarkaḷpōla avaravarkaḷ niyāyaṅkaḷ pala colli nūlkaḷ eḻutiṉārkaḷ. ivvaḷavu cantēkappaṭṭa kālattil teṉṉintiya caṅkītattai ivvaḷavu nuṭpamāy vicārittatāṉatu ivaruṭaiya pāṇṭittiyattait teḷivākak kāṭṭukiṟatu. uttara maturaiyil pāṇṭiya rājāṅkam viḻunta kālattil cōḻarkaḷum ataṉpiṉ teluṅkarkaḷāṉa nāyakka rājarkaḷum pāṇṭiya rājyattaic cila kālam āṇṭu vantārkaḷeṉṟu kāṇkiṟōm. akkālattil avviṭattiliruntu tāṅkaḷ arumaiyāy niṉaitta caṅkītattaiyum atai appiyācittiruntavarkaḷaiyum taṅkaḷ nāṭṭukkuk koṇṭupōy atikamāy virutti ceytārkaḷ. itaṉāl vijayanakaramum tañcainakarum mukkiyamāṉatāka nāḷatu varaiyum viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻnāṭṭiṉ kītam tamiḻ aracarkaḷaiyum tamiḻ teyvaṅkaḷaiyum aṇṭiyē nāḷatuvaraiyum piḻaittu vantirukkiṟateṉṟu aṟivāḷikaḷ aṟivārkaḷ.
karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṉ mutaṉmaiyaiyum atu kaiyāḷappaṭṭu vanta vitattaiyum kaṇṭaṟinta Capt. Daykaṭavuḷait tējacu cūḻntiruppatupōla teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaiyum tējacu cūḻntiruntateṉṟu collukiṟār. immakāṉuṭaiya perumaiyai eṉṉeṉṟu colvōm? mēṟṟicaiyiluḷḷa oru uttamarukku ivvuṉṉata eṇṇaṅkaḷ uṇṭāku māṉāl, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattaiyē tam jīvaṉākak koṇṭa vitvāṉkaḷukku ivveṇṇaṅkaḷ uṇṭāka vēṇṭāmā? uṇṭāṉāl mārkkam tavaṟi vaḻaṅkum kāṉaṅkaḷil piriyappaṭuvārkaḷā? mēlum, caṅkīta pārijātakkārarukkum (ki.pi. 1,600) caṅkīta ratṉākaram eḻutiya cāraṅkatēvarukkum (ki.pi. 1,200) avar kālattukku muntiya paratarukkum (ki.pi. 500) aṉēka āyira varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉē mutal ūḻiyil karnāṭaka caṅkītam ēṟpaṭṭateṉṟu nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. mēlum, pāṇṭiya rājyam aḻintapiṉ teṉṉintiyāvilirunta vijayanakaram, tañcai nakaram, tiruvaṉantapuram, maicūr mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷil teṉṉintiya caṅkītam aracarkaḷiṉ aṭaikkalam peṟṟu ātarikkappaṭṭu vantatu.
vaṭa intiyā kalakaṅkaḷiṉālum kuḻappattiṉālum niṟainta kālattil teṉṉintiyā camātāṉamāyiruntateṉṟum caṅkītam, viruttiyāṉateṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
The Music and the Musical Instruments of Southern India, by C.R. Day, P. 13.
“The theory, modes and notation in present use throughout the whole of India are derived from that taught originally by the earlier Sanskrit musicians; but owing to the south of India having been less disturbed by internal commotions and having been more subject to Hindu rule than either Deccan or Northern Provinces, the science of music would seem to have been maintained and cultivated long after the original art had been lost in the north.
Hence Southern India music or as it is more usually called Karnatic, bears as far as we can judge, a very close resemblance to what the Sanskrit must have been, and in many cases we can clearly trace the development and refinements introduced from time to time upon the original Ragas.”
“taṟkālam intiyā muḻutum upayōkapṭum caṅkīta cāstira vitikaḷum ārōkaṇaṅkaḷum curak kuṟippukaḷum ātiyil camaskiruta caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷuṭaiya muṟaiyiliruntē uṇṭāyiṉa. āṉāl teṉṉinti yāvil kuḻappaṅkaḷum kalakaṅkaḷum atikamāyillāmal intu rājāṅkattiṉ niḻalil intiyar amaitalāyk kālantaḷḷi vantapaṭiyāl, takṣaṇam vaṭa intiyā mutaliya nāṭukaḷaiviṭa innāṭṭil caṅkītamāṉatu nilaiyāy iruntu vantatumallāmal vaṭanāṭṭil cutta caṅkītam aḻintu pōṉa piṟakuṅkūṭa teṉṉintiyāvil caṅkītam apivirttiyaṭaintē vantatu.
ākaiyāl karnāṭaka caṅkītamāṉatu āti rākaṅkaḷil kāla vittiyācattukkut takuntapaṭi iṭaik kiṭaiyē uṇṭāṉa cuttamāyum aḻakāyumuḷḷa amcaṅkaḷai naṉṟē viḷakkik kāṭṭukiṟatu.”
tamiḻ icai nūlkaḷākiya akattiyam pañcapāratīyam perunārai peruṅkuruku mutaliya icai nūlkaḷ jalappiraḷayattāl aḻintu pōyiṉa veṉṟu muṉ pārttōm. ataṉ piṉṉuḷḷa cila nūlkaḷum kapāṭapuram kaṭal koṇṭa kālattil aḻintu pōyiṉa. araikuṟaiyāṉa cila ciṟu nūlkaḷ kaṭaiccaṅka kālattil pēṇuvāraṟṟuppōyiṉa. ataṉpiṉ camaskirutattil eḻutappaṭṭa cila nūlkaḷē vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟaṉa. ākilum, annūlkaḷil collappaṭukiṟavaikaḷukkum teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṟkum mikap pētamirukkiṟateṉṟu aṟivāḷikaḷ uṇarvārkaḷ. itaṉmuṉ, teṉṉintiya caṅkītam vēṟu, vaṭa intiya caṅkītam vēṟu eṉṟu pala kaṉavāṉkaḷiṉ apippirāyamum irukkiṟaJ. ivviraṇṭu muṟaikaḷukkum evvita pētamirukkiṟateṉṟu piṉvarum cila vākkiyaṅkaḷil collap paṭukiṟatu :-
The Music and the Musical Instruments of Southern India, by C.R. Day, P. 15.
“The exact definition of what constituted a sruti is difficult to determine; but it is thus gaguely given by the Sangita Ratnavali “A sruti is formed by the smallest intervals of sound and is perceivable by the ear; it is of 22 kinds; also every distinct audible sound is a sruti; it is a sruti because it is to be heard by the ear.”
Doubts however exist as to whether the intervals of the srutis were equal or not.
In the arrangement of the sruties, modern usage is diametrically opposite to the classical one; the latter placing them before the note to which they respectively belong, while the former gives position after the notes. It is difficult to dertermine when or by whom the alteration was effected. The arrangement of the frets of the Vina and other stringed instruments accord with the modern acceptation of the principle. Accordingly to the rule laid down in the classical treatises, the disposition of the notes is reversed in the case of the Darve instruments and out of this reversed arrangement perhaps the modern theory about the arrangement of the position of all sruties has been worked. (Tagore)”
“curutiyeṉpatu yātu eṉṟu nuṭpamāyc colluvatu kaṣṭamāṉālum, caṅkīta ratṉāvaḷiyil, nammuṭaiya kātāl kēṭkakkūṭiya atinuṭpamāṉa iṭaiveḷikaḷuḷḷa captantāṉ atu eṉṟum, atu 22 vakaippaṭṭateṉṟum kātiṉāl kēṭṭaṟiyakkūṭiya ovvoru taṉittaṉiyāṉa iṭaiveḷikkum antap peyareṉṟum kātiṉāl kēṭkappaṭuvatiṉāl ataṟkuc curutiyeṉṟa peyareṉṟum colliyirukkiṟatu.
āṉāl curaṅkaḷukku iṭaiyil varum iṭaiveḷikaḷ (ivēnacaeyaṭaḷa) yāvum orē aḷavuṭaiyaṉavō eṉpataip paṟṟi māttiram cantēkamuṇṭu.
curutikaḷai amaikkum oḻuṅkil, taṟkāla muṟaikkum āti muṟaikkum nērvirōtamirukkiṟaJ. curutikaḷ varum iṭamāṉatu āti muṟaippaṭi curutikku muntiyum taṟkāla muṟaippaṭi curutikkup pintiyumāyirukkiṟatu. inta māṟutal eppōtu uṇṭāṉateṉṟum yārāl uṇṭāṉateṉṟum terivatu pirayācaiyācaiyāyirukkiṟatu. āṉāl vīṇai meṭṭukaḷ vaikkappaṭum vitattaiyum maṟṟa tanti vāttiyaṅkaḷaiyum kavaṉittāl avai taṟkālam oppukkoḷḷappaṭṭa muṟaippaṭiyē irukkiṉṟaṉa.
tāruvāttiyaṅkaḷil ātimuṟaikku virōtamākavē meṭṭukaḷ ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. oruvēḷai ippaṭi māṟāṉa muṟaiyiliruntē taṟkāla curutikaḷuṭaiya stāṉam kuṟikkappaṭṭa tāyirukkalām.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vākkiyaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkaiyil caṅkītaratṉākarar atinuṭpamāṉa iṭaiveḷi kaḷuḷḷa 22 curutikaḷ varukiṟatākac collukiṟār eṉṟu eḻutiṉār. āṉāl curaṅkaḷukku iṭaiyil varum iṭaiveḷikaḷ orē aḷavuṭaiyaṉavā eṉṟu cantēkikkiṟār. ātimuṟaikkum taṟkāla muṟaikkum pētamiruppataik koṇṭē ippaṭic cantēkikkiṟār. āṉāl, vīṇai meṭṭukaḷ vaikkappaṭṭirukkum vitattaik kavaṉittāl taṟkālam oppuk koḷḷakkūṭiya muṟaippaṭiyē yirukkiṟateṉṟu collukiṟār. atāvatu, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṟku ottatāka virukkiṟateṉṟu nām niṉaikka vēṇṭum. tāru vāttiyaṅkaḷil ātimuṟaikku virōtamāka meṭṭukaḷ vaikkap paṭṭirukkiṟatu eṉkiṟār. ivviṣayattil tēcikamāṉa intustāṉi paḻakiyapiṉ ataṟkut takuntapaṭiyē vāttiyaṅkaḷil curastāṉaṅkaḷum naḻuvalāyirukka vēṇṭiyatu avaciyantāṉē. intat tēcattiṟku takunta nūl caṅkīta ratṉākarameṉṟu anēkar apippirāyappaṭukiṟārkaḷ. caṅkīta ratṉākararuṭaiya muṟaippaṭi pārttāl intustāṉ kītattiṟku muḻutum ottirukka māṭṭāteṉṟu aṟivārkaḷ. intustāṉ caṅkīta muṟaiyaiyē eḻuta vanta Mr. Clements, Mr. Deval mutaliyavarkaḷiṉ curuti nirṇayam cāraṅkatēvar apippirāyappaṭi alla eṉpatai itaṉ piṉ pārppōm. ēṉeṉṟāl, iṭaiveḷikaḷ orē aḷavuṭaiyavāyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟa cāraṅkar muṟaikku virōtamāka vevvēṟu iṭaiveḷikaḷuḷḷa curaṅkaḷai niccayam paṇṇiyirukkiṟārkaḷ. vaṭatēcattil upayōkikkum curattiṉ iṭaiveḷi kaḷukkum teṉtēcattil upayōkikkum curattiṉ iṭaiveḷikaḷukkum mikunta pētamuṇṭeṉpatai piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Oriental Music by Chinnasawmi Moodr. M.A., P. I, 4. P. 37.
“The mathematical ratios of the Indian Gamut likewise vary in the north and south of India. But this extremely complicated question may be left open for the present, because for all practical purposes the system of equal temperament which coincides almost exactly with the adjustment of frets on the Vina is found to meet all existing requirements more or less satisfactorily. It is admitted on all hands that this curious coincidence has been arrived at by the two nations through distinct processes, quite independently of each other; and historical research so far as it has been made, has established the fact that the Indian system has remained in statuquo for ages before the Lux ab oriente dawned upon the West.”
“vaṭa tēcattil upayōkappaṭum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷil oru curattukkum ataṟku aṭuttu varum curattukkum iṭaiyiluḷḷa iṭaiveḷi yaippaṟṟiya kaṇakkukkum teṉ tēcattil vaḻaṅkum curuti iṭaiveḷikaḷiṉ kaṇakkukkum vittiyācamuṇṭu. ākaiyāl cikku mukkāṉa inta viṣayattaippaṟṟi ippōtu yōcikkat tēvaiyillai. oru stāyiyiṉ curaṅkaḷaic camapākamāyp pirittu avaikaḷukku stāṉaṅkaḷai ēṟpaṭuttum muṟaiyāṉatu (Equal temperament) vīṇaiyil vaḻaṅkum meṭṭukaḷiṉāl curutikaḷai niccayappaṭuttum muṟaikku ottatākavēyiruppatāl namakkuc caṅkīta viṣayamāy avaciya māy vēṇṭiya kāriyaṅkaḷai yellām pūrtti paṇṇa atupōtumāṉatā yirukkiṟatu. ippaṭi iru jātiyārum oruvar vaḻiyai oruvar nōkkāmal vevvēṟuvitamāṉa vaḻikaḷiṉ mūlamāy orēvitamāṉa muṭivukku vantatāṉatu atika vintaiyāṉa icaik kuṟippāyirukkiṟatu eṉṟu yāvarum oppuk koḷḷukiṟārkaḷ. ākaiyāl, intiya muṟaiyāṉatu ātikālan toṭaṅki atāvatu kīḻttēcattiṉ caṅkītavoḷi mēltēcattil pirakācitta kālattukku muṉṉēyē toṭaṅki kaiyāḷappaṭṭu varukiṉṟateṉṟu carittira ārāyccikaḷiṉ mūlamāy aṟiya iṭamirukkiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷaik kavaṉikkumpōtu vīṇaiyil vaḻaṅkum meṭṭukaḷiṉāl curutikaḷai niccayappaṭuttummuṟai oru stāyiyiṉ curaṅkaḷaic camapākamāyp pirittu stāṉaṅkaḷai ēṟpaṭuttum muṟaikku ottiruppatiṉāl caṅkīta viṣayamāṉa kāriyaṅkaḷai pūrtti paṇṇa ituvē pōtumāṉatāyirukkiṟatu. itu viṣayattil mēṟṟicaiyārum teṉṉintiyarum orē vitamāṉa apippirāyaṅ kaḷaiyuṭaiyavarkaḷā yirukkiṟārkaḷ eṉpatu mikavum āccariyamāyirukkiṟateṉṟum carittira ārāycciyiṉ mūlamāy veku kālattiṟku muṉṉēyē itu intiyāviliruntateṉṟu eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟateṉṟum collukiṟār. iṟṟaikku 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē nālvakaiyāḻum avaṟṟiṉ ilakkaṇamum teṉmaturaiyiliruntateṉṟu nām itaṉmuṉ pārttirukkiṟōm. avaikaḷaippaṟṟiya viparamum curuti cērkkum muṟaiyaiyum piṉṉāl aṟivōm. cāraṅkatēvarāl collappaṭṭa tuvāvimcati muṟaikaḷ karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṟku utaviyāyirukkumā allavā eṉpataṉaip paṟṟi nām aṟivatu mika avaciyam. karnāṭaka caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum curutikaḷ iṉṉateṉṟu iṉṉum niccayamāyc collappaṭātiruppataṉāl tuvāvimcati curutikaḷukkum itaṟkumuḷḷa tāratammiyattai eṭuttuc colvatu avvaḷavu takutiyāyirukkamāṭṭāJ. iruvita curutikaḷaiyum iṉimēl ottup pārkkumpoḻutu, iraṇṭiṟku muḷḷa tāratammiyattait teḷivāy aṟintu koḷvōm. āyiṉum, caṅkītaratṉākaram eṉṉum nūlil kaṇṭapaṭi curuti kuṟikkiṟōmeṉṟu colpavarkaḷiṉ kaṇakkukaḷaic cāraṅkatēvar kaṇakkōṭu ottuppārkka vēṇṭiyatu mikavum mukkiyamāyirukkiṟatu.
cāraṅkatēvarō oru stāyiyil varum curaṅkaḷ camaaḷavuṭaiyavai kaḷāyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu iṭaiyil vēṟu curaṅkaḷuṇṭākāmal oṉṟaṟkoṉṟu tīviramāyp paṭippaṭiyāyp pōka vēṇṭumeṉṟum collukiṟār. āṉāl vaṭatēcattil karnāṭaka caṅkītamō ivviraṇṭiṟkum vittiyācamāṉa vēṟorumuṟaiyeṉṟu teḷivāyt terikiṟatu. mēlum, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum curutikaḷaiyum caṅkīta ratṉākarattil vaḻaṅki vanta curutikaḷaiyum naṉṟāy aṟinta paitākaras (Pythagoras)  eṉṉum kirēkka tattuva cāstiriyār teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil kaṇṭa ma, pa eṉṟa curaṅkaḷai kātiṟkēṭṭuc curuti kūṭṭum muṟaiyait tām ñāpakappaṭuttik koḷḷum cariyāṉa poruttamuṭaiyavaiyalla eṉṟu cilar vātikkavum itē mātiri aḷantupōṭum muṟaiyaic collum caṅkīta pārijātam pōṉṟa nūlkaḷaip pārtta intiya vitvacirōmaṇikaḷ tāṅkaḷ pāṭum cila rākaṅkaḷukku atu poruttamāyiruppatiṉāl paitākaras kaṇṭupiṭitta ṭaiyaṭāṉikskēl (Diatonic Scale) cariyeṉṟum ataṟkuppiṉ hārmaṉi (Harmony)  varumpaṭi āṅkilēyar kaṇṭupiṭitta cama aḷavuḷḷa curaṅkaḷ (Equal temperament) cariyallaveṉṟum kuṟai colla ārampittārkaḷ. ippaṭip palapēr collum apippirāyaṅkaḷaik kavaṉittavarkaḷ caṅkītaratṉākara muṟaippaṭi 22 curutikaḷaiyum attōṭu kalakka veku pirayācaippaṭṭārkaḷ. camaskiruta culōkaṅkaḷiluḷḷavai uṇmaiyeṉṟu stāpikka muyaṉṟavarkaḷ teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum curutikaḷaiyum avaṟṟai uḷḷatai uḷḷapaṭi kāṭṭi utaviyāyirunta vīṇaiyaiyum tavaṟutal uṭaiyateṉṟu coṉṉāloḻiya
